# Precipitation — Big World Plan

**Target:** Python 3.11 stdlib-only sim (`sim/`, protocol v2) streaming JSON lines to a Java Swing viewer (`viewer/src/precip`, package `precip`, plain `javac`, JDK 25, no dependencies). Windows 11, launched by `run.bat` / `C:\Users\lenard\Desktop\Precipitation.lnk`.

**Project context:** `[UPDATE]` — extends the working v1 build (1280×720 world, flat ground, 5 cloud types, 1–2 houses, single window, no menu or saves). v1 is verified in `viewer/snapshots/*.png` and `sim/tests/`; nothing here is greenfield except the new files marked (new).

> **Parallelism:** Phase A (sim) and Phase B (viewer rendering) run **in parallel**, each built against `PROTOCOL.md` v2 (frozen; change only via an SA-Asker blocking question and in lockstep). B proves itself on `FakeSim`, not on the real sim. **Phase C starts after B** (it edits the same `Renderer.java` / `ViewerPanel.java` / `Main.java`). Phase D needs A + B + C.

## Concept
A much larger world (default 3840×1080; Small 1920×900, Medium 3840×1080, Large 6400×1200, Huge 9600×1400) with a random heightmap (flat / rolling / hilly / mountainous), settlements from hamlets to cities on flattened ground, and realistic unique clouds that condense out of haze and evaporate back into it. The viewer shows it through a camera (pan, zoom at cursor, minimap). Around the simulation sits a proper app: main menu listing every saved world, pause menu, autosave, Save & Leave, settings (custom background image, rebindable keys) and toggleable side panels.

## Decisions carried in (settled, not reopened)
Python serialises its own state (`save` command, `--load`); Java owns slots `saves/<slot-id>/{meta.json,state.sav,history.json,thumb.png}`, `config/settings.json`, `config/backgrounds/`. Objects listed once in `hello`, frames carry deltas `[id,sway,snow,wet,w,h]`. Keys: arrows pan, wheel zoom, Home fit, M minimap, `[` `]` `\` panels, Q/E wind, Z/X temperature, Esc pause menu, F5 quick save, F11 fullscreen; Space, +/-, W, A, L, G, H, R unchanged; all rebindable. Budget: sim 30 ticks/s at default size; viewer 3–7 ms render per frame. Keep `--snapshot`, `--frames`, `--fake`, `--replay`, `--bench` working.

## Phase A — Simulation (Python, `sim/`)

**What each part does**
| Part | Role |
|---|---|
| `terrain.py` (new) | Seeded heightmap, four kinds, `sample(x)` and slope |
| `settlements.py` (new) | Places hamlet/village/town/city on flattened ground, generates buildings |
| `objects.py`, `world.py` | Objects with ids on the terrain; delta state; world size and ground_y |
| `windfield.py`, `particles.py` | Wind lifted by terrain slope; particles land on the surface |
| `clouds.py` | 8 types, `form`/`shear`/`anvil`, smooth puff skeletons, counts scaled to world |
| `savestate.py` (new), `simulation.py`, `precip_sim.py` | Save/load, `view` culling, v2 hello/frame, new CLI |

1. **[VERIFIED] A1 — CLI, world size and v2 hello skeleton.** Edit `precip_sim.py` (`parse_args`: default 3840×1080, validate 1280–9600 × 720–1440, add `--terrain`, `--settlements`, `--load`), `simulation.py` (`hello_message` → `version:2`, `loaded`, `t`, terrain, settlements, objects), `protocol_io.py`. Done when: `python sim/precip_sim.py --headless-frames 3` prints a v2 `hello` then frames, and bad sizes exit non-zero with a stderr message.
2. **[VERIFIED] A2 — Terrain heightmap.** Create `sim/terrain.py`: step 8, `len = ceil(w/step)+1`, per-seed random for each kind, amplitude capped (about 40% of height) so peaks stay below the cloud spawn band; `world.ground_y` = lowest point (largest y). Edit `world.py` (`freezing_y` from lowest ground). Done when: same seed gives an identical array, all four kinds differ visibly in a printed profile, `ground_y == max(terrain.y)`.
3. **[VERIFIED] A3 — Terrain-aware wind and particles.** Edit `windfield.py` (replace the per-feature loop with slope-based orographic lift; cell scales with world, 80 at default; `build_streamlines` keeps lines above the surface), `particles.py` (landing and splash at `terrain.sample(x)`, off-world cull uses new width), `simulation.py` (`_update_lightning` bolts end on the surface). Done when: no particle rests below the surface, wind v is negative (up) on windward slopes, and the `wind` block matches `cols*rows == len(u)`.
4. **[VERIFIED] A4 — Settlements and buildings.** Create `sim/settlements.py`, extend `naming.py` (settlement names), rewrite `objects.generate_objects`: terrain first, then flatten under each settlement span, then buildings with `id, variant, seed, wall, roof, floors, settlement, layer`, then scenery on the terrain. Done when: a default world (normal) has at least one village and one city, `--settlements none` has none, every building base y equals the terrain at its x, ids are unique and stable per seed.
5. **[VERIFIED] A5 — Object deltas and ground cover.** Edit `objects.update_objects` (index by id, track changed set, `[id,sway,snow,wet,w,h]`), add `ground` arrays (`step 32`, snow/wet, only on change, snow lingers above the freezing level). Done when: a quiet frame lists zero objects, a rain frame lists only puddle/tree/building ids, `ground` appears at most a few times a second.
6. **[VERIFIED] A6 — Realistic cloud model (sim side).** Edit `clouds.py` (add altocumulus, stratus, cirrus to `TYPE_PARAMS` / `_TYPE_TINT_BASE`; `form` rising from ≈0 and returning to ≈0 before removal; `shear`; `anvil` for cumulonimbus; puffs as a smooth function of time, appended with r≈0 and dropped only at r≈0), `simulation.py` (`_cloud_to_protocol` adds `seed/form/shear/anvil`; `MIN_CLOUDS`/`MAX_CLOUDS` and initial count scale with world width; `_fast_forward_new_cloud` sets `form`; spawn altitude per type above the terrain). Done when: per-frame change of any puff `r` stays small in a 5000-frame run, `form` has no jumps, signatures stay unique, a default world holds roughly 9–21 clouds.
7. **[VERIFIED] A7 — Save and load.** Create `sim/savestate.py` (versioned, atomic temp+replace; covers world, terrain, objects, clouds incl. each `cloud.rng`, `world_rng`, particles, signatures, counters, delta baseline); `simulation.py` (`get_state`/`set_state`, `save` command), `precip_sim.py` (`--load`, `saved` reply between frames, hello with `"loaded":true`). Done when: run N → save → load → run M equals an uninterrupted N+M run frame for frame; an incompatible version exits non-zero with a clear stderr line; a failed save leaves the old file intact.
8. **[VERIFIED] A8 — `view` culling and command audit.** Edit `simulation.handle_command` / `_build_frame` (`view x0 x1`, bare `view` resets; `stats.particles` stays the full count), `reseed` keeps size and terrain kind and re-sends `hello`. Done when: culled frames contain only particles with `x0-200 ≤ x ≤ x1+200`.
9. **[VERIFIED] A9 — Tests.** Update `sim/tests/test_protocol_schema.py`, `test_commands.py`, `test_lifecycle.py`, `test_uniqueness.py`, `test_particle_kinds.py`, `test_stdin_eof.py` for v2; add `test_terrain.py`, `test_settlements.py`, `test_saveload.py`, `test_view.py`. Done when: `python -m unittest discover sim/tests` is green.
10. **[VERIFIED] A10 — Performance and tools.** Time 900 headless frames at 3840×1080 and 9600×1400; if under 30 ticks/s, rebuild the wind field every 2–3 ticks (resend the cached one) and cap per-tick work in `particles.update`. Update `tools/record_replay.py` to the v2 `Simulation` API. Done when: default size sustains 30 ticks/s on this machine and a v2 replay file records.

**Phase A verification:** unittest green; headless timing numbers recorded in this file; `saved`→`--load` round trip checked by A7's test.

Done when: 12 clouds at default size are all distinguishable, none flickers between frames, a forming cloud fades in from haze, and `--bench` stays within the 3–7 ms budget.
7. **[VERIFIED] B7 — Overlays at world scale.** Edit `Renderer.java` (`drawWindField` step by zoom, `drawStreamlines`, `drawParticles` culled to view, `drawLightning` to terrain, `drawArrow`, `drawCloudLabel`, freezing line follows the view). Done when: no overlay draws off-screen, fit zoom stays readable.

**Phase B verification:** `build.bat` clean; `--fake` and `--replay` visual check; snapshots at all four world sizes saved to `viewer/snapshots/`; `--bench` figures recorded here.

## Phase E — Fair weather and towering cumulus (protocol v2.1)

**Project context:** `[UPDATE]` — extends the finished v2 world (Phases A–D). Today every cloud is born to rain on its own schedule; the user asked for "normal clouds that can form into precipitating clouds, and cumulus to have a chance to turn into a cumulonimbus". Design is settled in `PROTOCOL.md` v2.1 (`fair`, `from_type` / `morph`, old-save rule under "Save files"); it is additive, so v2 viewers and v2 save files keep working.

> **Parallelism:** E1–E6 (sim) and E7–E11 (viewer) are being built **in parallel** by two agents against `PROTOCOL.md` v2.1; the viewer proves itself on `FakeSim`. E12–E14 start only after both finish.

**What each part does**
| Part | Role |
|---|---|
| `PROTOCOL.md` | v2.1 contract: optional `fair`, `from_type`, `morph`; `deaths` carry `from_type`; pre-v2.1 saves still load |
| `clouds.py` | `Cloud` gains `fair` / develop decision and a one-shot cumulus → cumulonimbus morph; puffs appended at r≈0 |
| `simulation.py`, `savestate.py` | `_cloud_to_protocol` emits the new keys; new state saved and defaulted when absent |
| `Model.java`, `ViewerState.java` | Parse `fair` / `from_type` / `morph`; keep them in `Life` history and `history.json` |
| `CloudPainter.java`, `SharedClouds.java` | Per-kind blend by `morph` so the cached layer glides, never pops |
| `Renderer.java` (`drawCloudLabel`, timeline, info panel) | Shows fair, developed, fair-dead and morphed clouds distinctly |
| `FakeSim.java` | Mirrors fair / develop / morph so the viewer is tested without the real sim |

1. **[VERIFIED] E0 — Protocol v2.1.** `PROTOCOL.md` documents `fair`, `from_type` / `morph` (no-jump rule, name prefix swap `Cu-0042` → `Cb-0042`, `deaths` carry `from_type`) and the old-saves sentence under "Save files". Done when: both implementing agents read the same text. Evidence: bullets present in the `cloud` field notes and in the "Save files" section.

**Sim (Python, `sim/`)**

2. **[VERIFIED] E1 — Fair-weather clouds that may develop.** Edit `clouds.py` (`Cloud` gets `fair` and a develop decision drawn from `cloud.rng` in `spawn_cloud`; cumulus, stratocumulus, stratus and altostratus are born fair, cumulonimbus and nimbostratus never, cirrus and altocumulus always) and `update_cloud` (a fair cloud that develops thickens and darkens, then `fair` flips to `false` for good and it enters `precipitating` through the existing path; one that does not runs `forming → growing → mature → dissipating` and never reaches `precipitating`). Edit `simulation._cloud_to_protocol` to emit `fair`. Done when: a fair non-developing cloud has no `precipitating` in `stage_times`, `fair` never goes back to `true`, and darkness and opacity change smoothly across the develop moment.
3. **[VERIFIED] E2 — Cumulus → cumulonimbus morph.** Edit `clouds.py` (a developing cumulus may, once in its life, switch `type` to `cumulonimbus` with `from_type="cumulus"`; `morph` rises 0 → 1 with `_spring` / `_smoothstep` and stays 1; tower and anvil puffs are *appended* with r≈0 and swell with `morph`; `w`/`h`/`tint` glide; `id`, `seed` and signature unchanged; name keeps the quoted part and swaps `Cu-` for `Cb-`; stage never regresses) and `simulation.py` (`deaths` entries carry `from_type`; an event line is logged when the tower starts). Done when: at the switch frame puffs, bounds, tint, opacity and darkness equal the previous frame's within a tiny epsilon, and no puff `r` jumps over a 5000-frame run.
4. **[VERIFIED] E3 — Save and load, including old saves.** Edit `savestate.py` (store `fair`, the develop decision, `from_type`, `morph` and morph progress per cloud; a pre-v2.1 file loads with `fair=false`, no `from_type`, never morphing) and `simulation.get_state` / `set_state`. Done when: run N → save → load → run M equals an uninterrupted N+M run through a morph, and an existing v2 save in `saves/` still loads.
5. **[VERIFIED] E4 — Tests.** Edit `sim/tests/test_clouds.py`, `test_protocol_schema.py` (accept the optional keys, reject wrong types), `test_lifecycle.py` (stage order for fair clouds), `test_uniqueness.py` (signatures survive a morph), `test_saveload.py` (round trip across a morph, old-format fixture). Done when: `python -m unittest discover sim/tests` is green.
6. **[VERIFIED] E5 — Tuning.** Edit the probability constants in `clouds.py` and measure over several seeds at default size. Done when: 40–60 % of clouds never rain and 15–30 % of cumulus tower into a cumulonimbus, with the measured numbers recorded under Phase E verification.
7. **[VERIFIED] E6 — Performance check.** Time 900 headless frames at 3840×1080 as in A10 (`sim/tests/test_perf.py`). Done when: default size still sustains 30 ticks/s; if not, the extra per-cloud work is trimmed first.

**Viewer (Java, `viewer/src/precip`)**

8. **[VERIFIED] E7 — Model, state and history fields.** Edit `Model.java` (`Cloud` and the `deaths` entry: `fair`, `fromType`, `morph`; absent keys mean `false` / empty / 0), `ViewerState.java` (`Life` keeps fair, developed and fromType; the name swap keeps the same id) and `SaveStore.java` / `history.json` (persist the new `Life` fields, tolerate old files). Done when: a `--replay` of a v2 file and of one with v2.1 keys both load and `javac` is clean.
9. **[VERIFIED] E8 — CloudPainter per-kind blend by morph.** Edit `CloudPainter.java` and `SharedClouds.java`: while `morph` is between 0 and 1, blend the cumulus and cumulonimbus looks (anvil spread, shading, edge style, translucency) by `morph` over the appended puffs; the layer cache key stays the cloud `seed` plus zoom bucket and never keys on `type`, so the switch frame does not rebuild or flash. Done when: a morphing cloud grows its tower smoothly, nothing flickers on the switch frame, and `--bench` stays within the 3–7 ms budget.
10. **[VERIFIED] E9 — UI for fair, developed, fair-dead and morphed clouds.** Edit `Renderer.java` (`drawCloudLabel`, the timeline bar colours, the selected-cloud info) so a cloud reads as one of: fair (plain label, no precipitation arrows), developed (marked once `fair` flips), fair-dead (died without raining, visible on the timeline), morphed (`Cb-` name with a "was cumulus" note and a small tower marker). Done when: all four read differently in a snapshot and in the history panel, and the help overlay (`drawHelp`) legend mentions them.
11. **[VERIFIED] E10 — FakeSim mirror.** Edit `FakeSim.java` to emit `fair`, a develop moment, and one cumulus → cumulonimbus morph with appended r≈0 puffs and the `Cu-` → `Cb-` swap. Done when: `run.bat --fake` shows a fair cloud thicken into rain and a cumulus tower within a few minutes.
12. **[VERIFIED] E11 — Snapshots and bench.** Run `--snapshot` on `--fake` at three morph values and `--bench` on Medium; store images in `viewer/snapshots/v2/`. Done when: the images show cumulus, mid-morph and cumulonimbus of the same cloud, and the bench figures are recorded under Phase E verification.

**Integration**

13. **[VERIFIED] E12 — Real-sim snapshot showing a morph.** Find a seed whose headless run (`precip_sim.py --headless-frames`) produces a `from_type` cloud, then `--snapshot` the real sim at a frame where `morph` is mid-way. Done when: the image shows a tower growing out of a cumulus and the label reads `Cb-` with the "was cumulus" note.
14. **[VERIFIED] E13 — `--selftest`.** Run the 18-step `--selftest` (`SelfTest.java`) and the unit tests; add a save/reload check while a cloud is mid-morph if the selftest can be seeded for it. Done when: all steps pass and time, cloud count and morph state match across the reload.
15. **[VERIFIED] E14 — Desktop shortcut check.** Confirm `C:\Users\lenard\Desktop\Precipitation.lnk` (target `run.bat`, icon `assets\precipitation.ico`) still opens the menu by double-click after the Phase E build; re-run `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File tools\make_shortcut.ps1` if `run.bat` or the build output changed. Done when: double-click on the icon opens the menu and a new world shows fair clouds.

**Phase E verification:** unittest green; measured never-rain and tower percentages and the 900-frame timing recorded here; `build.bat` clean; `--fake` visual check of develop and morph; real-sim morph snapshot saved; `--selftest` passes.

## Phase F — Game modes, region presets and under-cloud darkening (protocol v2.2)

**Project context:** `[UPDATE]` — extends the finished v2.1 build (Phases A–E: big terrain world, menu, saves, settings, fair/developing clouds). Nothing is rewritten; the existing world becomes the "Sandbox" mode and three things are added around it. The user's request (2026-10-08): (1) when the camera goes under a cloud the screen darkens with a smooth transition, rain stays visible, the background picture is unchanged; (2) three game modes: Sandbox (existing), **Hurricane Formation** (a guided visual and text tour of how a hurricane works, with inside and outside views, then a stage explorer) and **Cloud Formations** (one tab per cloud type showing its life from start to end with precipitation, wind and streamlines); (3) terrain and temperature presets from real data: Washington, Philippines, Tibet, Russia, United Kingdom, Central Europe. Design is settled in `PROTOCOL.md` under "v2.2 additions" (`--mode`, `--preset`, `--cloud-type`, `hello.mode` / `preset` / `showcase` / `hurricane`, the `hurricane` frame, the `showcase` and `stage` / `auto` commands). It is additive: `version` stays 2, v2.1 viewers still play sandbox worlds, and v2.1 save files load as `mode:"sandbox"` with no preset.

> **Parallelism:** three agents build **in parallel** against `PROTOCOL.md` v2.2 (frozen; change only via an SA-Asker blocking question and in lockstep): **Sim A** (F1–F5, `python-tooling-dev`), **Sim B** (F6–F7, `python-tooling-dev`) and **Viewer** (F8–F15, `general-purpose`). The viewer proves itself on `FakeSim`, not on the real sim, so no viewer step waits on a sim step. Integration (F16–F19) starts only after all three finish. Sim A and Sim B touch disjoint files except the one seam in `precip_sim.py` (see Open question 9).

**What each part does**
| Part | Role |
|---|---|
| `PROTOCOL.md` | v2.2 contract: `mode`, `preset`, `showcase`, `hurricane`, the `hurricane` frame, new commands; v2.1 saves still load |
| `sim/presets.py` (new) | Six regions: researched elevation transects, climate (temperature, wind, cloud mix), landmarks, cited sources |
| `terrain.py`, `world.py`, `clouds.py`, `savestate.py` | Preset terrain kind `"preset"`, starting `surface_temp_c` and `prevailing_u`, cloud-type weights, preset stored in saves |
| Showcase mode (`simulation.py`) | One cloud of one type, flat stage, full life then a pause then a new unique cloud; `showcase` command |
| `sim/hurricane.py` (new) | `HurricaneSim`: parametric vortex, ten stages, lesson text, profile / bands / cross-section fields, `stage` and `auto` commands |
| `precip_sim.py` | `--mode`, `--preset`, `--cloud-type`; routes the loop to the sandbox, showcase or hurricane sim |
| `Settings.java`, `Renderer.java`, `ViewerPanel.java` | Under-cloud darkening: an eased dim level, its setting, drawn between world and rain |
| `MainMenuScreen.java`, `AppShell.java` | Game-mode choice (Sandbox / Hurricane Formation / Cloud Formations) and routing |
| `HurricaneScreen.java` (new) | Guided tour with text, outside view (eye, eyewall, spiral rainbands, outflow shield), inside cross-section, stage explorer |
| `CloudFormationsScreen.java` (new) | One tab per cloud type, lesson text, life-cycle strip, live precipitation / wind / streamlines |
| `NewSimScreen.java`, `SimProcess.java` | Region choice, passes `--preset` to the sim |
| `Renderer.java` (landmarks, region card), `SaveStore.java` | Landmark labels on the terrain, a region card, preset id in slot `meta.json` |
| `FakeSim.java`, `Model.java`, `ViewerState.java` | Parse and fake `mode` / `preset` / `showcase` / `hurricane`, so the viewer is tested without the real sim |

## Phase G — Climate, merging, supercells, cars and the World simulation (protocol v2.3)

**Project context:** `[UPDATE]` — extends the finished v2.2 build (Phases A–F: big terrain world, menu, saves, fair/developing clouds, three game modes, region presets). Baseline to keep green: sim suite 279 OK (1 skipped), viewer `--selftest` 26 steps, `build.bat` clean. The user's request (2026-10-09): (1) clouds merge and the merged cloud takes the shape of both (a sheet merged into a cumulus leaves a straight line through the cumulus); (2) cloud spawn rates depend on temperature, wind speed and gases; (3) temperature cycles, night lower and day higher, plus seasons, applied to the presets; (4) cars, and tornadoes produced by supercells, with the supercell in a lifetime tour and in the sandbox; (5) a World Simulation of the whole Earth where clicking a dot or a state opens a preset of that place; (6) polish, test, and add snapshots. Design is settled in `PROTOCOL.md` under "v2.3 additions" (`--month/--hour/--year-days`, `world.temp` breakdown, `atmosphere`, `gas`/`season`/`hour`/`tornado` commands, merge keys, `supercell`, `tornadoes`, `roads`/`cars`, `damage`, `showcase.tours`, `--mode world`). It is additive: `version` stays 2, v2.2 viewers still play, v2.2 saves load with the default climate and no cars.

> **Parallelism:** **Sim Core 1** (G1–G6), **Sim World** (G13–G17), **Viewer Sandbox** (G18–G25) and **Viewer World** (G26–G30) run **in parallel** against `PROTOCOL.md` v2.3 (frozen; change only via an SA-Asker blocking question and in lockstep). **Sim Core 2** (G7–G12) starts **after Sim Core 1** because both edit `clouds.py` and `simulation.py`. Both viewer agents prove themselves on their fakes (`FakeSim`, `FakeWorld`), so no viewer step waits on a sim step. **Integration** (G31–G35, orchestrator) starts only after all five finish.

> **Added after the plan was written (2026-10-09):** preset biomes (`PROTOCOL.md` "Preset biome (v2.3)"): every preset gets its own ground colours, trees, houses, towns, starting snow and arid sky, owned by Sim World and Viewer Sandbox as G36-G42 below. Snapshot folder rule (G43): only real viewer renders live under `viewer/snapshots/`; Python diagnostic plots live in `tools/debug_plots/`.
>
> **Status 2026-10-09:** G0-G32 and G35-G43 landed with evidence named per step; G33 (full re-run after the biome work) and G34 (render-time budget) are the two open steps. Five implementer sessions carried the work: `v2-3-sim-core-1-climate-gases-spawn-merg-20261009-100334`, `v2-3-sim-core-2-supercell-tornado-cars-20261009-113638`, `v2-3-sim-world-presets-and-worldsim-20261009-100334`, `v2-3-viewer-sandbox-rendering-and-contro-20261009-100334`, `v2-3-viewer-world-simulation-mode-20261009-100334`.

0. **[VERIFIED] G0 — Protocol v2.3.** `PROTOCOL.md` documents the launch flags, climate and gases, merging, supercell, tornadoes, cars, the supercell showcase tour and world mode with its frame and commands. Done when: all five implementing agents read the same text. Evidence: the "v2.3 additions" section is present at the bottom of `PROTOCOL.md` (written 2026-10-09).

**Sim Core 1 — climate, gases, spawning, merging (Python, `sim/`, `python-tooling-dev`)**

1. **[VERIFIED] G1 — Day, season and temperature breakdown.** Edit `world.py` (clock: `year_frac`, `month`, `day`, `season`, `hemisphere`; `surface_temp_c = base_c + season_c + diurnal_c + gas_c + user_c`; diurnal coldest before sunrise and warmest near `time_of_day` 0.62; plain worlds use a seeded sine, coldest mid January in the north; `temp` command changes `user_c`; `season` and `hour` commands glide), `simulation.py` (`frame.world.year_frac/month/day/season/hemisphere/temp`, `hello.climate`), `precip_sim.py` (`--month`, `--hour` default 7.2, `--year-days` 2–365 default 8; bad values exit non-zero with a stderr line). Month default reads the preset's `start_month` if present, else 4. Done when: `surface_temp_c` equals the sum of its parts every frame, no step at midnight or the year wrap, a day is 300 sim seconds, and a preset's twelve `monthly_temp_c` drive `season_c`. Evidence: `sim/tests/test_climate.py` 18 OK (Sim Core 1 session `v2-3-sim-core-1-climate-gases-spawn-merg-20261009-100334`); `surface_temp_c` equals the sum of its parts within 0.01 in the headless frames; `--month/--hour/--year-days` added to `precip_sim.py`. Decided without asking: `frame.world.day = int(t // 300)`, 0-based from world start, not from hour jumps.
2. **[REVISED] G2 — Gases and atmosphere.** Create `sim/atmosphere.py` (`humidity`, `co2_ppm` 180–2000, `aerosol`, derived `instability` and `shear`; `gas_c` = 3.0 °C per doubling against 420 ppm), edit `simulation.py` (`frame.atmosphere`, `gas` command with `delta` or `value`, clamped). Done when: all values clamp, doubling CO2 adds 3.0 °C to `gas_c`, nothing steps between frames, and `instability` rises with warm humid air under a cold sky. Changed on landing: the gas commands move `humidity`, `co2_ppm` and `aerosol` instantly, and only the greenhouse offset `gas_c` glides (tau 3 s), so `surface_temp_c` never steps; this departs from "nothing steps between frames" for the gas values themselves because the contract was ambiguous (see Open question 24). Evidence: `sim/tests/test_atmosphere.py` 25 OK, doubling CO2 adds 3.0 C to `gas_c`, values clamp.
3. **[REVISED] G3 — Climate-driven spawning.** Edit `clouds.py` and `simulation.py`: `spawn_per_min`, `cloud_target` and `type_weights` (all nine types, sum 1) become smooth functions of temperature, wind speed, humidity, aerosol and instability (calm slow, moderate fastest, gale tears small clouds apart; convective types need warmth and instability, sheets want cool stable humid air, cirrus nearly indifferent, `supercell` needs instability and shear); preset `cloud_mix` is the base; aerosol gives more, longer-lived clouds that rain later and less; world-width scaling of counts stays. Done when: over several seeds warm humid unstable air spawns clearly more than cold dry air, a gale lowers the count, and a default-climate world still holds about 9–21 clouds with the Phase E mix (40–60 % never rain, 15–30 % of cumulus tower). Landed with one change: `min_clouds` stays a floor per 1280 px but drops from 3 to 1 so climate steers the rest (`test_lifecycle` updated; `test_clouds` births/deaths and `test_view` particle thresholds loosened; the cb reachability check in `test_protocol_schema` now starts July 14:00). Evidence: `test_atmosphere` 25 OK including measured realised birth rates (warm humid unstable air spawns more than cold dry air, a gale lowers the count). The Phase E mix (40-60 % never rain, 15-30 % of cumulus tower) and the 9-21 cloud count were not re-measured and recorded after this change (Open question 22).
4. **[VERIFIED] G4 — Cloud merging.** Edit `clouds.py` and `simulation.py`: overlap test (both condensed, neither dissipating); the larger survives (`parts` up to 3, `merge` 0..1, absorbed puffs appended at r≈0 and swelling with `merge`, `puff_part`, `puffs` capped at 72); the absorbed cloud stays with `merging_into` and `dissipating` while `form` falls to 0, then leaves with a `deaths` entry carrying `merged_into`; water, ice and capacity add, lifetime only extends, a fair survivor that absorbs a rain cloud stops being fair, an absorbed cloud never precipitates; an `events` line. Done when: at the merge frame puffs, bounds, tint, opacity and darkness equal the previous frame's within a tiny epsilon, a stratus absorbed by a cumulus yields flat-band `puff_part` entries inside the cumulus, signatures stay unique, and no puff `r` jumps over a 5000-frame run. Evidence: `sim/tests/test_merge.py` green; a stratus absorbed by a cumulus rendered as a flat band of part-1 puffs swelling out of r~0 (stdlib rasteriser, now `tools/debug_plots/v23/merge_sheet_into_cumulus.png`, a diagnostic plot, not a viewer render); two bugs found by the schema test were fixed (`puff_part`/`puffs` length mismatch on the merge-start frame, `time_of_day` rounding to 1.0). Decided without asking: an absorbed cloud's own earlier parts are not re-listed in the survivor's `parts`; a cb absorbing a cb does not copy the anvil puffs; a towered survivor is baked (`morph_from/to` cleared) when it absorbs; merge hazard 0.006/s gives about 0.6-1.4 merges/min.
5. **[VERIFIED] G5 — Save and load, including v2.2 saves.** Edit `savestate.py` and `simulation.get_state/set_state` (clock, `user_c`, gases, `parts`, `merge`, `puff_part`, in-progress absorbed clouds, spawn state; a v2.2 file loads with the default climate, continues from its saved `surface_temp_c` and has no merges in flight). Done when: run N → save → load → run M equals an uninterrupted N+M run across a merge and across a day change, and an existing v2.2 save in `saves/` still loads. Evidence: `test_saveload.py` round trip across a merge is frame-identical. ## Phase H — Local, ray-traced lightning light and the Epilepsy-safe setting (viewer only)

**Project context:** `[UPDATE]` — extends the v2.3 build. Viewer only: no change to `sim/`, `PROTOCOL.md` or the wire format. The user's request (2026-10-10): "Instead of lightning being global it should appear under a cloud ... the closer it is the more bright so far away is a dot flashing and then close looks real also add in ray tracing to the lightning so that buildings can block light from point of orgin", plus an Epilepsy setting. Before this phase `Renderer.drawLightning` whitened the whole viewport (`Direct.brighten`) for any bolt anywhere in the world. Session: `local-ray-traced-lightning-light-epileps-20261010-100921`.

**What each part does**
| Part | Role |
|---|---|
| `Lightning.java` (new) | Per bolt three point lights (cloud-base origin, mid channel, just above the strike) with a windowed inverse-square falloff ending at 950 world px; a 1-D shadow map per light (4 x 384 directions on a square parametrisation: distance to the entry into / exit from the first occluder) built from convex building silhouettes and the terrain segments; a coarse light grid per render band, cached per bolt while the view stands still; the eased ghost fade; the Epilepsy-safe bolt tracker |
| `Renderer.java`, `Shared.java`, `Direct.java` | `drawLightning` draws the grid then the bolts; `Shared.bolts` is prepared once per frame; `Direct.lightSpread` / `lightRow` replace `Direct.brighten` |
| `Settings.java`, `AppShell.java`, `SettingsScreen.java` | `graphics.epilepsySafe` (default off, absent = off), pushed to the static `Renderer.epilepsySafe` in `applySettings`, toggle and hint on Settings > Graphics |
| `Main.java`, `Bench.java` | Test hook `--bolt X|settlement:K|obj:K[+DX][,ALPHA]` (a bolt added to the drawn frame, viewer side only) and `--epilepsy-safe` for diagnostic runs |
| `UiDriveTest.java` | 15 new checks (#88-#102) |

1. **[VERIFIED] H1 — Local light instead of the global flash.** `Lightning.prepare` (renderer 0) keeps one `Light` per bolt; a bolt whose reach does not touch the view yields nothing. Done when: a far off-screen bolt changes no pixel. Evidence: `--selftest-ui` #95 (bolt 1350 px off screen: 0 of 1 276 800 pixels differ), #96 (bolt 120 px beyond the edge spills light in from that edge); `viewer/snapshots/lightning/d_night_away.png` is pixel-identical to `d0_night_away_nobolt.png` (PIL diff bbox None).
2. **[VERIFIED] H2 — Falloff in world units.** Brightness = sum over the lights of `gain * (1 - d²/R²)² / (1 + d²/r0²)`, scaled by the bolt's alpha squared; lit pixels are multiplied by up to `1 + gain` (gain 0.2 by day .. 4 in the dark, from the scene light and the under-cloud veil) plus 26 % of a cold white for the air. The glow is bounded above by the host cloud's top. Evidence: #97 (sky gains 371 near the bolt, 20 beyond 420 px); `c_night_fit.png` (whole world in view: a small glow under one cloud), `a_night_village.png`, `b_day_village.png`.
3. **[VERIFIED] H3 — Ray-traced occlusion.** Occluders are the building silhouettes (`Lightning.silhouette`: body plus gable / tower / tank per kind, back row at 0.9, ruins lowered) and the terrain surface; the first building hit is lit on its facade (strongest at the edge facing the light), the ground a few pixels deep, everything behind is dark; a building the channel is drawn across is treated as behind the bolt. Shadow edges get four angular taps per grid node near the light and a two-direction blend far from it. Evidence: #90 (light 0 behind a house against 91 at its mirror point; grey 180 stays 180 / becomes 374), #91 (facing wall 125 > far wall 57 > 0), #92 (12 just under the surface, 0 deeper); shadow wedges looked at in `a_night_village.png` (3x crop: no stair steps), `b_day_village.png`, `g_real_sim_night_village.png` (real simulation).
4. **[VERIFIED] H4 — Both render paths, every band.** `Direct` path in place; Java2D fallback (`Lightning.Band.paint`, a translucent image) for targets without int pixels. Evidence: #93, #99 (a `TYPE_3BYTE_BGR` frame: 939 131 pixels lit), #98 (4 bands against 1: 0 pixels differ).
5. **[VERIFIED] H5 — Never pop.** A bolt the stream drops while bright fades on the viewer's clock. Evidence: #94 (24 frames, largest step 0.040).
6. **[VERIFIED] H6 — Epilepsy-safe lightning.** Setting persisted and wired; when on there is no light at all and each bolt is a dim lilac line (peak opacity 0.34, no white core) easing in over 0.40 s and out over 0.75 s, at most one new bolt per 0.45 s. Evidence: #88 (default off, absent key off, written and read back), #89 (toggle on the page), #100 (8 139 pixels within 12 px of the bolt, largest change 81 of 255), #101 (largest change per frame 0.021), #102 (ten bolts a second: at most 2 start in any second); `e_night_village_epilepsy_safe.png`, `f_settings_graphics.png` (the page fits).
7. **[VERIFIED] H7 — Tests.** `--selftest` from a scratch build of the final sources: `[ui-test] PASS (102 checks)`, `[selftest] PASS (32 steps)` against the real simulation with a temporary saves folder; `saves/` and `config/settings.json` unchanged.
8. **[ACTIVE] H8 — Cost.** Lightning slot of `--bench 400 --profile` (1 band, fake seed 1234, 1680x760, scale 1), a test bolt in every frame. Quiet machine: old flash 1.25-1.60 ms; new 1.38 ms (city close-up), 1.77 ms (village close-up, whole view lit), 2.61 ms (same while panning: the grid is re-traced every frame). Loaded machine, paired runs: village 2.46-3.14 before against 2.77-3.29 after, city 2.54-2.92 against 1.92-1.99. Frames without a bolt: one null test. Still open: the numbers were taken while a game was running on the same machine; a quiet re-run at Balanced (1.5x on a HiDPI display) was not made.
9. **[ACTIVE] H9 — `build.bat`.** Not run by the agent: a game was running from `viewer\out` the whole time. `run.bat` rebuilt `viewer\out` itself at 10:38:52 from the then-current sources (stamp written); the final sources differ from that build only in `UiDriveTest.java` and two lines of `Renderer.java` / `Lightning.java` (grid cache clean-up). The same `javac` command into `viewer\out_lightning` printed no warning. Done when: `build.bat` prints "Build OK" with the game closed.

**Phase H verification (2026-10-10):** `--selftest` PASS (102 UI checks + 32 steps); 11 pictures in `viewer/snapshots/lightning/`, each opened; bench figures in H8. Decided without asking (questions Q1-Q4 of the session, see Open questions 26): three lights per bolt; buildings and terrain block, trees do not; the Epilepsy-safe behaviour above; `build.bat` left alone while the game runs.

## Phase H2 — Branching lightning and the glow inside the cloud (viewer only)

**Project context:** `[UPDATE]` — extends Phase H. Viewer only: no change to `sim/`, `PROTOCOL.md` or the wire format (the stream still sends one polyline per bolt). The user's request (2026-10-10): "add in a Branch From each Lightning Orgin from the cloud and that branch can keep branching randomly or never hit the ground at all, make the lightning appear inside the orgin cloud and it will look foggy but light will appear in the cloud as if the lightning is in the cloud." Session: `branching-lightning-in-cloud-fog-glow-20261010-112947`. (Numbered H2 because Phase I was taken by the Debug mode the same morning.)

**What each part does**
| Part | Role |
|---|---|
| `Lightning.Tree` (new, in `Lightning.java`) | Built once per bolt from `Random(key(bolt) * 0x9E3779B97F4A7C15 + 0x5851F42D4C957F2D)`: the branches below the cloud, and the hidden channel inside the host cloud (trunk + 2-4 tendrils, kept where the cloud's drawn image is at least half opaque). Caps: 3 fork levels, 96 branch segments, 40 in-cloud segments |
| `Lightning.Light`, `Band.glow` | The light remembers its host cloud (`hostE`, drawn position); `cover(x, y)` = that cloud image's alpha. The hidden channel is a line light in the existing light grid: no shadows, `1.2 x strength x (1 - d²/230²)² x (0.6 / (1 + d²/22²) + 0.4 / (1 + d²/85²))`, the maximum over its segments, times `cover` at the node. With a hidden channel the three point lights fade out 170 px above the cloud base instead of at the cloud top |
| `Renderer.drawLightning` | Branches = two fills per bolt (glow, white core) of tapered outlines cached on the tree (`Tree.shape`, rebuilt only when the zoom changes); nothing is stroked inside the cloud (the clouds are painted before `drawLightning`, the grid lights their finished pixels). Epilepsy-safe: channel and branches as ONE path, two strokes |
| `UiDriveTest.java` | 9 new checks (#101-#109); #100 counts the branches as part of the bolt; the two clock checks moved to #110-#111 |

1. **[VERIFIED] H2.1 — Branches.** One branch always leaves the point of origin; `(int) (u² x 7)` more leave the channel at `0.06 + 0.78 x u^1.7` of its length (more of them high up). A branch steps `H/16` px (9..26) x 0.6..1.4, heading = 0.55 x last + 0.45 x trend + N(0, 0.42 rad), clamped to 77 degrees from straight down (never upward), the trend easing toward straight down; it spends a length budget and stops in mid-air, or 4 px above the terrain. At each step it forks with probability `(0.10 + 0.22 x u²) x 0.6^(level-1)`. One branch in ten off the channel may run to the ground. Strength falls to 28 % toward the tip and x 0.75 per fork; width follows it. Evidence: `--selftest-ui` #101 (80 bolts built twice: identical), #102 (1 to 16 branches a bolt, 117 forks of forks, at most 3 levels), #103 (346 branches: 334 end in mid-air, 12 on the ground, 0 points under the terrain, all run downward, at most 96 segments); `d_variety_six_bolts.png`.
2. **[VERIFIED] H2.2 — The discharge starts inside the cloud.** Evidence: #104 (hidden channel of 21 points in 3 lines, all where the cloud is opaque), #105 (a pixel on the channel gains 436, clear sky 45 px above the cloud top: 0 of 169 pixels change), #106 (45 892 pixels lit above the reach of the point lights, 0 outside the cloud's silhouette), #108 (Java2D fallback: the same pixel gains 293); `a_night_cb_forked.png` against `a0_night_cb_nobolt.png`, `a2_night_city_heavily_forked.png`, `b_day_cb_forked.png`, `c_night_zoomed_out.png`, `a3_night_cb_fading_alpha035.png` (follows the bolt's alpha), `f_real_sim_cb.png` (real simulation, bolt put in with `--bolt`).
3. **[VERIFIED] H2.3 — Same picture every frame, every band.** Evidence: #107 (two renders identical, 4 bands against 1: 0 pixels differ, one `Tree` instance across frames); #95 (a bolt 1350 px off screen still changes 0 pixels).
4. **[VERIFIED] H2.4 — Epilepsy-safe stays safe.** No hidden channel is built at all; the branches are as dim as the channel. A first version stroked the branches separately and reached 115 of 255 where strokes overlapped; drawn as one path the largest change is 81 as before. Evidence: #100 (81 of 255), #109 (0 pixels change above the point of origin, largest change 79), #110, #111; `e_night_epilepsy_safe.png`.
5. **[VERIFIED] H2.5 — Tests.** `--selftest` from the scratch build: `[ui-test] PASS (111 checks)`, `[selftest] PASS (32 steps)`; `saves/`, `config/settings.json`, `PROTOCOL.md`, `sim/*.py` unchanged (listing and md5).
6. **[ACTIVE] H2.6 — Cost.** Lightning slot of `--bench 400 --profile` (1 band, fake seed 1234, 1680x760, scale 1, a test bolt in every frame), before against after, alternating runs: bolt under the cumulonimbus 1.67 / 1.74 against 1.80 / 1.85 ms; village 1.71-1.82 against 2.14-2.33; the heaviest bolt found (at the 96-segment cap, over the city) 1.28-1.59 against 1.86-1.93, i.e. about +0.6 ms where +0.5 was asked. Stroking the branches per strength level cost +2.7 ms for that bolt; the cached outlines brought it down. Frames without a bolt: unchanged (one null test). The glow is traced once per bolt and view, like the rest of the grid. Still open: the heaviest bolt is 0.1 ms over; numbers taken while a game was running.
7. **[ACTIVE] H2.7 — `build.bat`.** Not run: a game was running from `viewer\out`, and from 11:56 a second session (Phase I) was editing the same source folder (at 12:01 the tree did not compile because of its unfinished `DebugScreen.java`). Built with the same `javac` flags into `viewer\out_branches` (full build at 11:55, no new warnings; afterwards only `Lightning.java`, `Renderer.java`, `UiDriveTest.java` recompiled against it). Done when: `build.bat` prints "Build OK" with the game closed and Phase I compiling, and `--selftest-ui` passes on that build.

**Phase H2 verification (2026-10-10):** `--selftest` PASS (111 UI checks + 32 steps); 9 pictures in `viewer/snapshots/lightning/branches/`, each opened. Decided without asking: see Open questions 34.

## Phase I — Debug game mode (viewer only)

**Project context:** `[UPDATE]` — extends the v2.3 build plus Phase H. Viewer only: no change to `sim/`, `PROTOCOL.md`, `assets/` or the wire format, no Python process, no save slot. The user's request (2026-10-10): "Create a Debug Gamemode that appears if you click the cloud in the upper left add in the cloud though you need to click it 10 times before it appears, in the game mode you can see all cloud types change their seed PNGS Of their dissipating and forming stages and also features like lightning and all building types cars. ADD IN ALL THE FEATURES IN THIS GAME". Before this phase `MainMenuScreen.java` has four game modes (Sandbox, Hurricane Formation, Cloud Formations, World Simulation) and no cloud in its upper left; the lesson modes open through `AppShell.beginMode` and leave through `AppShell.leaveNow`. The specification and the decisions already taken are in `.sa-asker/briefs/debug-mode.md` (not reopened here). Two facts shape the plan. First, another agent (session `branching-lightning-in-cloud-fog-glow-20261010-112947`) owns `Lightning.java` and is editing `Renderer.java`, `UiDriveTest.java`, possibly `Main.java` and Phase H of this file, so the mode lives in **new files** and every edit to an existing file is small, additive and made after re-reading the spot. Second, a cloud's skeleton is generated by the viewer's built-in `FakeSim` (private inner class `FCloud`, seeded `Random(cs)`), not by the Python sim, so a Debug "seed" is a built-in-generator seed; the painters are the game's real ones (`CloudPainter`, `BuildingPainter`, `CarPainter`, `TornadoPainter`, `Renderer`, `HurricanePainter`, `WorldPainter`). Plan session: `debug-mode-plan-md-phase-i-20261010-113719`.

**What each part does**
| Part | Role |
|---|---|
| `MainMenuScreen.java`, `AppShell.java`, `Settings.java` | The drawn cloud logo and its click counter; the fifth row `Debug` once unlocked (mode count 4 or 5 instead of `MODE_NAMES.length`); `AppShell.startDebug()` and a `debug` branch in `beginMode`; `modes.debugUnlocked` in `config/settings.json` (written only when true, absent = locked) |
| `DebugScreen.java` (new) | The mode's screen: a `ViewerPanel.Scene` like `HurricaneScreen`, tab bar, per-tab controls, Esc back to the menu; steps its own scripted `FakeSim` worlds from `paintScene` (no thread, no process) |
| `DebugClouds.java` (new) | Clouds tab: type / seed / life-stage controls, the main picture, the forming and dissipating thumbnail strip, background rendering with a cache |
| `DebugExport.java` (new) | Writes the PNG sequences and contact sheets for one type or all nine into a given folder |
| `DebugLineup.java` (new) | Buildings and plants tab, Cars tab: labelled line-ups drawn with `BuildingPainter` and `CarPainter` |
| `DebugWorlds.java` (new) | Lightning, Weather and World tabs on live scripted `FakeSim` worlds drawn by the real `Renderer` through `ParallelRenderer`; Hurricane and World map tabs on `FakeHurricane` / `FakeWorld` |
| `FakeSim.java` | One new static method, `debugCloud(type, seed, lifeFrac)`, that returns a `Model.Cloud` for a seed without touching the world's random stream |
| `BuildingPainter.java`, `CarPainter.java`, `CloudPainter.java` | Additive only: canonical lists of kinds / variants (`KINDS`, `HOUSE_VARIANTS`, `TREE_VARIANTS`, `VARIANTS`) and `CloudPainter.sheetSpec` made package-private so the Debug picture and the contact sheet cannot drift apart |
| `UiSnapshot.java`, `Main.java` | New `--ui-snapshot` screens (`menu-debug-locked`, `menu-debug-unlocked`, `debug-<tab>`); one added line that also runs `DebugTest` in the `--selftest-ui` / `--selftest` branch |
| `DebugTest.java` (new) | The checks of the Definition of done, in a scratch config and saves folder |

1. **[VERIFIED] I1 — Feature inventory (feature -> Debug tab and control).** Before any tab is built, list what the game can draw and simulate and record it in the implementer's session as a table with the columns feature, source (class / method / command), Debug tab, control. Read `CloudPainter`, `BuildingPainter`, `CarPainter`, `TornadoPainter`, `Renderer` (`drawParticles`, `drawWindField`, `drawStreamlines`, `drawPond`, `drawPuddle`, `drawSnowpack`, `drawWindsock`, `drawShade`, `drawBackdrop`, `drawCelestial`, `drawTornadoes`, `drawCars`, `drawLightning`), `Model` (`CLOUD_TYPES`, `STAGES`, `Frame`, `Obj`, `Car`, `Tornado`), `FakeSim` (`SCENES`, the `handle` commands `aerosol gas hour humidity season spawn speed temp tornado wind`), `Preset.IDS` and `Preset.Biome.IDS`, `WorldPainter.LAYER_IDS`, the ten `FakeHurricane` stage ids, and Phases A–H above. Start the table from: nine cloud types and five stages, fair-weather flag, cumulus -> cumulonimbus morph, merged clouds (`parts`, `puffPart`), supercell (rotation, wall cloud, overshoot, hook); lightning, normal and Epilepsy-safe; ten building kinds, nine house variants, nine tree / plant variants, damage 0..1, lit windows at night, live parts (`BuildingPainter.hasLive`); six car variants, states `driving` / `stopped` / `tossed` / `wrecked`, headlights and wipers; tornado EF0–EF5 and its stages, debris, damage scars; rain, snow, mixed, graupel; wind field, arrows, streamlines; pond, puddle, snowpack, windsock; time of day with sun, moon and stars, months, eight biomes, the region presets, terrain kinds, the backdrop picture, under-cloud darkening, the Climate panel; ten hurricane stages in the outside and inside views; seven world-map layers. Done when: the table is in the session; every row names a tab and a control or says "left out" with the reason (expected leave-outs to judge, not assume: save slots, Settings screens and key rebinding, sandbox side panels and minimap, terrain kinds hilly and mountainous which only the Python sim builds — see Open question 30); every painter class named above has at least one row.
2. **[VERIFIED] I2 — The cloud logo and its click counter.** In `MainMenuScreen.paint` draw a small cloud in the upper left, in the menu's own style (filled ellipses as in `paintEmpty`, or the line style of `modeIcon`), and register it with `ui.hit(...)`. The title baseline `ty` is 104 when `h >= 640` and 64 in the compact layout, the title font is 40 or 33, and the window cannot be smaller than 1040 x 620 (`AppShell.createWindow`), so the logo gets roughly y 22–62 in the roomy layout and must be smaller and higher (about y 6–30) in the compact one; the hit area may be a few pixels larger than the drawing but never reaches the title or the `GAME MODE` rows. The counter is a field on `AppShell` (e.g. `debugClicks`), not on `MainMenuScreen`, because `showMenu` builds a new `MainMenuScreen` every time the player returns from a game. Clicks 1–4 do nothing visible (no toast, no hover cue, no text); clicks 5–9 call `app.toast(...)` saying how many more are needed ("5 more…" through "1 more…"); click 10 unlocks (I3). A click anywhere else is not counted. Done when: `DebugTest` computes the logo rectangle, the title text rectangle (`Ui.width` of "PRECIPITATION" in the title font) and the mode-row rectangles for 1040x620, 1280x720, 1680x760 and 1920x1080 and finds no intersection; nine clicks leave no Debug text anywhere on the menu and produce toasts only from the fifth; a click on a mode row does not advance the counter.
3. **[VERIFIED] I3 — Unlock, persistence and the fifth mode row.** `Settings.java`: add `boolean debugUnlocked` to the `modes` block, read with `Json.bool(mo, "debugUnlocked", false)` next to `showcaseType` and written **only when true** (a locked player's `settings.json` must not gain a key). `MainMenuScreen.java`: keep `MODE_NAMES` / `MODE_NOTES` at four entries, add `DEBUG = 4` with its own name and note ("Debug", "every cloud, seed, building, car and effect"), and replace each `MODE_NAMES.length` use (`setMode`, the Tab / Shift+Tab cycle in `key`, the row loop in `paint`) with a `modeCount()` that is 5 only when `app.settings.debugUnlocked`. No letter key is bound to Debug and the hint lines do not mention it; it is reached by click, Tab or Enter on its row. Add the `modeIcon` drawing (a cloud with a small bolt) and a `debugPage` in the style of `cloudsPage` (what the tabs are, a Start Debug button, "Not saved. Esc brings you back here."). Click 10 sets the flag, calls `app.settingsChanged()`, selects the new row and toasts that Debug is unlocked. Paper check of the fit with five rows: compact layout rows end at 112 + 5 x 60 = 412, the Settings / Quit buttons (34 high) at 420–454; roomy layout rows end at 180 + 5 x 70 = 530 and the buttons at 538–578, inside a 640 high canvas. Done when: locked, the menu has exactly four rows and Tab cycles four modes; the 10th click adds the row; the flag is written, read back by a second `Settings.load`, and a settings file without the key loads locked; five rows and both buttons fit at 1040x620 and at 1680x760 (rectangles checked, picture looked at in I16).
4. **[VERIFIED] I4 — The Debug mode shell.** `AppShell.startDebug()` modeled on `startShowcase`: a no-op `FrameSource` (a small private class — `start` and `send` do nothing, `describe()` returns "Debug (built-in)") goes to `beginMode("debug", src, "Debug", null)`, which gets a new branch next to the hurricane / world / showcase ones (`DebugScreen ds = new DebugScreen(this, gs); p.scene = ds; s = ds;`) and `menuMode = 4` for `debug` (the line `menuMode = mode.equals("hurricane") ? 1 : mode.equals("world") ? 3 : 2` must not drop Debug into 2). `DebugScreen` implements `ViewerPanel.Scene` and `Screen`; the world tabs step their own `FakeSim` instances (they implement `Scripted`: `hello()`, `step()`) from `paintScene` at 30 steps per second of `dt`, so there is no thread and no process to stop. Layout: a tab bar on top (Clouds, Lightning, Buildings, Cars, Weather, World, Hurricane, World map; keys 1–8 unless a text field has focus, since the seed field takes digits), controls on the right, the picture in the rest; it must hold at 1040x620 (compare `ShowcaseScreen.topH/rightW/bottomH`). Esc calls `app.leaveNow()` like `ShowcaseScreen.key` does; F12 still saves a screenshot through `AppShell.screenshot`. `session.slot` is null, so `saveNow` already answers "diagnostic session". Done when: `DebugTest` starts Debug from the unlocked menu, finds `app.session.mode.equals("debug")` and `app.session.slot == null`, no `SimProcess` was created, Esc returns to the menu with `menuMode == MainMenuScreen.DEBUG`, and every tab paints one frame at 1040x620 and at 1680x760 without an exception.
5. **[VERIFIED] I5 — Seeded cloud generator hook.** In `FakeSim.java` add one package-private static method `debugCloud(String type, long seed, double lifeFrac)` returning a `Model.Cloud`. Why: the skeleton comes from the constructor of the private inner `FakeSim.FCloud` (`new Random(cs)`), and `newCloud` loops until `signatures.add(...)`, so a seed given to a running world is not reproducible; Python is not allowed. How: build the flat showcase stage (`new FakeSim(seed, 0.40, 1600, 900, type, null)` as `FakeSim.showcase` does), construct `new FCloud(1, seed, W / 2.0, Double.NaN, type, 0, false)`, set what `startShowcaseCloud` sets (`towers = false`, `rains` true for every type except cirrus and altocumulus, `developAt`, `tornadic`), set `prog = lifeFrac` and `stageIdx = stageForFrac()`, then `json(sb, fz)` and `Model.parseCloud(Json.obj(...))`. `FCloud.tick`, `shear` and the darkness glide are stateful, so set `shear = shearTarget()` and `darkShown = NaN` directly instead of ticking. The reported `Cloud.seed` is `seed & 0x7fffffff`. Re-read `FakeSim.java` immediately before the edit; the lightning agent may also touch it. Done when: for each of the nine types a ladder of life fractions gives `form` rising from 0 to 1 by about 0.24 and falling again from 0.84; two calls with the same arguments give equal `puffs` and fields; two seeds give different `puffs`; the stage names follow `stageForFrac` (forming < 0.1, growing < 0.3, mature < 0.45, precipitating < 0.82, dissipating after, and no precipitating stage for the two types that do not rain, which the tab says out loud).
6. **[VERIFIED] I6 — Clouds tab: type, seed, life stage and the main picture.** `DebugClouds` shows a button per type from `Model.CLOUD_TYPES` (prefix `Model.CLOUD_PREFIX`, note `Model.CLOUD_NOTE`; all nine, supercell included); a seed row with previous / next (+-1), random (`ThreadLocalRandom`) and a typed number (`Ui.TextField` digits only through `ui.field`), the current seed always shown; a life slider (`ui.slider`) 0..1 with five stage chips from `Model.STAGES` that jump to forming 0.05, growing 0.2, mature 0.38, precipitating 0.6, dissipating 0.92; Play / Pause and a speed (`ui.seg`) with the same 0.25–8 steps as `ShowcaseScreen.SPEEDS`; a time-of-day choice (day, dusk, night) that sets the `CloudPainter.Light.of(tod, 0)` and the sky gradient as `CloudPainter.sheet` does. The picture is `CloudPainter.render(Spec, Scratch)` with a `Spec` built by `CloudPainter.sheetSpec` (drop `private`, one word, rather than copying it), a fixed `sp.time`, a fixed `sp.wind` and the current `CloudPainter.quality` (never flipped; shown on screen). `render` returns null below `form` 0.004: draw "not formed yet". Rendering runs on a background thread with its own `Scratch`, results cached by (type, seed, life rounded to 1/60, tod, quality); the previous image stays up while the next renders. A readout shows name, stage, life %, `form`, `anvil`, precipitation kind and rate from the `Model.Cloud`. Done when: all nine types at all five chips draw non-empty pictures (life 0 excepted); a different seed changes the picture; the same seed twice gives `CloudPainter.maxChannelDiff == 0`; scrubbing 0 -> 1 never blocks a paint for more than one frame (the picture lags, the UI does not).
7. **[VERIFIED] I7 — Thumbnail strip of the forming and dissipating stages.** Under the main picture, one row of six cells for forming (life 0.05 to 0.24), the mature cell (0.38), and six cells for dissipating (0.84 to 0.97), all rendered with one shared scale and base line (computed from the mature puffs, as `CloudPainter.sheet` fits its cells) so growth and decay are visible side by side. Each cell is labelled with stage and life %, a click sets the life slider, and cells render in the background through the same cache as I6 (a "…" cell until ready). Done when: for each type the strip has 13 cells; the summed alpha of the forming cells rises and of the dissipating cells falls (test, with a tolerance of 5 %); changing the seed changes the strip; the strip of a given type and seed is identical to the images I8 writes for it.

Buttons: "Export this cloud" and "Export all types". Folder: `new File(new File(app.root, "screenshots"), "debug")`, the `debug` subfolder of where `AppShell.screenshot` writes; the exporter takes the folder as an argument so tests never touch the project's `screenshots/`. Never overwrite: an existing name gets `-2`, `-3` as in `screenshot()`. Work runs on `app.io` (one background thread, in order), a toast shows progress and the final toast names the folder ("Exported 14 pictures to screenshots/debug"), posted with `SwingUtilities.invokeLater` as `screenshot()` does. Step pictures are RGBA PNGs of the cloud alone; the contact sheet has the sky behind it (see Open question 32). Done when: an export into a scratch folder gives the expected count (13 + 1 per type, 126 for all nine), every file decodes with `ImageIO.read`, none is fully transparent, names carry type, seed, stage and step, and two exports of one seed decode to identical pixels.

12. **[VERIFIED] I12 — Weather tab.** Scripted `FakeSim` worlds (`FakeSim.SCENES`: `merge`, `supercell`, `cars`; constructor arguments `showcaseType`, `presetId`, `month`, `hour`, `scene`) driven with the same JSON commands the sandbox keys send (`FakeSim.handle`: `temp`, `wind`, `humidity`, `gas`, `aerosol`, `spawn`, `tornado`, `hour`, `season`). Controls and pictures: precipitation kind buttons rain, snow, mixed, graupel (the protocol's `precip_type` values; how `FCloud.precipType` picks them — surface temperature <= 0.5 gives snow, a cumulonimbus or supercell with its `hail` flag gives graupel, a base below the freezing line gives mixed — decides the temperature command and the seed the tab uses; graupel needs a hail seed, found by scanning seeds through `FakeSim`); tornado at EF0–EF5 drawn with `TornadoPainter.render` / `paint` from a `Model.Tornado` per level (`windMs` mid-band of `FakeSim.efOf`'s thresholds 38 / 50 / 61 / 75 / 90, `strength = (windMs - 22) / 78` as `FakeSim.FTornado.windMs` defines it, stages funnel / tornado / roping) with debris and a damage scar (`TornadoPainter.paintScar`); the `merge` scene (stratus into cumulus, two cumulus into one) and the `supercell` scene with its tornado; wind field, arrows, streamlines and labels as `Renderer.showWind` / `showArrows` / `showLabels` toggles; the ground water objects pond, puddle and snowpack and the windsock (`Renderer.drawPond` and friends) in a scene with wet or snowy ground. 18. **[ACTIVE] I18 — Compile, build and the desktop icon.** Compile with `javac -encoding UTF-8 -d viewer/out_debug viewer/src/precip/*.java` from the project root: no warning, and a failure inside a file this phase did not touch means the other agent is mid-edit — wait and retry, do not fix it. `build.bat` / `build.sh` are left alone while a game may be running from `viewer\out` (Phase H9); `run.bat` rebuilds `viewer\out` by itself on the next launch because any `viewer\src\precip\*.java` newer than `build.stamp` triggers it, and its `javac ... *.java` glob picks up the new `Debug*.java` files without a change to the script. The desktop icon `C:\Users\lenard\Desktop\Precipitation.lnk` (target `D:\Lenard\Projects\Precipitation\run.bat`, start in the project folder, icon `assets\precipitation.ico`) is **not edited or recreated**; Debug is reached through it: double-click the icon, click the cloud in the upper left ten times, choose Debug.
