## Phase J — Advanced graphics settings (viewer only)

**Project context:** `[UPDATE]` — extends the build with Phases H, H2 and I. Viewer only: no change to `sim/`, `PROTOCOL.md`, `assets/`, `saves/` or the wire format, no Python process. The user's request (2026-10-10, verbatim): "make a Advanced Settings For Graphics that contain Max Particle, Render, turn Off Lightning Detail, Global lightning, Branching Off Lightning, Dont Render what you cant see, Simple Clouds, No tint, No Flash, no snow, no trails, no Shining Particles, No Puddle impact". Before this phase Settings has four tabs (`SettingsScreen.TABS` = Background, Graphics, Game, Controls) and Graphics holds Cloud quality, Render resolution (`Settings.renderScale`, an index 0..2 into `SCALE_VALUES` = 1.0 / 1.5 / native), Fullscreen, Show FPS, Dim under clouds (+ strength) and Epilepsy-safe lightning. The specification and the decisions already taken are in `.sa-asker/briefs/advanced-graphics-plan.md` (not reopened here): a fifth tab "Advanced" after Graphics that fits the 900x600 dialog and the 1024x581 minimum window; persisted as the nested object `graphics.advanced` in `config/settings.json` with **no `Settings.VERSION` bump** (a file without the keys loads with today's look); the existing pattern is canonical (a `Settings` field, pushed by `AppShell.applySettings` into a `static volatile` flag, as `Renderer.epilepsySafe`, `CloudPainter.quality` and `ViewerPanel.maxRenderScale` are); every option defaults to today's behaviour except #6, which defaults on and must be pixel-identical on and off. Three facts found in the code shape the plan. First, the parallel render bands read the flags on their own threads, so a flag flipped mid-frame would draw two bands differently; the options are therefore read once per frame by renderer 0 into `Shared` (`prepareShared`), never by a band from the static. Second, the scenery tiles, funnel images, lightning lights and cloud images are all cached and none of them knows about the new options, so one version counter (`Advanced.version`) is bumped on every change and each cache checks it (J3). Third, the particle list on the wire has no particle id and its `rot` field changes every few ticks (`sim/particles.py` writes a fresh row per particle each tick; `rot` is stepped every third or sixth tick), so "a stable per-particle hash" must come from what stays the same while a particle falls (J5). Plan session: `advanced-graphics-plan-md-phase-j-20261010-152416`. Read while planning: `Settings`, `AppShell.applySettings`, `SettingsScreen`, `ViewerPanel.paintComponent`, `Renderer` (`prepareShared`, `drawWorld`, `drawParticles`, `drawRipples`, `drawPuddle`, `drawSnowpack`, `drawLightning`, `drawBackdrop`), `Shared`, `Scene`, `Lit`, `Lightning`, `Direct.lightRow`, `CloudPainter.render` / `signature`, `SharedClouds.prepare`, `UiDriveTest`, `UiSnapshot`, `Main`.

**What each part does**
| Part | Role |
|---|---|
| `Advanced.java` (new) | The 13 options as one small immutable value (`maxParticles` percent 100/75/50/25, `renderScale` 0 / 0.75 / 0.5, and eleven booleans), `static volatile Advanced cur` (the published value), `static volatile int version` (bumped whenever `cur` changes), `DEFAULT`, `equals`. No logic of its own |
| `Settings.java` | One field `Advanced adv` (mutable copy edited by the tab), read from and written to `graphics.advanced`; written only when some value differs from the default, so a player who never opens the tab gets no new key (the rule Phase I set for `modes.debugUnlocked`); every value clamped on read |
| `AppShell.java` | `applySettings` publishes `Advanced.cur`, bumps `version` on a change, and derives `ViewerPanel.maxRenderScale` from the Graphics page and the Advanced render scale together; `styleBackdrop` ANDs `bgTint` with the No tint option |
| `SettingsScreen.java` | `TABS` gets "Advanced" at index 2 (`ADVANCED = 2`, `GAME = 3`, `CONTROLS = 4`); `paintAdvanced`: two value controls, then eleven switches in two columns, a one-line hint of the switch under the pointer |
| `Renderer.java`, `Shared.java`, `Scene.java` | `Shared.adv` (this frame's snapshot) and `Shared.bakedStyle`; `prepareShared` re-bakes on a version change; the switches inside `drawParticles`, `drawRipples`, `drawSnowpack`, `drawWorld`, `drawBackdrop`; `Scene.noSnow` |
| `Lightning.java`, `Direct.java` | `prepare` takes the options: no light, global level, unshadowed lights, no glow, no branches; the grid cache and the shadow maps are keyed by the version; the light colour can be neutral |
| `CloudPainter.java`, `SharedClouds.java` | A tier below quality Low (`simple`), part of `Spec.signature`; the cloud light without colour casts for No tint |
| `Lit.java`, `BuildingPainter.java`, `TerrainPainter.java` | `Lit` carries the No tint flag; the snow of roofs, trees, roads and mountain caps obeys No snow |
| `ViewerState.java`, `Model.java` (added while building, J5) | `Model.Frame.particleRank` and `ViewerState.rankParticles`: the rank 0..99 every particle keeps from frame to frame while Max particles is below 100 % |
| `Main.java`, `Bench.java`, `UiSnapshot.java` | A diagnostic `--adv name[=value],...` option (like `--epilepsy-safe`), a `settings-advanced` screen for `--ui-snapshot` |
| `AdvancedTest.java` (new), `UiDriveTest.java` | The checks of this phase, called from the one expression in `Main` that already runs `UiDriveTest` and `DebugTest`; `UiDriveTest` changes in one place only (the pixel click on the Controls tab moves down one tab row, J19) |

**The 13 options: the hook, and what must be rebuilt when the option flips**

Stored under `graphics.advanced` as `maxParticles`, `renderScale`, `lightningDetailOff`, `globalLightning`, `branchingOff`, `cullHidden` (the only one that defaults to true), `simpleClouds`, `noTint`, `noFlash`, `noSnow`, `noTrails`, `noShine`, `noPuddleImpact`.

| # | Option | Hook (file, method: what changes there) | Caches to invalidate on a flip |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Max particles | `Renderer.drawParticles`: a pure keep test (`rainHash` of the fall-line cell and the size bits) right after the view rectangle test in each of its four loops (in-cloud pass ~1702, rain/sleet ~1741, the rest ~1804); `Shared.adv.particlePct` | none (sprite atlases are keyed by kind and size; nothing is baked from particles) |
| 2 | Render | `AppShell.applySettings` computes the cap; `ViewerPanel.paintComponent` (~260) already renders at `maxRenderScale` and stretches the buffer when the display scale is higher, so it needs no change, only a cap below 1.0 | `buf` is reallocated by the size test (~267); scenery tiles, sprite atlas (`spQ`) and cloud images re-key themselves on the new view scale; check there is no blank frame (J6) |
| 3 | Lightning detail off | `Lightning.Light.build`: skip `addSolid` / `addTerrain` (shadow maps stay empty, `Emitter.open = true`, `Band.trace` skips the taps); `Lightning.Light` constructor: no cloud mask, so `Tree.grow` builds no hidden channel and `Band.glow` has nothing to trace | `Lightning.State.lights` (Light, Emitter shadow maps, Tree) and `Band.grids` |
| 4 | Global lightning | `Lightning.prepare`: no `sees` test, no `build`, every present bolt kept; `Lightning.Band.compute`: a flat grid (`k[]` all the same value, no `trace`) that the unchanged `apply` / `paint` spread over the view; `Frame.global` | `Band.grids` (a grid traced for the local light must not be reused), `State.lights` |
| 5 | Lightning branching off | `Lightning.Tree.grow` (MAX_DEPTH / MAX_SEG ~371): the branches are grown as today and then dropped (`lines`, `depth`, `grounded`, `strength`, `box`, `segments` cleared) so the random stream, and with it the hidden channel in the cloud, is the same with the option on or off; `Tree.shape` already returns with `coreShape == null` for an empty tree and `drawLightning` already tests it | `State.lights`, `Safe.known` (the epilepsy-safe trees are grown from the same method) |
| 6 | Don't render what you can't see | `Renderer.drawParticles` (a per-cloud opaque map for a particle whose whole footprint is covered), `Renderer.prepareShared` (visible particle list); the audit of what is already culled is J11 | the opaque map hangs on `CloudPainter.Image` and dies with it; nothing else |
| 7 | Simple clouds | `CloudPainter.render` (~323): `simple` selects a grid coarser and lighter than quality 0 (`cellPx` ~482, `cap` ~483, `oct` ~496, `steps` ~736) and a flat colour; `Spec.signature` (~178) adds it only when true, so every existing hash is unchanged | cloud images rebuild by themselves (signature changes, async, cross-faded); `Shared.Thumb` portraits (same signature); the Debug cloud cache key (Phase I, I6) |
| 8 | No tint | `Lit` constructor / `Lit.lit(Color)` (~34): skip the night-blue and dusk-orange mixes; `Lit.differs`; `Renderer.prepareShared` (~353-354) builds `Lit` and `CloudPainter.Light.of` with the flag; `Renderer.drawBackdrop` (~831-840): no dusk veil; `Lightning.TINT_*` (~766, used at ~982) and `Direct.lightRow` (`a8 = a - (a >> 3)`): neutral light colour | scenery tiles (`Shared.far/near/scenery/ground`), the tornado funnel cache (`Renderer` ~461: its `sig` has `lit.light` and `lit.day` but not the cast), cloud images (the light key already holds the colours), the lightning grid colour (applied per frame, nothing cached) |
| 9 | No flash | `Lightning.prepare`: after the bolt loop `out.lights = new Light[0]` and no `build` (the expensive shadow maps are never made); strokes and branches are drawn as today; epilepsy-safe is tested first and wins | `State.lights` |
| 10 | No snow | `Renderer.drawParticles` third loop (`case 2`, ~1813); `Renderer.drawWorld` object loop (`case "snowpack"`, ~690) and the sparkle in `drawSnowpack` (~1224); `Scene.snowAt` (ground cover and the altitude term); `BuildingPainter.Ctx` (~52, roofs and trees); `TerrainPainter` ~124 and ~159 (mountain caps); `Renderer.coverLevels` (~503-514, the painted levels) | scenery and ground tiles and `Shared.bakedGround` / `bakedObj` (all through the version) |
| 11 | No trails | `Renderer.drawParticles` rain loop: no `blitStreak` and no head core, the drop sprite (`blitDrop`) drawn at every zoom (`rnDrop` forced to 1 in `rainBegin` ~1866, a minimum radius); sleet: no `sleetPath` entries (~1747-1757, 1794-1800), the sleet sprite dot of the third loop stays | none (drop sprites share `rainSprites`, which clears itself at its byte limit) |
| 12 | No shining particles | `Renderer.drawParticles`: no `glint` (~1769), no `addGlint` (~1789) and no "no matter what" fallback glint (~1792), no `blitGlint` (~1793); the ice-crystal twinkle in the in-cloud pass (~1712-1716), sprite kind 7 (`drawIceTwinkle` ~2231) and `drawIceCrystal` (~2211); the sparkle dots of `drawSnowpack` | none (glints are drawn from `glintXY` every frame, the atlas is only asked for kind 7 when a twinkle is wanted) |
| 13 | No puddle impact | `Renderer.drawRipples` (~1188, called from `drawPond` ~1183 and `drawPuddle` ~1210): return at once; `drawParticles` third loop `case 5` (~1817, splash particles). The puddle and pond shapes stay; the legend sprite of kind 5 (~2535) stays | none (the objects are drawn live, not baked) |

**How the lightning options combine** (decided here so that the code has one order): epilepsy-safe is tested first and wins over everything that concerns light; branching off applies to the safe bolts too.
| Setting | Light | Bolt |
|---|---|---|
| Epilepsy-safe on (any other lightning option) | none | dim, eased, one at a time (Phase H6), branches unless #5 |
| No flash | none, no shadow maps built | drawn normally: stream alpha, white core, branches unless #5 |
| Global lightning (without No flash) | every present bolt lights the whole view by one level, no falloff, no shadows, no glow | normal |
| Lightning detail off (alone) | the three point lights with their falloff, no shadows, no glow in the cloud | normal |
| Global and detail off together | as Global (nothing is left to shadow) | normal |
| none | today | today |

1. **[VERIFIED] J1 — Baseline and a safe place to build.** Before the first edit: check no game is running from the folder (`powershell -NoProfile -Command "Get-CimInstance Win32_Process -Filter \"Name='java.exe' or Name='javaw.exe'\" | Select-Object ProcessId,CommandLine"`, look for `precip.Main`); compile the untouched sources with `javac -encoding UTF-8 -d viewer/out_j_base viewer/src/precip/*.java` (the "before" build for J21); record `config/settings.json` (size and SHA-256; seen while planning: 1 456 bytes, SHA-256 starting `b23af22c`) and `saves/` (every file with size and time; seen: `simulation-1-tmogj6`). A game was running while this plan was written (that slot's `state.sav` was autosaved at 15:36 and `settings.json` rewritten at 15:37, still 1 456 bytes), so expect the answer to be yes and expect both listings to move for that reason alone. Why: later phases compared their pictures to a build made after the edit and could not prove "nothing changed"; this phase's strongest claim is that every default looks exactly as before. Phase H9, H2.7 and I18 left `build.bat` alone because a game was running, and this phase builds into `viewer/out_advanced` for the same reason. Done when: the baseline classes exist and compile with no warning, the two listings are written into the session, and the running-game answer is recorded. Evidence (Implementation session `advanced-graphics-implement-phase-j-20261010-153942`, 2026-10-10): no `precip.Main` process was running at the start or at the end (only the VS Code Java language server); `viewer/out_j_base` built from the untouched sources (174 classes, javac 25.0.3, no output without `-Xlint`); `config/settings.json` 1 456 bytes, SHA-256 `b23af22c…`, written 15:37:30; `saves/simulation-1-tmogj6` five files (state.sav 1 013 651 bytes, 15:36:37). Both listings are in the session.
2. **[VERIFIED] J2 — The settings model and its file format.** `Advanced.java` (new, package-private, immutable, with a copy-and-change helper) and in `Settings.java` a field `adv`, read inside the existing `graphics` block of `read` (next to `epilepsySafe`, ~146) from the nested object `advanced` with `Json.num` / `Json.bool` and clamped (`maxParticles` to {100, 75, 50, 25}, `renderScale` index 0..2, an unknown or missing key keeps the default, a non-object `advanced` is ignored), and written by `toJson` (~223) as `gr.put("advanced", ...)` only when `!adv.equals(Advanced.DEFAULT)`. `Settings.VERSION` stays 1. Why: the brief settles "no bump"; writing the block only when it differs keeps `config/settings.json` byte-identical for a player who never opens the tab, as Phase I did for `debugUnlocked`. Done when: `AdvancedTest` shows a default `Settings` writes no `advanced` key; a file `{"version":1,"graphics":{"cloudQuality":2}}` loads with every option at its default and `cullHidden` true; every option set to a non-default is written and read back by a second `Settings.load`; out-of-range values clamp; `Settings.VERSION == 1`. Evidence: `Advanced.java` (immutable, `with` / `toggle` / `withParticles` / `withRenderScale`, `read`, `toJson`, `parse`), `Settings.adv`. `AdvancedTest` checks 1-5 and 7: a default file has no `advanced` key and `VERSION == 1`; `{"version":1,"graphics":{"cloudQuality":2}}` loads with every default; all 13 keys written and read back; each of the 11 switches round-trips alone; 60 % -> 50, scale 7 -> 2, -40 % -> 25, scale -3 -> 0, unknown keys and wrong types keep the default, `"advanced":5` is ignored.
3. **[REVISED] J3 — Publishing, one version counter, one snapshot per frame.** `AppShell.applySettings` (~316) gets, next to the `epilepsySafe` line and before the `panel == null` return (so the menu, the lesson modes and Debug obey it too, as H did): `Advanced.publish(settings.adv)` which sets `cur` and increments `version` only if the value changed, and the render cap (J6). `Shared` gets `Advanced adv = Advanced.DEFAULT` and `long bakedStyle`; `Renderer.prepareShared` reads `Advanced.cur` once at the top (`s.adv`), and when `s.adv`'s version differs from `s.bakedStyle` it takes the "stale" path of the baked scenery at once (`s.ridgeVersion++; s.sceneVersion++; coverLevels(s, true)`, ~373) and clears `s.funnelCache`, bypassing the one-second debounce of the async path (~378), then passes the version on to `Lightning.prepare` and `Lightning.Frame`. The bands and the painters only ever read `sh.adv`; the only static read outside `prepareShared` is where a worker thread has no `Shared` (`BuildingPainter.Ctx`, `CloudPainter.render`), and those are covered by the re-bake and by the cloud signature. A diagnostic `--adv name[=value],...` in `Main` (next to `--epilepsy-safe`, ~145; `--adv particles=50,noTrails,global`) sets `Advanced.cur` for `--snapshot`, `--bench` and `--ui-snapshot`. Why: without this, flipping No tint or No snow would change the live objects at once and the baked ground a second or more later, and a band that read the static a moment later than another would draw a different picture. Done when: a test flips each option between two renders of the same frame and the second render shows it at once (no stale tile), two renders with no change are identical, and 4 bands against 1 band differ in 0 pixels with every option on (the check #98 of Phase H, repeated). Revised: a change is detected by the identity of the published instance (`Advanced.publish` replaces `cur` only when the value differs), not by comparing a version read separately, which could have paired an old value with a new number; the caches are keyed by a per-view counter `Shared.advVer` (there is no `Shared.bakedStyle`). `Advanced.version` still exists. Evidence: `prepareShared` takes the stale path at once and clears the funnel cache; `AdvancedTest` check 12 (each picture option changes the next frame and leaves 0 stale pixels when switched back; two unchanged frames identical) and check 13 (every option on, 4 bands against 1: 0 pixels differ, rain and snow). `--adv` works for `--snapshot`, `--bench` and `--ui-snapshot`.
4. **[VERIFIED] J4 — The Advanced tab.** In `SettingsScreen`: `TABS = {"Background", "Graphics", "Advanced", "Game", "Controls"}`, constants `BACKGROUND = 0, GRAPHICS = 1, ADVANCED = 2, GAME = 3, CONTROLS = 4`, a `case ADVANCED -> paintAdvanced(ui, cx, cy, cw)` in the switch (~55), and `paintAdvanced` modelled on `paintGraphics`: "Max particles" (`ui.seg` of All / 75 % / 50 % / 25 %), "Render resolution" (`ui.seg` of Graphics page / 0.75× / 0.5×, with a one-line note that the Graphics value still applies as an upper limit), then a heading and eleven switches (`ui.toggle`) in two columns of `cw / 2` each, label to the right of the 46 px switch fitted with `ui.textFit`, and one hint line at the bottom that shows the text of the switch under the pointer (`ui.hover`) or "Changes are saved as you make them" otherwise. Left column: Lightning detail off, Global lightning, Branching off, No flash, Simple clouds, No tint. Right column: Don't render what you can't see, No snow, No trails, No shining particles, No puddle impact. Every click edits `s.adv` and calls `changed()` (= `app.settingsChanged()`). The Tab / Down / Up keys already cycle `TABS.length`. Paper check of the fit: the dialog is `min(900, w - 24)` by `min(600, h - 24)`, so at 1024x581 it is 900x557, the content area is `ch = dh - 62 - 66 = 429` and `cw = 672`; two value rows of 60 plus a heading of 28 plus six switch rows of 38 plus the hint line of 30 is 386, under 429; the five tabs end at `62 + 5 x 44 = 282`. Why: 13 controls do not fit in a single column at 557 px, and the brief fixed two columns under the two value controls. Done when: the new tab is selected by click and by Tab, `AdvancedTest` finds every control rectangle inside the content area at 1680x760, 1280x720 and 1024x581, no label wider than its column (`Ui.width` against `cw / 2 - 58`; if "Don't render what you can't see" does not fit, it becomes "Skip what you can't see" with the full text in the hint), and each click flips the matching field and `Advanced.cur`. Evidence: `AdvancedTest` checks 8-11: Tab reaches Advanced from Graphics and goes on to Game, a click on the third tab row selects it; all 13 control rectangles inside the page and none overlapping at 1680x760, 1280x720 and 1024x581, widest label 181 px ("Don't render what you can't see" fits unshortened); each switch and both value rows flip their own field, `Advanced.cur` and the temp settings file; the hint of the switch under the pointer is shown on the bottom line (longest 485 px of 522 available). The switch is labelled "Lightning branching off".
5. **[REVISED] J5 — #1 Max particles.** In `Renderer.drawParticles` add one private static keep test and call it at the head of each of the four loops, straight after the existing view-rectangle test and before the glint bookkeeping, so that every band and every pass agrees: `keep = pct >= 100 || ((rainHash((int) Math.floor((x0 - slope * y0) / RAIN_CELL), Float.floatToIntBits(size)) >>> 12) % 100) < pct`, with `x0, y0` the particle's position without the extrapolation `ex`, and `slope = vx / vy` (0 when `|vy| <= 1`). Debris (kind 7, 8) is not drawn here and is never thinned. Why: the wire gives no particle id and `rot` changes every few ticks, but the fall line `x - slope * y` and the size stay the same while a drop falls — the glint already relies on the same hash ("nothing that depends on the list order"); the high bits are used so the choice does not line up with the glint (`h % 6`) or the length jitter (`(h >>> 5) & 3`). Snow drifts, so its fall line changes and a thinned flake can flip in or out between two ticks; the check below puts a number on it and the fallback is a coarser cell (24 px) for kinds 2-5. Done when: `AdvancedTest` counts the drawn particles of one frame at 100 / 75 / 50 / 25 % (within 5 % of the share), renders the same frame with 4 bands and 1 band (0 pixels differ), renders two consecutive 1/30 s frames of the built-in sim and counts the particles whose keep decision changed (target: under 3 % at 50 %), and 100 % is pixel-identical to the baseline of J1. Revised: the fall-line hash flickered. Measured at 50 % on the built-in sim: 9.8 % of the raindrops and 33 % of the other particles changed sides from one frame to the next (the wind bends the fall line). Instead every particle carries a rank 0..99 from frame to frame (`Model.Frame.particleRank`, `ViewerState.rankParticles`: a particle takes the rank of the particle of its kind and size that was heading for its place; the stream rounds `t` to 0.01 s, a third of a frame, so the frame counter gives the time step). Only computed while the option is below 100 %; a frame that was never ranked (a still picture, the frame on screen when the option is switched) is thinned by a hash of its position. Evidence, `AdvancedTest` check 15: shares 74.1 / 49.6 / 25.2 % drawn for 75 / 50 / 25; 0.00 % of 3 852 raindrops and 0.00 % of 22 509 other particles change sides at 50 %; none while a paused stream repeats a frame; 100 % is the unchanged picture; 4 bands against 1: 0 pixels. Not measured with the Python sim's particles.
6. **[VERIFIED] J6 — #2 Render (the resolution cap).** In `AppShell.applySettings` set `ViewerPanel.maxRenderScale` to the Graphics value (1.0, 1.5 or 0 = native) lowered by the Advanced one when that is set: `low = {0, 0.75, 0.5}[adv.renderScale]`, effective = `low == 0 ? graphics : (graphics == 0 ? low : Math.min(graphics, low))`. `ViewerPanel.paintComponent` needs no change: with `native0 = 1.0` and a cap of 0.5 it already gives `sx = 0.5` (`native0 > cap` selects the cap), renders into a half-size `buf` and stretches it bilinearly in the blit; the panel and every `Scene` screen (`HurricaneScreen`, `WorldScreen`, `DebugScreen`, which get `sx` from `paintScene`) follow it. The `Overlay` (menus, dialogs, `Ui`) is drawn after the blit at full resolution and stays sharp, but text the `Renderer` itself draws into the buffer (side panels, labels, the FPS readout) gets soft at 0.5×. Why: the cap is the existing mechanism, and `--render-scale 0.5` is already accepted by `Main` (~122), so it can be tried before any UI exists. A new key (`graphics.advanced.renderScale`) rather than new entries in `Settings.SCALE_NAMES` because `renderScale` is stored as an index and renumbering it would change what an existing file means (Open question 35). Done when: pictures at `--render-scale 1`, 0.75 and 0.5 (`--snapshot`, 1280x720) are looked at for blank tiles, seams between bands and wrong sprite sizes; flipping the option in a running window shows no blank frame (one frame of old picture at worst); `--bench` at 0.5 is faster than at 1.0 by a visible margin; the Graphics page and the Advanced page agree on the effective cap (a test sets all nine combinations). Evidence: `Advanced.capScale`; all nine combinations agree (`AdvancedTest` check 6); switching to 0.5x in the off-screen panel shows the same picture stretched on the next paint (147 906 pixels softer, 0 black: check 10); `--bench` 17.2 ms at 1.0, 11.4 ms at 0.75, 6.9 ms at 0.5; pictures `b_render_050_on.png` and `b_render_075_on.png` looked at: no blank tile, no seam. Defect to know about: the HUD text the renderer draws into the buffer is not merely soft at 0.75x, the small text is hard to read (glyphs hinted at a fractional scale, then stretched); the dialogs drawn by the overlay stay sharp. Not tried in a real window, nor on the Hurricane and World-map screens.
7. **[REVISED] J7 — #3 Lightning detail off.** In `Lightning.Light`'s constructor pass `null` instead of `this::cover` to `Tree.grow` when the option (or Global, or No flash) is on, so no hidden channel is grown and `Band.glow` has nothing to trace; `cloudAtt` then fades the point lights out at the cloud top, as before Phase H2. In `Light.build` skip the two `addSolid` loops and `addTerrain`, and mark each `Emitter` `open`; in `Band.trace` take `vv = 1` for an open emitter instead of the one-or-four-tap lookup. The three point lights, their falloff (`REACH` 950) and the stroke stay. `Lightning.prepare` takes the options as one extra parameter; the old signature stays as an overload that passes `Advanced.DEFAULT`, because `UiDriveTest` calls it directly (~1000, 1042, 1047) and `Tree.grow` with seven arguments (~845, 1121). The lights, grids and trees already built are dropped when the version changes: the version is folded into the `worldVer` argument (`State.worldVer`, ~627, already clears `lights` and `safe`) and added to `Band.Grid`'s equality test (~815), which would otherwise keep a grid traced for the shadowed light because the view did not move. Why: "plain bolts" must not be the same thing as No flash, so this reading keeps the cheap part of the light and drops the two expensive ones, the shadow maps and the cloud glow (Open question 35). Done when: with the option on, a house between the bolt and a point still lights up behind it (the shadow of check #90 is gone), the pixels inside the host cloud above the base do not gain what #105 measured, `Light.em` holds no solids, and flipping the option while a bolt is showing changes the picture on the next frame. Revised: the channel is still grown into the cloud and only its light is dropped (`Tree.dropGlow`), because with a null mask the piece of channel that shows in clear air under an uneven cloud base went missing and the bolt looked different. Evidence, `AdvancedTest` checks 21-22: shadow-map entries 2 584 -> 0 and every emitter `open`; 247 821 pixels that the shadows kept dark are lit; light segments in the cloud 30 -> 0, a pixel on the channel gains 149 instead of 407 of 765 (what is left is the point lights, which now fade out at the cloud top); switched off again the picture is the shadowed one pixel for pixel. Cost: no gain with a still camera (the grid is cached per view: 2.22 ms against 2.11), 1.03-1.09 ms against 1.96-2.00 ms while panning. Without terrain in the way the light also shows on the soil below the street (picture `c_lightning_detail_off_on.png`).
8. **[VERIFIED] J8 — #4 Global lightning.** In `Lightning.prepare`, when the option is on (and the safe branch has not returned): keep every present bolt whose alpha is above 0.004 whatever its distance (no `l.sees`), call neither `build` nor `Tree.grow` with a mask, and set `Frame.global = true` with `Frame.lights` holding those lights. In `Band.compute` a global frame skips the per-light `Grid` and `trace` altogether: texel 64, `nx` / `rows` from the view as now, every node of `k[]` set to `min(4080, level)` with `level = 4080 x GLOBAL_LEVEL x min(1, sum of alpha²)`; `apply` (and `paint` for the Java2D fallback) are unchanged and spread that flat grid, so the view is brightened by `1 + gain x k` exactly like the lit parts of a normal frame, `gain` coming from the scene light as now. `GLOBAL_LEVEL` starts at 0.5 and is chosen by looking at day and night pictures. Why: this is the cheap flash the game had before Phase H (the whole viewport whitened for a bolt anywhere in the world), rebuilt on the grid pipeline that exists so that the bands, the fallback and the epilepsy rules need nothing new; `Direct.brighten` no longer exists. Done when: a bolt 1 350 px off screen now changes the view (the opposite of check #95, which passes with the option off), all pixels brighten by about the same amount, no building casts a shadow, 4 bands against 1 differ in 0 pixels, the frame costs less than a local-light frame in `--bench --profile` (the Lightning slot), and with the option off the picture equals the baseline of J1. Evidence, `AdvancedTest` check 23: a bolt 1 350 px beyond the edge changes 1 276 800 of 1 276 800 pixels (0 with the option off), each of 20 546 colours by one amount, no shadow map (`Light.em == null`), 4 bands against 1: 0 pixels, the Java2D fallback lights the view too; with Lightning detail off as well it is the same picture; with No flash nothing is lit. `GLOBAL_LEVEL` stayed 0.5 after looking at `d_lightning_global_on.png` (night: a strong flash) and `d_lightning_global_day_on.png` (day: mild). One "done when" is only half met: the lightning slot is not cheaper with a still camera (2.10 ms against 2.11 ms, the cost is applying the light to every pixel), it is while panning (1.39-1.41 ms against 1.96-2.00 ms).
9. **[VERIFIED] J9 — #5 Branching off.** Add a `branching` parameter to `Lightning.Tree.grow` (a new overload; the seven-argument one passes `true`) and, when it is false, empty `lines`, `depth`, `grounded`, `strength`, `box` and `segments` after the branches have been grown and before `intoCloud` runs, so that the random stream the hidden channel draws from is exactly the one it would have drawn from; `Tree.shape` returns early and leaves `coreShape == null`, `joined()` builds `whole` from `main` alone, and `Renderer.drawLightning` already skips the branch fills when `coreShape` is null. The `Light` constructor and `Safe.advance` (~718, ~728) pass the option, so the epilepsy-safe bolts lose their branches as well. Why: growing and discarding costs a few microseconds once per bolt, and keeps the picture of the main channel and the glow identical to the one with branches. Done when: `AdvancedTest` finds `lines.isEmpty()` for 80 bolts with the option on and the same `main` and the same `glow` array as with it off; the picture differs from the normal one only where branches were; epilepsy-safe with the option on draws the channel alone and still peaks at 81 of 255. Evidence, `AdvancedTest` check 25: 80 bolts have no branch left (all 80 had some), the same `main` and the same `glow` as with branches (80 / 80); in the picture only the 4 branches are missing (2 478 pixels on them, 0 elsewhere); an Epilepsy-safe bolt is the channel alone, largest change 77 of 255. Lightning slot 1.70 ms against 2.11 ms.
10. **[VERIFIED] J10 — #9 No flash.** In `Lightning.prepare`, after the bolt loop and the `strokes` / `trees` / `strokeA` arrays are filled, set `out.lights = new Light[0]` when the option is on; skip `l.build(...)` for it (the shadow maps are the expensive part) and pass a null cloud mask as in J7. `Renderer.drawLightning` already calls `lightBand.idle()` for an empty `lights` array and goes on to draw the strokes. The epilepsy-safe branch (~630) returns before this and is untouched. Precedence as in the table above. Why: Epilepsy-safe also dims and slows the bolt; the user asked for a bolt that looks as it always did but lights nothing. Done when: with the option on a bolt gives 0 lit pixels away from the bolt itself (the strokes), the bolt's own pixels equal the baseline's, `Light.em` stays null, a safe bolt is unchanged by the option, and `--bench` shows the lightning slot below the local-light figure of H8. Evidence, `AdvancedTest` check 24: `Frame.lights` empty and `Light.em == null`; 10 096 pixels differ from the picture without a bolt, all on the bolt and its 4 branches, 0 elsewhere; 13 of 15 points of the channel as white as with the light; Epilepsy-safe gives the same picture whatever No flash, Global and Detail off say. Lightning slot 0.82 ms against 2.11 ms (0.13 ms against 1.98 ms while panning).
11. **[VERIFIED] J11 — #6, what is already culled (audit by reading, nothing run).** Today: `Renderer.drawParticles` skips every particle outside the view rectangle widened by `pad` (and, for rain, by the streak `reach`), narrowed to the band's own rows (`bandWy0` / `bandWy1`), in all four loops; the sim itself lists only particles within 200 px of the view once the viewer has sent its `view` command (`PROTOCOL.md` line 138, `ViewerPanel.sendView`). Static objects are skipped outside the view by x (`o.x ± o.w ± 140`) and outside the band by y (`drawWorld` ~694-696); buildings and trees of the baked ground by x (`paintGround` ~315, 325). `SharedClouds.prepare` (~109-119) builds and keeps an image only for a cloud whose box meets the view widened by 20 % of its width plus 60 px (2 000 px downward for a raining one), and drops the image of one that leaves; `Direct.over` clips every blit to the band's rectangle and returns before touching a pixel when nothing overlaps (~42-44). Cars (`prepareShared` ~444) and tornado funnels (~459) are skipped outside the view by x; a bolt that cannot reach the view yields no `Light` in `Frame.lights` (`l.sees`, H1). **Not culled today:** (a) a particle whose sprite lies wholly behind an opaque part of a cloud image is still drawn and then painted over (`underCloudImage` is only a bounding-box test used to split the passes under the shade veil, not an opacity test); (b) every band scans the whole particle array in each of four loops per pass; (c) the far-ridge, near-ridge, scenery and ground tiles are blitted whole every frame, the lower part of the far ridge being overpainted by the ground; (d) `drawClouds` lists every cloud and each band tests it again (cheap, an image-less entry returns at once); (e) the veil of a raining cloud is drawn from an image that can reach 2 000 px below it. Why: the implementer must not redo (a) the work that exists, and must spend effort only where something is left. Done when: this list is in the session and no step of J12 repeats an item of the first paragraph.
12. **[REVISED] J12 — #6 Don't render what you can't see (the part that is missing).** Switch `cullHidden`, default true; off = exactly today's code, which makes the on/off picture comparison possible. Measure before building: `--bench 400 --profile` (1 band, fake seed 1234, 1680x760, scale 1, a heavy rain scene and a thick `cumulonimbus` in view) and read which slots are worth attacking. Candidates, each built only if the profile shows a gain beyond the run-to-run spread (about 0.1 ms in Phases H and H2): (a) in `prepareShared` (renderer 0) build once per frame a compact list of the particles inside the view (indices, one pass), and let the loops of `drawParticles` walk that list instead of the whole array; (b) a per-cloud opaque map, built lazily on a `CloudPainter.Image` as a coarse grid (cells of 8 px, a cell is set only when every pixel of it has alpha 255), used only when `e.drawPrev == null` and `e.fade256 == 256` (no cross-fade running) and `cur.scale == v.ws`, to skip a particle drawn before the clouds (passes 0 and 1, not the in-cloud droplets and crystals drawn over them) whose whole sprite footprint (rain: the streak length at the pass's `lenQ` plus the width and a 2 px margin; snow and the rest: the sprite box) lies inside set cells; (c) only if item (c) of the audit in J11 shows a real cost, skip the part of a tile that the opaque ground covers. The footprint test is conservative: a doubtful particle is drawn. Why: the user asked for "dont render what you cant see" and the output must not change; thicker cloud interiors are opaque only at High quality and in the cores of cumulonimbus, so (b) may remove few particles and the plan must allow for a result of "no measurable gain". Done when: with the option on and off the same frames are pixel-identical (`AdvancedTest` over rain, snow, night village with a bolt, supercell with tornado, at 1 and 4 bands, and with a cloud mid-cross-fade: 0 pixels differ), the number of particles skipped and the bench figures are in the session, and a candidate with no measurable gain is marked `[SKIPPED]` here with the figures instead of being left in. Revised: measured first, as planned. Baseline `--bench 400 --profile`: 15.8-17.7 ms a frame, the particles about 2.2 ms for about 970 listed, so (a) a visible-particle list cannot gain more than the spread and was not built; (c) was not built either: the ridge and scenery tiles already end at `groundY + 6`. Built: (b), per sprite instead of per particle: each blit of a streak, drop, head core, glint or snow / graupel / sleet / splash sprite drawn before the clouds is tested with its exact rectangle against the opaque 4 px cells of the cloud images that will be copied over it (`CloudPainter.Image.opaqueSat`, made on the worker; `Renderer.hiddenByCloud`), and a cross-fading cloud counts where both of its images are opaque. Evidence, `AdvancedTest` check 14: on and off identical in 8 renders (rain, snow, dusk, supercell with tornado; 1 and 4 bands: 0 pixels); 400 particles planted behind opaque cloud: 1 450 blits left out, 0 when off, still 0 pixels differ. In use: 0 blits skipped at the default zoom in every scene tried (fake default, High quality, cold with 6 extra clouds, supercell; Python sim seeds 1234 and 7): precipitation is spawned at the cloud base, so little of it lies behind a cloud; about 41 blits a frame under a storm at zoom 1.3 (20 863 in 500 frames), where the frame time is the same within the spread (16.69 / 17.01 ms on, 16.81 / 16.78 ms off). No measurable gain. A cloud in mid cross-fade was not tested with a picture (the fade runs on worker threads and cannot be repeated exactly); the rule rests on `Direct.overFade` keeping a pixel opaque in both images opaque. Whether to keep the switch at all was asked (session Q1); nobody answered in two nine-minute waits, so the default was applied and recorded `ASSUMED`: the switch and the test stay, and its hint says that it seldom finds much.
13. **[REVISED] J13 — #7 Simple clouds.** Add `static volatile boolean simple` to `CloudPainter` (published from `Shared.adv` by `AppShell.applySettings`, like `quality`) and in `render` branch on it before the quality settings (~323): a grid of `cellPx` about 5 and `cap` about 6 000 cells, `oct` 2, `steps` 1 (the light march of ~736 reduced to one tap), and a flat colour per cell from the light's ambient and direct colours with a soft alpha edge, no rim, no silver lining, no anvil fibres or updraft striations; merged clouds and supercells keep their silhouette (the slab, wall cloud and anvil are shapes, not shading) but not their internal shading. `Spec.signature` (~178) adds `+ (simple ? 7 : 0)` only when true so that every other hash stays what it was. Why: `render` already has three tiers driven by `q`; a fourth tier below them is the least code, and the signature makes `SharedClouds` rebuild every cloud on its own, cross-faded, without a hand-written invalidation (the Graphics page already says "clouds repaint within a few seconds"). Done when: every cloud type renders non-empty in simple mode at forming and dissipating (the life fractions of Phase I), a simple cloud costs less than the same cloud at Low in the cloud slot of `--bench --profile`, the silhouette (alpha above 8) of a simple cloud overlaps that of the Low render by at least 90 %, a picture of each of the nine types is looked at, and with the option off the images are identical to the baseline (`CloudPainter.maxChannelDiff == 0`). Revised: there is no `CloudPainter.simple` static: `Spec.simple` is taken from `Advanced.cur` when the spec is made, so the signature and the render always agree. The recipe (Open question 38): grid 5 px / 6 000 cells, 2 octaves (cirrus keeps 3: it is nothing but its streaks), one even colour that only darkens toward the base of a dark cloud, no light march, no relief, no striations, no fine detail, no rim; the soft fringe is kept as an even veil because without it the outline shrank to 49-87 % of the Low one. The full-resolution pass got a method of its own (`simpleFill`): `render` is too long for the JIT to compile well, and with the loop inside it a simple cloud still cost 61 ms against 85 ms. Evidence, `AdvancedTest` check 26: all nine types at three ages (27 pictures), at least 97 % of the Low outline, flatter inside in 27 of 27, 8.3-8.9 ms against 82-88 ms at Low; bench: 7.6 ms a cloud render against 56-58 ms on the worker threads (the frame itself is unchanged, 17.3 ms). The Debug cloud cache keys carry the option. Pictures looked at for the sandbox scene (`g_simple_clouds_on.png`), not one by one for the nine types.
14. **[VERIFIED] J14 — #8 No tint.** Give `Lit` a final `boolean flat` (constructor `Lit(tod, over, flat)`; the two-argument one, used by `DebugLineup` and `DebugTest`, keeps today's behaviour) and in `Lit.lit(Color)` return `mul(c, light)` alone when it is set, dropping the night-blue mix (`night * 0.25`) and the dusk-orange mix (`dusk * 0.12 * day`); add it to `differs`. `Renderer.prepareShared` builds `s.lit` and `s.cloudLight` with the flag (`CloudPainter.Light.of(tod, over, flat)`: the warm-sun weight `w` is 0 and the dusk purple ambient terms are skipped, so the colour is the day colour at the night's intensity). `Renderer.drawBackdrop` (~831-840) uses `backdropTint && !flat` for the picture's dusk-orange veil and its brightness ramp; `Lightning.Band.paint` uses 255, 255, 255 instead of `TINT_*` and `Direct.lightRow` takes `a8 = a` (a new overload with a `neutral` argument; the existing one stays for the tests). The brightness, the sky gradient, the stars and the sun and moon are not touched. `AppShell.styleBackdrop` (~335) sets `r.backdropTint = settings.bgTint && !settings.adv.noTint`; the Background tab's own toggle keeps its stored value and is not edited (Open question 35). Why: `Lit.lit` is the one place every painter takes its colours from (buildings, terrain, cars, ponds), so one flag there covers them; the funnel cache and the scenery tiles hold finished pixels and are re-made through the version (J3). Done when: at dusk the colour of a lit wall equals its day colour times `light` to within 2 levels, a night village has no blue cast (the blue-minus-red difference of a grey wall is no higher than at noon), the backdrop picture's pixels are the same at dusk with the option on and with the picture's tint off, a bolt's light is neutral (equal red, green and blue gain), and with the option off the pictures equal the baseline. Evidence, `AdvancedTest` check 20: a wall at dusk is `595042`, its own colour times the daylight exactly (tinted `4a4442`); a grey wall at night has blue minus red 0 (tinted 10); the cloud light keeps the noon hue (blue / red 0.90 at dusk and at noon, 0.79 tinted); lightning adds a neutral white (`656565` against the cold `646465`); a background picture is shown as with its own tint off (1 680 of 1 680 sky pixels equal) and the stored Background switch stays on. Cost: none (18.75 / 19.07 ms against 18.57 / 18.41 ms at dusk). The sky gradient, and with it the haze of the far hills, keeps the colours of the hour (pictures `h_no_tint_dusk_*.png`, `h_no_tint_night_*.png`).
15. **[REVISED] J15 — #10 No snow.** One condition per place that draws snow, each reading `sh.adv.noSnow` (or `Scene.noSnow` for the painters that run on worker threads and have a `Scene`): `drawParticles` third loop skips `kind == 2` before the sprite lookup; `drawWorld` skips `case "snowpack"`; `drawSnowpack` (reached by other callers) returns first; `Scene` gets a final `noSnow` and `snowAt` returns 0 for it, which removes the ground blanket and the road tint (`TerrainPainter` ~194, ~379) and the altitude term; `BuildingPainter.Ctx` (~52) reads `snow = 0`; `TerrainPainter` ~124 and ~159 (the cap of a far mountain, which is drawn above `freezingY` "whatever the sim says") gets `o.snow` = 0 and no altitude cap; `Renderer.coverLevels` (~503-514) takes `sn = 0` and the object level without `snow` so the painted levels do not keep triggering partial repaints. The pond that freezes because `o.snow > 0.5` (`drawPond` ~1146) is left as it is: the water is frozen by the sim's cover, not painted snow. The "snow & ice" word on the freezing-line label (~1028) is HUD text and stays. Why: snow reaches the screen through four independent paths (particles, objects, the ground cover in the baked tiles, snow on buildings and mountains), and a switch on only one of them leaves white where the player turned it off. Done when: in a snow scene (`--tod`, built-in sim, snow world) the count of snow-coloured pixels (`Renderer.precipColor` white and the cover colours) on the ground, roofs and flakes is 0 with the option on, rain, sleet and graupel are unchanged, a re-bake follows a flip at once, and with the option off the picture equals the baseline. Revised: instead of one condition in each painter the painters are handed the objects without their snow (`Renderer.snowless`, a copy made once per object list; a pond keeps its value, so it stays frozen), the cars likewise; `Scene.noSnow` covers the ground blanket, the road tint and the altitude caps (`TerrainPainter`), `coverLevels` the painted levels. Added to the plan: a cloud that snows hangs no shaft of snow under it either (`SharedClouds.spec`), which the first picture showed left over. Evidence, `AdvancedTest` check 19: a world with 707 flakes, snow on 80 objects and 121 ground samples has 1 287 near-white pixels below the clouds, 6 with the option on (lit windows and lamps); the rain world with its 127 flakes left out is pixel for pixel the picture of its other particles alone. Bench in snow: 16.2 ms against 17.7 / 18.0 ms. Picture `i_no_snow_on.png`.

Cost: `--bench 400 --profile` (1 band, fake seed 1234, 1680x760, scale 1) of the baseline against the final build with no option on (must not be slower beyond the run-to-run spread, because `cullHidden` is on by default and the keep test and the version check run every frame), then once per option on its own, in the scene that shows it best (rain for 1, 11, 12 and 13; snow for 10; a village with a bolt for 3, 4, 5 and 9; a cloudy sky for 7 and 8; `--bench-scale 0.5` for 2), alternating the runs as H2.6 did; the table of figures goes into this step. Why: Phases H8 and H2.6 quoted numbers taken while a game was running; say so again if that is the case. Done when: all 6 or more frames are pixel-identical, the per-option table is in the session, an option that is slower than doing nothing is named, and `[ACTIVE]` is kept with the reason for any figure taken on a loaded machine. Evidence. Identity: 13 scenes (rain with the whole HUD, life card and help, snow night village, bolt, Epilepsy-safe bolt, supercell with tornado, dusk, polar biome, puddle close-up, background picture at dusk, High quality, Cloud Formations mid-life, Hurricane) at 1680x760 scale 1, 1024x581 scale 1 and 1680x760 scale 1.5: 39 of 39 PNGs pixel-identical between `viewer/out_j_base` and the final build (PIL, `getbbox() is None`). The whole set was rendered again after the last edits: 38 of 39 identical; the odd one (the background picture at dusk, 1680x760) differed in 320 pixels of the freezing-level label's text, and the difference was in that one baseline render: three more renders of each build gave one and the same file. So the text of a snapshot is not perfectly repeatable from run to run, in the old build as in the new. Cost, `--bench 400 --profile`, 1 band, fake seed 1234, 1680x760, no game running (VS Code open): baseline 16.83 / 17.66 / 17.08 and 16.67 / 16.93 / 16.86 ms, final with defaults 17.05 / 17.08 / 17.20 and 17.01 / 16.94 / 16.90 ms: the same within the spread. Per option against the same scene without it: particles 50 % 16.72, 25 % 16.73 (17.1; few raindrops in this scene); render 0.75x 11.39, 0.5x 6.89; no trails 16.37; no shining 17.48 (the particle slot 0.09 against 0.18 ms, the total is noise); no puddle impact 17.00; simple clouds 17.31 with 7.6 ms a cloud render against 56-58 ms on the workers; no snow 16.21 (17.69 / 18.04 in the snow scene); with a bolt at night (18.11 / 18.35): detail off 17.93, global 17.85, no branches 17.08, no flash 16.12; no tint 18.75 / 19.07 (18.57 / 18.41 at dusk); every option on 15.82. Nothing is reliably slower than doing nothing; a first No tint run at 20.21 ms did not repeat.

## Phase K — Rain dot, droplets on windows and cars, puddles settle (viewer and sim)

**Project context:** `[UPDATE]` — extends the build with Phases H–J. This phase is a **record of finished work**, written afterwards from two completed SA-Asker sessions and the orchestrator's own checks; it is not a design, and nothing here is `[PLANNED]` except the follow-ups K14 and K15. Two agents worked **in parallel on disjoint files**: the viewer agent owned `viewer/src/precip/Renderer.java` (rain block and one hook in `drawWorld`), `BuildingPainter.java` and `CarPainter.java`; the sim agent owned `sim/objects.py`, `sim/simulation.py` (two call sites) and `sim/tests/`. `PROTOCOL.md`, the wire format, `saves/`, `config/` and `Settings` were not touched (a diff against the pre-change copy kept by the orchestrator shows exactly three viewer files and four sim files changed; `PROTOCOL.md` identical). The user's request (2026-10-10, verbatim, two messages): "Make Rain drops Smaller Just a Dot And Keep The Trail It emits add in Water Droplets to windows and cars." and "also Add a update For Water Puddles where instead of just floating if water puddle spawns on a edge it tries to Force it down and also checks a slope and then finds the lowest point nothing happens if its flat". The reading of both (the orchestrator's, not confirmed by the user): the raindrop head becomes a small round dot at every zoom while the streak behind it and the shining glint stay; wet windows and cars show water beads and a few running drops; a puddle that is placed on an edge is forced down onto the ground, one on a slope runs to the lowest point, one on level ground does not move at all, and a puddle never grows out over lower ground or up a bank. Sessions: `rain-dot-droplets-on-windows-and-cars-20261010-173720` (viewer, `general-purpose`, outcome DONE) and `puddles-settle-to-the-lowest-point-20261010-173720` (sim, `python-tooling-dev`, outcome PARTIAL: no real-viewer picture of the puddle, and three wall-clock performance tests failed under load; the orchestrator has since taken two real-sim close-ups, K12). This record: `plan-md-phase-k-record-20261010-192829`. Every claim below was checked against the code unless it says "as reported", which means it comes from a session log and was not re-measured here.

**What each part does**
| Part | Role |
|---|---|
| `Renderer.java`, rain block of `drawParticles` (~1836-1914), `rainBegin` (~2008), `blitDot` (~2084), `makeDotMask` (~2152) | The raindrop head is one round white dot mask keyed by its size in device pixels (`DOT_KEY`), on a thin blue rim that fades in between zoom 1.5 and 2.8; the streak keeps its length, slant and fade and no longer thins out for a big drop |
| `Renderer.drawWorld` (~750-759) | A ten-line hook that calls the live running-drop pass for front-row buildings, before the trees |
| `BuildingPainter.java` | `Ctx.beads` (still beads, baked with each pane into the scenery tile, keyed to the six wetness levels the tiles already use), `panes`, `hasDrops`, `paintDrops` (running drops, live) |
| `CarPainter.java` | `droplets` and `beads`: beads on every pane, the roof, the bonnet and the upper flanks, plus one running drop per pane |
| `sim/objects.py` | `settle_puddle`, `puddle_rests`, `_footprint_low` (placement); `_reach`, `puddle_cap` (growth); the puddle loop of `generate_world`; `ObjectField(objs, terrain)` with its derived `puddle_cap` dict and the growth line that uses it |
| `sim/simulation.py` | `ObjectField(objs, self.terrain)` at ~125 and `ObjectField.from_state(..., sim.terrain)` at ~1054; nothing else |
| `sim/tests/test_objects.py`, `test_presets.py` | `TestPuddlesSettle` (11 tests, new); one existing spot test now accepts a forced-down puddle `y`; two full-world hashes re-recorded |
| `viewer/snapshots/v2/rain-dot/` | The pictures of the viewer part (29 files on disk) |

**The numbers chosen**

Rain dot (`Renderer.java` ~1984-1990, logical px unless said otherwise):
| Constant | Value | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `RAIN_DOT_PAD`, `RAIN_DOT_MIN`, `RAIN_DOT_MIN_ALONE` | 0.15, 0.7, 1.0 | Dot radius = `max(min, 0.5 x streak head width + pad)`; the floor is 1.0 with "No trails" so a lone dot cannot vanish |
| `RAIN_RIM_Z0`, `RAIN_RIM_Z1`, `RAIN_RIM_PX` | 1.5, 2.8, 0.45 | The blue rim fades in over this zoom range and reaches this far past the dot; with "No trails" it is on at every zoom |
| `RAIN_RIM_RGB`, `RAIN_RIM_ALPHA` | `0x6C98E0`, 150 of 256 | Rim colour and strength |
| streak alpha | `(205 + 45 x boost) / 255`, no zoom term | Before, it was cut by 25 % as the old drop appeared |

By the formula in the code the dot is about 1.4 px across at zoom 1 and roughly 2.6-3.6 px at zoom 4 depending on drop size (about 0.9 px more with the rim); the session reports "4-5 px at zoom 4 where a 12x22 px teardrop was" and "2 px at zoom 1" from a crop, which fits the largest drops with their rim.

Droplets (`BuildingPainter.java` ~43-63, `CarPainter.java` ~307-317; world px unless said otherwise):
| Constant | Buildings | Cars |
|---|---|---|
| Zoom gate (device px per world px) | beads `BEAD_MIN_PX` 1.6, running drops `RUN_MIN_PX` 1.6 | `DROP_MIN_PX` 2.0 |
| Bead radius | `BEAD_R0` 0.26 - `BEAD_R1` 0.60 | 0.20 - 0.42 |
| Density (beads per square world px on a soaked surface) | 0.10, 2-16 per pane | glass 0.30 (at most 7 per pane), body 0.22 (at most 18 per panel) |
| Shape ladder (device px across) | one soft square under 3.0 (every other one), a lens with a dark underside from 4.0, true ellipses from 5.5 | square under 3, ellipses from 5.5 |
| Running drops | on 40 % of the panes at most (48 panes per building), wetness above 0.25, cycle 5-12 s, on the glass 55 % of it | one per pane, cycle 3.5-8 s, on the glass 60 % of it, only above wetness about 0.3 |
| Wetness steps | beads follow the six levels the tiles are keyed by (`round(wet x 6) / 6`), times `1 - 4 x snow` | continuous, times `1 - 4 x snow`; wipers leave 45 % of the windscreen beads (`WIPED`) |

Puddles (`sim/objects.py` ~83-195; px):
| Constant | Value | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `PUDDLE_TOL` | 1.0 | How far the ground may fall under a footprint before the puddle counts as floating (the viewer draws the lens centred 1 px below `y`) |
| `PUDDLE_BANK` | 3.0 | How far the ground may climb above the water at either end of a grown puddle |
| `PUDDLE_FORCE_MAX` | 3.0 | A blocked puddle is forced down onto the ground when that is no more than this; deeper, it is moved to the nearest allowed level spot |
| `PUDDLE_MAX_W` | 140.0 | The widest rain makes a puddle (as before); `puddle_cap` is at least the 20 px a puddle starts as |
| `_SETTLE_ITERS` | 2000 | Safety bound of the downhill walk |

1. **[VERIFIED] K1 — Baselines before any edit.** Viewer: an untouched scratch build with seven "before" rain pictures (fake seed 77, 4 spawned rain clouds, 1500 frames) and bench figures; sim: a float-gap measurement (script kept in the orchestrator's scratchpad, not in `sim/`) over 1239 puddles from plain worlds (several seeds) and the presets, at base size and at the size a long rain grows them to. Why: "nothing floats" and "rain is no slower" can only be claimed against numbers taken first. Done when: both baselines are in the sessions. Evidence (as reported): rain-streaks slot 2.23-2.62 ms (1 band, 720 particles), frame 10.0-10.3 ms average with 4 bands; the finding that buildings are baked into the ground `TileLayer` (re-baked when `round(wet x 6)`, snow or damage level changes) and only windmill, factory and skyscraper have a live pass, while cars are painted live every frame. Puddles at tolerance 0.5 px: at base size 166 float (26 of them by more than 2 px, worst 7.13); at grown size 637 float (457 by more than 2 px, worst 194 px, mean width 101). So the floating is in the sim, not only in how the viewer draws it.
2. **[VERIFIED] K2 — The raindrop head is a small dot (`Renderer.java`).** In the rain loop of `drawParticles` the zoom-grown teardrop sprite (`blitDrop`, `makeDrop`, `DROP_KEY`, `rnDrop`, `dropR`, `RAIN_DROP_Z0/Z1`) and the head core (`blitCore`, `CORE_KEY`) are removed and replaced by one round mask, `blitDot(g, rQ, rgb, alpha, cx, cy)`, drawn twice (a rim, then the white dot), keyed by size in quarter device px so one zoom shares sprites with the next. The streak (`blitStreak`, `RAIN_EXPOSURE`, `RAIN_LEN_*`, `RAIN_TIER_ALPHA`, the boost over the shade veil) is untouched; `rainBegin` no longer cuts its alpha by 25 % as the drop appears. Why: the user wants a dot and a trail; the old head grew with `dev x zoom` into a visible teardrop and made the streak thin out for it. Done when: the head is a dot at far, normal and close zoom, the rain-lag fix still holds (no atlas clears, no rebuild storm while zooming), and the sleet, snow, hail, splash and in-cloud passes are unchanged. Evidence: `grep` finds no `blitDrop`, `DROP_KEY` or `dropR` left in `viewer/src/precip`; the diff of `Renderer.java` against the pre-change copy touches only the rain block, `rainBegin`, the sprite makers and the drawWorld hook. As reported: 0 atlas clears and 0.5-0.6 sprite builds per frame in a zoom sweep before and after; rain-streaks slot unchanged within the run-to-run spread (3.73 / 4.03 / 4.14 ms before, 3.95 / 3.96 / 3.65 after under the veil; 0.83-1.19 and 0.83-1.21 without it); whole frame 4 bands 10.5-11.9 ms average before, 11.1-12.0 after.
3. **[VERIFIED] K3 — Glint, "No trails", "No shining particles" kept working.** The glint selection (`h % RAIN_GLINT_EVERY`), the "no matter what" fallback, `glintLevel` and `blitGlint` are as before. One thing did change: the glint is now anchored at the **centre of the dot** (`addGlint(cx, cy, ...)`) where it used to sit `headOff` behind the tip (`0.7 x dev`, or `1.1 x dropR` once zoomed in). "No trails" draws the dot alone with the floor `RAIN_DOT_MIN_ALONE` (1.0 px) and the rim at every zoom, which replaces the old "drop at every zoom, `max(zoom, 1.1)`" rule; "No shining particles" still sets `glint = false` and skips the fallback. Why: the user asked earlier that the shining particle always appears in the rain, so it must not go; the dot has no `headOff` to hang it from. Done when: a rain picture shows dots with trails and glints, and with "No trails" a visible dot alone. Evidence: read in the code (lines ~1884-1914); pictures `before_*` / `after_*` and `crop4x_rain_day_close_before_after.png` (looked at by the viewer agent, as reported). Not settled: at zoom 4 the glint star (reported about 14 px) is larger than the dot; the brief said to leave the glint and report it, and it is open question 44. The claim in the session log that the glint code is "untouched" is true of everything except its anchor point.
4. **[VERIFIED] K4 — Still beads on the panes of wet buildings (`BuildingPainter.Ctx.beads`).** Inside `Ctx.window(...)`, straight after a pane is filled, `beads(x, y, w, h, lit2)` scatters the beads of that pane; `Ctx` gets `px` (device px per world px) and `beadWet` (`round(wet x 6) / 6 x clamp(1 - round(snow x 12) / 12 x 4, 0, 1)`, 0 under `BEAD_MIN_PX`). Positions and sizes come from a private number stream of the building's `seed` and the pane's position (`mix`, `step`, `unit`; never the building's own `Random`, which would change its look), so there is no `Math.random` and no flicker between frames or render bands. The beads are **baked into the scenery tile**, which is already re-baked when the wetness level changes, so they cost nothing per frame. Why: measurement showed the buildings are tile-baked; static beads belong in the bake. Done when: dry pictures are identical to before, wet ones show beads, and the bake is not much dearer. Evidence (as reported): dry town and dry car pictures 0 px different from the pre-change build; 1, 4 and 7 render bands give the same wet picture (0 px); off-frame tile bake in a wet town 1.19-1.31 ms before and 1.39-1.41 ms after per tile at zoom 2, 1.32-1.47 and 2.10-2.16 at zoom 4 (+13 % and about +55 %, the cost of the beads themselves). Pictures `drops_town_{dry,wet}_{day,night}.png`, `drops_town_wet_day_zoom2.png`, `crop3x_windows_dry_wetbefore_wetafter.png`, `crop4x_windows_night_dry_wet.png`.
5. **[VERIFIED] K5 — Running drops on the panes of front-row buildings.** `BuildingPainter.paintDrops(g, o, lit, anim, haze, px, x0, y0, x1, y1)` runs over a cached list of pane rectangles (`panes(o)`, recorded once per object by painting the intact building into a 1x1 sink with `Ctx.rec`, cached in `PANES`, capped at 8 192 entries), and for each chosen pane draws a drop that starts at the top, slides down in fits and starts (its `y` never runs back up) with a thin wet trail above it, and is gone for the rest of its cycle; wide panes (a shop front) take up to four lanes. `hasDrops` gates it: zoom, `wet > 0.25`, `damage < 0.04`, `snow < 0.25`. `Renderer.drawWorld` calls it for buildings with `layer != 1` only, band-clipped, before the trees. Motion comes from `anim`; every choice from the seed and the pane number. Why: running drops move, so they cannot be in the tile; a live pass over a cached list keeps the bake untouched. Done when: a wet front-row building shows moving drops, a dry or snowed-in or storm-damaged one shows none, and 1 band and 4 bands agree. Evidence: gating read in `hasDrops` and the hook at `Renderer.java` 750-759; as reported, 4 bands against 1 identical, and at zoom 2 buildings plus cars cost together +0.26 to +0.36 ms (objects 0.38-0.39 -> 0.49-0.52 ms, cars 1.39-1.42 -> 1.54-1.65 ms in one band), at zoom 4 about +0.05 ms each; whole frame 25.4-25.9 -> 26.0-26.2 ms in one band. Back-row buildings get the still beads only (open question 43, item 4).
6. **[VERIFIED] K6 — Beads and running drops on cars (`CarPainter`).** After the roof snow and wet sheen and before the wiper, `paint` calls `droplets(...)` when the car is not `simple`, not `wrecked`, not `tossed`, `wet > 0.04` and `px >= DROP_MIN_PX` (2.0). `droplets` puts beads on every pane (the windscreen last, at 45 % strength when the wipers are on), on the flat of the roof (not under a snow cap, `snow <= 0.05`), a bonnet or cab-roof strip chosen by variant (hatchback, pickup and truck one strip each, van and bus none, every other variant, the sedan, a bonnet strip and a boot strip) and the upper flanks (two panels on a truck, one on the rest); one drop per pane runs top to bottom with a wet trail while `wet` is above about 0.3. Everything comes from the car id (`seed = c.id x 0x9E3779B97F4A7C15 + 5`) and `anim`. Why: cars are painted live every frame, so beads and drops go straight into `paint`, gated by zoom so far-away cars stay as cheap as before. Done when: a wet car shows beads and a running drop on its glass, a dry one is unchanged, a wrecked or airborne one has none. Evidence: gating and constants read in `CarPainter.java` 260-265 and 307-377; as reported, dry car picture 0 px different from before; pictures `drops_car_{dry,wet}_day.png`, `drops_car_wet_night.png`, `crop4x_car_wet_before_after.png`.
7. **[REVISED] K7 — The droplet cost, made cheap enough.** First version: batched paths for the beads and the running drops. It cost too much: running drops 26-78 microseconds a building, cars +40 microseconds, and the tile bake doubled. Measured instead what Java2D charges for one antialiased shape: a rectangle fill 0.2 microseconds, an ellipse 1.2, a path 8-30. Rewrote everything to single rectangle fills while a bead is under 5.5 device px (a soft square under 3 px, a rounded blob of two rectangles up to 5.5, a true ellipse above), raised the zoom gates to 1.6 (buildings) and 2.0 (cars) device px per world px, and cut the density from 0.16 to 0.10. Why this is kept in the plan: the brief's budget was "droplets add no more than about 0.5 ms to the frame", and the cheap shapes are the reason the final numbers in K5 are inside it. Done when: the measured cost is under the budget. Evidence (as reported): +0.26 to +0.36 ms at zoom 2, +0.1 at zoom 4. The old path-based code is gone; do not bring it back without a new measurement.
8. **[VERIFIED] K8 — Puddles settle when they are placed (`sim/objects.py`).** In `generate_world`, `flat_spot("puddle")` is unchanged and still draws `rng` the same number of times; afterwards each picked puddle that already rests on level ground (`puddle_rests`: nothing under its 20 px footprint falls more than `PUDDLE_TOL` below its centre) keeps its **exact** `x` and `y` and keeps its spot against the others; every other puddle goes through `settle_puddle(terrain, x, avoid_sets["puddle"], taken)`: each round it moves to the lowest ground under its footprint (`_footprint_low`), so on a slope it travels to the foot of it and at an edge it drops over it, and it stops where its own centre is the lowest point it covers. It never enters a building footprint or a pond (the same `avoid` spans and `SPOT_MARGIN` as `flat_spot`), never leaves `20 .. w - 20`, and never lands within `half` of an earlier puddle (`taken`, so two puddles sliding into one hollow do not sit on top of each other). A walk that is blocked ends at the last allowed `x`; if the ground there still falls away by no more than `PUDDLE_FORCE_MAX` the puddle is forced down (`y` = the lowest ground under its footprint); deeper, or if it was picked on top of another puddle, it goes to the nearest allowed level spot (a scan, whole world), and a world with none forces it down. `settle_puddle` draws nothing from `rng`. Why: this is the user's three cases in the user's order (edge forced down, slope to the lowest point, flat unchanged) as one pure function of the terrain and the already-chosen `x`, so the seeded stream and every non-puddle object stay as they were. Done when: tests (a) to (e) of K10 pass and the float-gap numbers drop. Evidence: code read (~115-165, 329-373); `TestPuddlesSettle` 11 of 11 pass (run again here, 54 s); as reported, puddles floating by more than 1 px at base size 81 -> 2 of 1239, worst 7.13 px before; 3 935 of 4 368 plain-world puddles and 539 of 567 preset-world puddles exactly where they were.
9. **[VERIFIED] K9 — Growth is capped to what the hollow holds.** `ObjectField(objs, terrain=None)` computes, for each puddle, `puddle_cap(terrain, x, y)`: the width at which the ground falls more than `PUDDLE_TOL` below the water or climbs more than `PUDDLE_BANK` above it on either side (`_reach`, the puddle is centred), inside the world, at most `PUDDLE_MAX_W`, at least 20 px; the growth line (`w = round(min(self.puddle_cap.get(i, PUDDLE_MAX_W), o["w"] + 0.06 x n), 1)`) uses it. The cap is derived from the terrain and the puddle's `x, y`, so **nothing new is saved and nothing new is on the wire**; without a terrain (a bare field in a test) a puddle grows to `PUDDLE_MAX_W` anywhere, as it always did. `simulation.py` passes `self.terrain` at both construction sites. Why: the brief said a puddle must not float at the sizes it grows to, and the measurement showed 637 of 1239 floating at grown size against 166 at base size, so placement alone does not fix it. Done when: no puddle floats beyond tolerance at the size a long rain grows it to, a level floor is not limited, and a save from the old code with a puddle wider than its hollow loads and does not snap back. Evidence: code read (~189-195, 499-505, 566); tests `test_a_puddle_grows_as_wide_as_its_hollow_and_a_level_floor_is_not_limited`, `test_a_puddle_wider_than_its_hollow_from_an_old_save_does_not_snap_back`, `test_the_cap_comes_from_the_terrain_so_a_save_needs_nothing_new`, `test_no_puddle_floats_at_the_size_a_long_rain_grows_it_to`; as reported, floating by more than 1 px at grown size 561 -> 2 of 1239, worst 194 px -> 1.03 px. Saves: objects are stored in full (`ObjectField.to_state`), so an existing save keeps its puddle positions; the old-code save was loaded by the new code and round-tripped frame for frame (as reported). The price: mean grown width falls in hilly and mountainous worlds, about 65 -> 37 and 75 -> 35 px in the sim agent's sample (open question 42).
