### The water model in numbers (the one table both implementers build from)
Water is **area in the 2-D world, px²**. The sim tallies landed rain in 32 px buckets (`rain_hits[k]`, count of particles that landed on the bucket this tick, `particles.ParticleSystem.update`, already merged with melted snow by `Simulation._melt_on_warm_ground` for presets). Every constant lives in `sim/water.py` (one place), is never read by the viewer (the wire carries what it needs) and is a **starting value from planning arithmetic that LS6 re-tunes by measurement**.
| Name | Start | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `HIT_AREA` | 1.0 px² per landed particle **[REVISED] shipped as 1.15** (`sim/water.py`; LS6 chose it from a grid of 1.0 / 1.15 / 1.3 on the 14 storms over towns of 650 px and up: at 1.0 a cumulonimbus floods small drains in only 12 of 14 and a supercell floods normal drains in 0 of 14; at 1.3 a nimbostratus floods small drains in 5 of 14 and a cumulonimbus floods normal drains in 5 of 14, which breaks C3; the second sim agent re-ran the grid and kept 1.15) | Water one landed particle adds. 1 hit/s on a bucket is then 1/32 px/s of depth on flat ground at 1.0 (1.15/32 at the shipped value). The two other rows of this table that the closed form uses (`INLET_K`, `SOAK`, `CURB`) shipped as written |
| `BASIN_MIN_DEPTH` | 1.0 px | A local minimum of `terrain.y` whose lower rim is less than this above its bed is not a basin (the same 1 px as Phase K's `PUDDLE_TOL`); water crosses it. World edges are outflows, not basins |
| `SOAK` | 0.015 px/s x wetted width | Loss per body: `SOAK x width(level)` px²/s (soak and evaporation). Width-proportional so a body dries in finite time (the old puddle dried from `wet` 1.0 in about 100 s, `0.01 /s`) |
| `MIN_VOL` | 0.3 px² | A body below this is removed (its id is retired) |
| `CURB`, `INLET_SPACING`, `FLOOD_DEPTH` | 10, 60, 3 px | As in `hello.drains`; a street body spills at `max(y(x0), y(x1)) - CURB` (the lower end) |
| `INLET_K` | 0.2667 | One inlet removes `INLET_K x size^2` px²/s; `cap = inlets x INLET_K x size^2`. Per px of street that is about `0.0044 x size^2` px/s whatever the town's length, so **size alone decides** |
| Classes | small 3.0, normal 4.7, large 8.2 px | Per-px capacity 0.040 / 0.098 / 0.299 px/s |
| `normal` by kind | hamlet 2.0-4.0, village 3.0-5.5, town 3.5-6.5, city 4.0-8.0 px | Uniform in the range with `u = (crc32(f"{seed}:{id}:drain") & 0xFFFFFF) / 0xFFFFFF`; `random` is uniform in 2.5-10 with `crc32(f"{seed}:{id}:drain-random")`. Never the world's `random.Random` (the seeded world must not shift) |
| Catchment | the settlement span only | Rain on a bucket whose centre is in `x0 .. x1` goes to that street body; rain elsewhere runs downhill to the nearest basin; water that would end in a street from outside is routed past the town to the nearest basin on the side it came from (or leaves at the world edge) |
| Cars | stall depth = 1.8 x wheel radius | Wheel radius from `CarPainter.dims`: sedan 3.2, hatchback 3.1, pickup 3.5, van 3.4, truck 3.9, bus 3.9 px, so a sedan stands at 5.8 px; `v_des` is multiplied by `max(0, 1 - depth / stall)` |

Per sub-step (at most 0.25 s of sim time, so speed x8 cannot overshoot): `V += hits x HIT_AREA - SOAK x width x dt - cap x dt` (the last term for street bodies only), `V >= 0`; if the level has reached the rim, `V` is clamped to the rim volume and the excess goes to the receiving body (over the lower rim; a world edge loses it); two bodies whose levels both reach the rim between them merge (volumes add, the larger keeps its id). The level of a body is found from `V` by bisection to 0.01 px over its own span (`V(L)` is the integral of `max(0, terrain(x) - L)` over the connected span, the terrain being linear between samples). A flat street of span `s` has `depth = V / s`, so **the test vector for both implementers** is `d(t) = d0 + (Q/s - cap/s - SOAK) t` while `0 < d < CURB`: with `s` = 400 px, size 4.7 (7 inlets, `cap` 41.2 px²/s), `Q` = 100 px²/s (0.25 px/s) the depth rises 0.132 px/s (3 px after 22.7 s, spill at 10 px after 75.8 s); with `Q` = 0 it falls 0.118 px/s (5 px clears in 42 s). LS's test and LV's `FakeWater` must reproduce these to 1 %, recomputed from the constants if LS6 changes them.

**As built, the vector is tested at two values of `HIT_AREA`** (the second sim agent's `test_water.py`: `TestTheFlatStreetVector` patches `HIT_AREA` 1.0, `INLET_K`, `SOAK` and `CURB` to the plan's values and asserts the plan's literals 0.132 px/s, 22.7 s, 75.8 s and 42.4 s to 1 %; `TestTheFlatStreetVectorAtTheShippedConstants` computes the same closed form from the constants in `sim/water.py`, so a later re-tune cannot silently break the physics; both with the 1 % tolerance kept; 12 of 12 pass, agent-reported). The three tests that broke when `HIT_AREA` went from 1.0 to 1.15 feed the field landed hits, so their `Q` depends on the constant; that is why the plan's literals are pinned to 1.0 inside the first class. The viewer's `FakeWater` reproduced the vector (rise 0.1319 px/s, 3 px at 22.8 s, spill at 75.9 s, 5 px cleared in 42.4 s, all within 1 %) **at `HIT_AREA` 1.0**; moving it to 1.15 with the same two-value check is the first item of the open viewer follow-up. Constants the build added to the table (all in `sim/water.py`): `WET_DEPTH` 0.05 px (below it a street counts as having no water and is not listed), `SPLIT_HYST` 0.5 px, `PROCESS_EVERY` 3 ticks, `INFLOW_WINDOW` 2.0 s, `WATER_EVERY` 3 and `FLOOD_EVERY` 6 frames, `WATER_DY` 0.05 and `WATER_DX` 0.5 px. `INLET_K` 0.2667, `SOAK` 0.015, `MIN_VOL` 0.3, `BASIN_MIN_DEPTH` 1.0, `CURB` 10, `INLET_SPACING` 60, `FLOOD_DEPTH` 3, the class sizes 3.0 / 4.7 / 8.2 and the kind ranges shipped as in the table.

**What the sim delivers today (measured while planning; a throwaway script outside the project, to be re-done properly in LS0):** `Simulation` 3 840 x 1 080, rolling, seed 11, one cloud spawned by command, surface forced to about +30 C so the rain lands as rain, merging and auto-spawn off. Rain hits per second per 32 px bucket under the cloud's rain span, mean over the precipitating stage (and the busiest single bucket):
| Rain class | Mean hits/s per bucket | Busiest bucket | Precipitating for | `precip_rate` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| nimbostratus (steady) | 3.4 | 13 | about 78 s | 0.08 falling to 0.03 |
| cumulonimbus (downpour) | 4.3 | 15 | about 90 s | 0.11 falling to 0.04 |
| supercell (forward flank) | 8.0 | 28 | about 139 s | 0.17 falling to 0.07 |
| stratus | none: it never rained in 200 s (it is born fair) | | | |
The spread between the rain classes is only about 2.4x, and a drizzle is the weak tail (2-3 hits/s) of a nimbostratus, not a class of its own. With `HIT_AREA` 1 that is 0.107 / 0.134 / 0.25 px/s of depth on a flat street; against the class capacities plus `SOAK` (0.055 / 0.113 / 0.314 px/s) the **arithmetic** is: small drains gain 0.05 / 0.08 / 0.20 px/s (3 px after 58 s / 38 s / 15 s), normal drains gain nothing / 0.02 / 0.14 px/s (never / 143 s / 22 s), large drains gain nothing in any of them. These numbers are not tuned and not tested; they are why the sizes span 7x in capacity.

**Re-measured in LS0 (agent-reported; 8 seeds, the cloud's rain span pinned over the biggest town, speed 1; raw files were in a scratchpad folder that is gone, the script `tools/measure_rain_hits.py` stays):** hits per second per 32 px bucket 4.36 (nimbostratus) / 5.78 (cumulonimbus) / 8.19 (supercell) against the planner's 3.4 / 4.3 / 8.0; raining for 80 / 99 / 174 s; hits per px of the town's own span per second 0.064 / 0.093 / 0.102 (the town is only partly under the span). So the spread between the classes is about 1.9x per bucket, not 2.4x, and the supercell differs from the cumulonimbus mostly by how long it rains. At speed x8 the rain per **simulated** second falls to 3.72 / 3.66 / 6.29 because `PRECIP_TICK_CAP` (14 particles per cloud per tick) bites (a cumulonimbus delivers 37 % less). Two merged clouds over one town: 7.6 / 10.3 / 15.4, about 2x, not more. A cloud that drifts freely misses a given town in half the runs, which is why the table pins the span. The frame is **26.5 kB dry and 46.5 kB in rain** (p95 tick 5.3 ms) at 3 840 x 1 080, seed 12, before the water code: the "about 125 KB" budget written in LS0 was not the real figure.

**The tuning contract (LS6 measures; none of these is allowed to be met by a test that only checks "it rained")** — the failure fingerprint of this project is a test that asserts an event but not its size or duration (`.stacker/state.json`: showcase rain stage lasted 8-10 s):
* C1 drizzle (a raining cloud with `precip_rate` under 0.05): no class floods; streets at most `wet`.
* C2 steady rain (nimbostratus): `normal` and `large` stay `wet`; `small` may pond and may reach `flooded` only after more than 45 s of its peak rain.
* C3 downpour (cumulonimbus): `small` reaches `flooded` within the cloud's rain; `normal` ponds at most; `large` stays `wet`.
* C4 supercell forward flank: `small` reaches `overflowing`; `normal` reaches `flooded`; `large` never reaches `flooded` (merged storms over one town are measured and reported, not forced).
* C5 a 5 px flood under `normal` drains clears within 120 s once the rain stops; a 1.5 px natural puddle forms within 30 s of steady rain and is dry within 120 s of its end (the old puddle's rate).
* C6 an unlimited natural pool: in one long storm a hollow's body grows past 140 px wide with nothing but its rim stopping it (no `PUDDLE_MAX_W`-like constant exists in `sim/water.py`).

**How the contract came out (`[UPDATE]` 2026-10-11; 22 storms per rain class, 8 seeds x every town, `HIT_AREA` 1.15; the full table is under "Phase L verification").** C1 **partly met**: no class floods in a drizzle, but small drains pond about 2 px (the contract said "at most `wet`"). C2 **met** on towns of 650 px and up (a nimbostratus floods none of the three classes in 14 of 14 runs). C3 **met for towns of 650 px and up** (cumulonimbus: small flooded 14 of 14, normal 0 of 14, large 0 of 14) and **missed for short towns** (under 650 px the rain core covers the whole street and normal drains flood in 7 of the 8 such storms). C4 **missed in part**: a supercell makes small drains overflow in 11 of 14 and normal drains flood in only 8 of 14 (the large ones never flood: met), because the supercell and the cumulonimbus differ by duration, not intensity. C5 is **not separately evidenced** in the sessions (the closed form clears 5 px at normal size in 42.4 s, inside the 120 s asked; the 1.5 px natural puddle's 30 s / 120 s timings are not recorded). C6 is covered by `test_water.py`'s no-cap pool test per the session log; this sync did not re-run it. The whole-world check the contract asks for, "`normal`-mode worlds show both flooding and non-flooding towns in the same storm", holds in the 22-storm table (short towns flood, long ones do not).

7. **[REVISED] LS1 — `sim/water.py`, the basin map (pure functions).** *As built:* the module is `TerrainMap` (the ground cut at its divides into segments, each holding a basin, a street, a pond or the world edge; a hollow shallower than `BASIN_MIN_DEPTH` joins the neighbour across its lower divide) plus `find_basins`, `catchment`, `volume_at`, `level_for_volume`, `span_at` as thin wrappers; **level and volume are exact** (`Profile`: volume is a piecewise quadratic in the level, so the level needs no bisection to 0.01 px as planned). Rain on the slopes of a town is routed past it to the nearest non-street element on its own side. Evidence (agent-reported): `test_water.py` sweeps the basin map over the 4 terrain kinds and the 21 presets x 8 seeds. The done-when's last clause, "a flat world yields no basin", does not hold for a sandbox with settlements (the census in LS0 found hollows between the pads); how `test_water.py` treats it is not recorded in the sessions. *Plan text:* Create the module (the only new sim file) with the terrain-derived part: `find_basins(terrain, street_spans, pond_spans)` (local minima of `terrain.y` after pruning those shallower than `BASIN_MIN_DEPTH`, each with its two rims and its sill), `catchment(terrain, ...)` (bucket k at `x = k * GROUND_STEP` -> a basin, a street, a pond, or the edge, by walking downhill; street-from-outside is routed past the town), `volume_at(terrain, span, level)`, `level_for_volume(...)` (bisection to 0.01 px) and `span_at(terrain, anchor, level)` (shoreline by linear interpolation of the samples). No state, no `random`, derived from the terrain, the settlement spans and the pond objects, so nothing is saved for it. Done when: `test_water.py` proves, for every terrain kind, the 21 presets and eight seeds each: volume -> level -> volume within 0.5 %; no body's span contains terrain above its level (the float gap is 0 by construction, measured like Phase K's K1); `x0` / `x1` lie where the level meets the terrain within 0.2 px or at a curb or world edge; a flat world yields no basin.
8. **[REVISED] LS2 — Natural pools.** *As built:* `WaterField.update` tallies the buckets every tick and `_process` runs every 3rd tick (`PROCESS_EVERY`) in sub-steps of at most 0.25 s: `_settle` (spill over the lower rim, merge when both levels stand at the shared rim, a pair that would hand water to each other runs off), `_soak`, `_finish` (remove below `MIN_VOL`, refresh level and shore). **Added, not planned: a merged body splits again** when its level falls `SPLIT_HYST` 0.5 px below an inner rim (the largest part keeps the id, the others are new bodies). A ledger of rain, soaked, drained, lost and absorbed water backs the conservation test. Evidence (agent-reported): `test_water.py` covers fill, spill, merge, split, dry and conservation; this sync did not re-run it. The viewer's `FakeWater` does not split (merged pools stay merged until dry), so the Debug Flood tab and the game differ there. *Plan text:* `WaterField.update(dt, rain_hits)`: add each bucket's `n x HIT_AREA` to its basin, every third tick process the accumulated inflow in sub-steps of at most 0.25 s (soak, spill over the lower rim, merge when two levels reach the shared rim, remove below `MIN_VOL`, retire ids), keep `bodies` for the wire. Pools exist only while they hold water (a puddle starts as a thin film at the bed and widens). Done when: conservation holds (rain added = stored + soaked + spilled + lost at the edge, within 0.1 %, over a long storm on rolling, hilly and mountainous worlds); a basin of about 400 px fills past 140 px wide and then spills into the next one with no cap (C6); two hollows fed to their shared rim become one body that keeps the larger id; a body dries to nothing and is removed; the same inputs give the same bodies after a save / load (state in LS5).

## Phase M — Buildings regenerate: scaffold and the whole building process (protocol v2.5; sim and viewer)

**Project context:** `[UPDATE]` — extends the build with Phases H–L (the whole project was read for this phase: Python 3.11 stdlib sim in `sim/`, a plain-`javac` Java2D viewer of about 70 files in `viewer/src/precip`, JDK 25, Windows 11, no git, desktop icon `C:\Users\lenard\Desktop\Precipitation.lnk` -> `run.bat`). **This phase starts after Phase L's LI closes** and is designed against the code Phase L leaves behind: protocol v2.4 (`hello.water`, `hello.drains`, `frame.water` / `flood` / `drains`, `sim/water.py`, `WaterPainter.java`, `DrainPainter.java`, `DrainCard.java`, `FakeWater.java`, `DebugFlood.java`), windsocks gone, the glint as a dot, droplets that follow the wind, Debug mode with nine tabs. Where Phase L is still being edited when this is read, function names are quoted and line numbers are not. Phase I's Debug mode and Phase J's Advanced settings are the patterns this phase follows; Phase L's two-agent split (one sim agent, one viewer agent, no shared file) is the shape of the work.

**The user's request (2026-10-10, verbatim):** "Make the Building Regenerate with visual Scaffold and the entire process of building." It is read as five things: (1) after a tornado, every damaged or destroyed building is **rebuilt by the sim over game time** and ends whole (this relaxes v2.3's "damage never decreases"); (2) the rebuilding is a **visible, ordered process** and not a fade: wait for the storm, fence the site and clear the rubble, **erect scaffold** (poles, ledgers, plank decks, ladders, safety netting, bay by bay and lift by lift), raise the structure floor by floor, walls and cladding, roof, windows and fittings, take the scaffold down, finished; (3) lightly damaged buildings skip the stages they do not need, a destroyed one is built from the ground up, big kinds get a tower crane and small ones a skip, a ladder and pallets, with tiny workers on the front row; (4) the pace is sized so that a town is **visibly under reconstruction between storms** (not whole again in seconds, not never); (5) Debug mode shows the whole process and a setting and a live command control it. The design is the planner's and is **not confirmed by the user**: each reading is an open question (58–67, the defaults applied) and SA-Asker session `phase-m-plan-buildings-regenerate-with-s-20261010-202031` (Q1–Q10, advisory, asked while this was written).

The design is additive: `version` stays 2; every new key is optional; a v2.4 viewer on a v2.5 stream keeps the last damage picture it saw and draws no reconstruction (it ignores a `damage` row that is not higher, exactly as it does today, and ignores the unknown keys); a v2.5 viewer on a v2.4 stream, replay or save draws no reconstruction (no `hello.repair`); and a v2.4 save with damaged buildings in it **starts rebuilding from the saved damage** when loaded by v2.5. The one rule that changes is the sentence in the Tornadoes section of `PROTOCOL.md`: *damage never decreases* becomes *damage rises only when a tornado hits and falls only while the object is being rebuilt*.

> **Parallelism and file ownership.** Two implementers and the orchestrator; **MS and MV share no file** and never need to talk: the only coupling is the v2.5 block below (moved into `PROTOCOL.md` by M0) and the model table. MV proves itself on `FakeSim` + `FakeRepair` and on a hand-written replay; MS proves itself headless and through the protocol schema test.
> * **MS — Sim reconstruction agent** (`python-tooling-dev`): owns `sim/repair.py` (new), `sim/tests/test_repair.py` (new), `tools/measure_repair.py` (new), `tools/debug_plots/v25/` (new, diagnostic plots), and small additive edits to `sim/objects.py` (two methods and one line in `apply_tornadoes`), `sim/simulation.py`, `sim/precip_sim.py`, and the tests `sim/tests/_schema.py`, `test_protocol_schema.py`, `test_tornado.py`, `test_saveload.py`, `test_commands.py`, `test_objects.py`, `test_perf.py`. **May not touch** `sim/water.py`, `settlements.py`, `terrain.py`, `particles.py`, `cars.py`, `viewer/`, `PROTOCOL.md`, `PLAN.md`, `saves/`, `config/`, `scrap/`.
> * **MV — Viewer reconstruction agent** (`general-purpose`): owns `ScaffoldPainter.java`, `SiteLife.java`, `FakeRepair.java`, `SiteCard.java`, `DebugRebuild.java`, `RepairTest.java` (all new), the new folder `viewer/snapshots/v25/`, and small additive edits to `Model.java`, `ViewerState.java`, `Shared.java`, `BuildingPainter.java` (a hook in `paint`, `liveWorks`, the tree's regrowth), `Renderer.java` (`coverLevels`, `paintGround`, the live object loop in `drawWorld`, one card call), `FakeSim.java`, `Advanced.java`, `Settings.java`, `SettingsScreen.java`, `AdvancedTest.java`, `NewSimScreen.java`, `GameHud.java`, `Action.java`, `KeyMap.java`, `ViewerPanel.java` (`perform`), `DebugScreen.java` (one tab and one key), `UiSnapshot.java`, `Main.java` (the `--until repair:` form and one added test call). **May not touch** `sim/`, `PROTOCOL.md`, `PLAN.md`, `saves/`, `config/`, `scrap/`, `build.bat` (each edit of a shared file is small, additive and made after re-reading the spot, Phase I's rule; the Phase L files are all closed by then).
> * **Orchestrator:** M0 (the contract) before either starts; MI (integration) after both finish. The two agents may run at the same time and each compiles into its own scratch folder (`javac -d`), as Phase L does.

**What each part does**
| Part | Role |
|---|---|
| `PROTOCOL.md` | v2.5 contract: `hello.repair`, `hello.objects[].repair`, `frame.repair`, `frame.repair_info`, falling `frame.damage`, the `repair` command, `--repair`, the save rule, old streams and old saves |
| `sim/repair.py` (new, MS) | The reconstruction model as pure functions (`plan`, `damage_at`, `size_k`) and the `RepairField` state machine: sites, crews and queue, the storm gate, hold, second hit, healing, trees, wire rows, state |
| `sim/objects.py`, `sim/simulation.py`, `sim/precip_sim.py` (MS) | Lowering `dmg_f` and the wire `damage` in 1/24 steps, the list of objects hit this tick, building the field (sandbox only), calling it after `objects.update`, hello and frame keys, the `repair` command, `--repair`, save, load and old saves |
| `ScaffoldPainter.java` (new, MV) | Everything still that a site shows, drawn into the ground tile: hoarding and warning signs, rubble being cleared, the scaffold (poles, ledgers, decks, ladders, toe boards, netting) bay by bay and lift by lift, the bare frame, the fresh cladding, the roof, the glazing, material stacks, work lamps; readable on the back row at 0.9 scale and haze |
| `SiteLife.java` (new, MV) | Everything that moves, front row only: tower crane (jib, trolley, hook, load, red light at night), hoist, workers, flapping net, rest when `hold` |
| `FakeRepair.java` (new, MV) | The Java mirror of `sim/repair.py`: the same constants (one commented block), `plan`, `damage_at`, the same state rules, emitting JSON in the protocol's shape, so the viewer and Debug run without Python; plus the FakeSim-only `wreck` command |
| `Model.java`, `ViewerState.java`, `Shared.java` (MV) | Parse and keep the new keys; accept a falling `damage` row only when `hello.repair` is present; per-object repair state; coalescing of bake changes |
| `BuildingPainter.java`, `Renderer.java` (MV) | The hook that lets the existing damage painting run in reverse under the scaffold; the bake key; the live pass; trees regrow |
| `SiteCard.java`, `GameHud.java`, `Action.java`, `KeyMap.java`, `ViewerPanel.java`, `NewSimScreen.java`, `Settings.java`, `Advanced.java`, `SettingsScreen.java` (MV) | The site card (stage name and percent), speed chips and the `B` key, the New Simulation "Reconstruction" choice, the Advanced "No construction details" switch |
| `DebugRebuild.java` (new), `DebugScreen.java` (MV) | Debug mode's tenth tab, Rebuild: one building of each kind with a scrubber through the whole process, and a small wrecked town that rebuilds |

### What the sim delivers today (measured while planning; throwaway scripts in the session's scratch folder, to be redone properly in MS0)
`Simulation` stepped at 0.1 s of sim time per step (speed 3; the tornado's stage lengths are constants and the damage is linear in `dt`, so this does not change the figures below), read-only use of `sim/`.
* **A tornado forced over a town** (3 840 x 1 080, rolling, `normal` settlements, seeds 11–21; one supercell spawned over the settlement with the most damageable buildings, `rot_peak` and `wall_peak` 0.95, the `tornado` command at maturity; 11 runs): the funnel is **on the ground (`ground_factor` > 0) for 57–73 s** (mean 65 s), alive 75–94 s from the command, and **damage rows are produced over a 44–64 s window** (9 of 11 runs; the two EF0 runs that missed the town produced none). Peak ratings 0 to 4. Damageable buildings at 0.04 or more afterwards (8 runs scheduled): 1, 6, 7, 9, 9, 10, 13, 14 (trees are extra, 0–15 of 66). The final damage is **bimodal for small kinds** (houses: 0.02–0.08 in an EF0; 0.04–1.0 in an EF1–EF2, mostly above 0.4 and several at 1.0) and **partial for towers** (apartments 0.15–0.35, offices 0.07–0.46, skyscrapers 0.02–0.25 from the facade rule; one apartment reached 1.0 in an EF3).
* **A supercell world** (preset `oklahoma`, month 5, hour 16 with the clock held, 1 920 x 1 080, `normal` settlements, 4 seeds x 1 800 s): 2, 3, 2 and 2 tornadoes (9 in 7 200 s, **one per about 800 s**); the gaps from the end of one grounded tornado to the next touchdown were **1 600, 581, 318, 1 021 and 888 s** (median 888 s, shortest 318 s); each tornado was grounded 60–70 s; a tornado was on the ground for 5.4–11.1 % of the time (7.9 % on average). A cool April morning makes none (the existing `TestHowOften`).
* **Wind at the buildings** (the largest wind speed over all damageable buildings, sampled each second, 4 seeds x 1 800 s): median 5.2–5.5 m/s; above 16 m/s in 2.0–6.2 % of the seconds and above 20 m/s in 1.3–5.2 %; the maxima (30–59 m/s) are the tornado itself. A gust limit of 18 m/s therefore stops work in about 2–6 % of the time, nearly all of it near a tornado.
* **Object sizes** (5 worlds, 3 840 x 1 080, rolling): house 59 x 49 (39–91 x 36–95), shop 50 x 53, barn 93 x 64, church 64 x 120, windmill 52 x 99, water tower 40 x 87, apartment 70 x 101 (4–7 floors), office 65 x 171 (8–32 floors), skyscraper 58 x 233 (200–400 high, 15–36 floors), factory 120–170 x 70–100 (from the generator), streetlamp 6 x 31 (medians). **19 of 104 houses and all 3 barns have no settlement** (farms), and **a third to two thirds of every town kind stands in the back row** (`layer` 1: 35 of 104 houses, 13 of 34 offices, 25 of 39 apartments, 15 of 23 skyscrapers), which is why the scaffold has to read in the ground tile and not only in a live pass.
* **The planned stage model run over the real damage lists** (a standalone script, the model table below, crews per settlement kind, the 25 s settle): the first scaffold stands **31–43 s** after the tornado is gone, the last building of the town is finished **187–628 s** after (median about 370 s, 3 to 10 minutes), 1–7 scaffolds stand at once, single jobs last 45–496 s. Against a median gap of 888 s between natural tornadoes that is a town being rebuilt for about a fifth to seven tenths of the time (the shortest gap measured, 318 s, is a second hit on a town that is still half built) and whole in between; at speed 8 the same town is done in 23–78 s of real time.
These figures are measurements of the **current** sim, taken before any Phase M edit; MS0 repeats them as a script kept in `tools/`.

### The reconstruction model in numbers (the one table both implementers build from)
Every constant lives in `sim/repair.py` (one place) and is mirrored, in one commented block, in `FakeRepair.java`; the viewer never computes the sim's rates (the wire carries `stage`, `p` and `damage`). All are **starting values from planning arithmetic and the schedule check above** that MS6 re-tunes by measurement.
| Name | Start | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| Stages | 0 `wait`, 1 `clear`, 2 `scaffold`, 3 `structure`, 4 `walls`, 5 `roof`, 6 `fitout`, 7 `strip`, 8 `done`, 9 `regrow` | Buildings use 0–8, trees 9. `done` is only ever the last row of a site |
| `MODES` | off 0, slow 0.5, normal 1.0, fast 3.0 | Work rate: seconds of work done per second of sim time (the `speed` command multiplies it again, since `dt` already includes it) |
| `T_FULL` (s of work, `d0` = 1, reference size) | house 150, shop 160, barn 140, windmill 210, water_tower 240, church 300, apartment 330, factory 380, office 560, skyscraper 780, streetlamp 25 | The whole job with every stage at its full length |
| `A_REF` (w x h, px²) | house 59 x 49, shop 50 x 53, barn 93 x 64, windmill 52 x 99, water_tower 40 x 87, church 64 x 120, apartment 70 x 101, factory 145 x 85, office 65 x 171, skyscraper 58 x 233, streetlamp 6 x 31 | Medians of the generated worlds above |
| `size_k` | `sqrt(w x h / A_REF)` clamped to 0.7–1.5 | A bigger building takes longer, less than in proportion |
| `jitter` | `0.85 + 0.30 u`, `u = (crc32(f"{seed}:{id}:repair") & 0xFFFFFF) / 0xFFFFFF` | Per object, so a street does not finish on one tick. **Never the world's `random.Random`** (the seeded world and `tor_rng` must not shift) |
| `SHARE` of `T_FULL` | clear 0.08, scaffold 0.14, structure 0.28, walls 0.20, roof 0.12, fitout 0.12, strip 0.06 | Streetlamp only: clear 0.40, fitout 0.60 (no scaffold) |
| `need` (0–1, the part of a stage's full length that is needed) | clear `0.3 + 0.7 d0`; scaffold `0.5 + 0.5 d0`; structure `S = smooth((d0 - 0.50) / 0.50)`; walls `max(S, smooth((d0 - 0.10) / 0.60))`; roof `max(S, smooth((d0 - 0.04) / 0.40))`; fitout `0.4 + 0.6 d0`; strip = scaffold | `smooth(x) = x²(3 - 2x)` clamped to 0–1, the viewer's `Lit.smooth`. A stage whose need is under `MIN_NEED` 0.05 is **skipped** (a roof-bitten house has no structure stage; a destroyed one has all seven) |
| Stage seconds | `T_FULL x size_k x jitter x SHARE x need`, then scaled up so the whole job is at least `MIN_JOB_S` (45 s; streetlamp 20 s) | At normal speed, before dividing by the mode's rate |
| `damage_at` | `damage = d0 x (1 - done)`, `done` = work finished in {structure, walls, roof, fitout} over the total of those four; constant (`d0`) in `wait`, `clear`, `scaffold`; constant (0) in `strip`; the wire value is rounded to steps of **1/24** (two decimals) while a site is open | The painter's own thresholds do the rest: the ruin line (damage 0.8–1) rises as the walls go up, the torn corner (0.55–1) closes, the roof bites (0.04 up) close one by one, the lit windows return. `DAMAGE_STEP` equals the viewer's bake level `round(damage x 24)` |
| **Test vector** (house, jitter 1, `size_k` 1, normal) | `d0` 1.0: clear 12.0, scaffold 21.0, structure 42.0, walls 30.0, roof 18.0, fitout 18.0, strip 9.0 = 150.0 s; damage after structure 0.611, after walls 0.333, after roof 0.167, after fitout 0. `d0` 0.2: clear 5.77, scaffold 13.76, walls 2.43, roof 6.92, fitout 10.22, strip 5.90 = 45.0 s (scaled up to the minimum), damage 0.200 -> 0.175 -> 0.104 -> 0. Skyscraper `d0` 0.3: 295.2 s. Streetlamp `d0` 1: clear 10.0, fitout 15.0 | MS's test and MV's `FakeRepair` check must reproduce these to 0.01 s, recomputed from the constants if MS6 changes them |
| Reach of the figures | destroyed house 89–259 s (`size_k` 0.7–1.5, jitter 0.85–1.15); destroyed skyscraper about 550–1 350 s; a facade-damaged tower 100–310 s (one office 496 s) | |
| `HEAL_BELOW`, `HEAL_S` | 0.04, 20 s | Damage under 0.04 draws as intact: it is healed without a site (a `damage` row of 0 after 20 s) |
| `SETTLE_S` | 25 s | A site leaves `wait` after the gate has been open for this long without a break, and when a crew is free |
| Storm gate (`GATE_HZ` 5) | `SAFE_RADIUS` 1 200 px: no tornado (any stage, the funnel included) whose foot `x` is within it; `GUST_LIMIT` 18 m/s: the speed of `wind.sample(x, y - h)` at the site, smoothed over 3 s | While the gate is closed `wait` does not advance and every other stage sets `hold` (progress frozen, the crane and the workers rest). It reopens after 5 s of open gate |
| `CREWS` | hamlet 2, village 3, town 4, city 6 simultaneous sites per settlement; an object with no settlement (farms) has its own crew | The rest wait in `wait`, oldest hit first (ties by id) |
| Second hit | when a tornado raises `dmg_f` of an open site by `RESET_RISE` 0.03 or more above its planned value, the site is reset: stage `wait`, work lost, `d0` = the new `dmg_f` (the plan is recomputed), the scaffold is lost | Damage is one number, so the work done so far cannot be kept (open question 63). A hit that does not reach `RESET_RISE` is absorbed |
| Trees | `TREE_WAIT_S` 60, `TREE_REGROW_S` 480 at normal; `damage` falls linearly to 0 in 1/24 steps; stage 9, `p` = growth | A snapped tree (`d0` of about 0.45 or more) grows back from a sapling, a bent one straightens; no scaffold |
| Rows | a `frame.repair` row is sent when the stage changes, `hold` changes, or `p` has moved 0.04 since the last row for the id; collected and sent at most every 3rd frame | At most about 25 rows per site per stage |
| Viewer geometry (`ScaffoldPainter`) | bay 16 px wide, lift 11 px high (one floor, the generator's `floors = round(h / 11)`), poles at the bay joints, a plank deck and a toe board on each lift, a ladder every third bay, safety netting on every lift of the street side; at most 6 workers per site; tower crane for the kinds in `DAMAGE_BIG` and for `church`; skip, ladder and pallets for the rest | The viewer's own numbers; they are the planner's and only the stage names and `p` cross the wire |
| Bake steps (`P_STEPS` per stage) | wait 1, clear 4, scaffold 8, structure `min(12, floors)`, walls 6, roof 6, fitout 6, strip 8, regrow 6 | `p` is quantised to these in the ground tile's level key; the live pass uses the eased `p` |

**The tuning contract (MS6 measures; none of these may be met by a test that only checks "it happened")** — the failure fingerprint of this project is a test that asserts an event but not its size or duration:
* R1 a house destroyed at normal is back in 89–259 s of work, a skyscraper destroyed in about 550–1 350 s, and `slow` doubles and `fast` divides by 3 (to 5 %);
* R2 no damaged building of 0.04 or more is whole within 45 s of its tornado being gone, and none still waits 90 s after the gate has been open (when a crew is free);
* R3 after a forced tornado over a town of 6–14 damaged buildings the first scaffold stands within 60 s of the tornado being gone and the last building is finished within 720 s (the measured 187–628 s), at normal;
* R4 while a tornado is within 1 200 px, or the gust at the site is over 18 m/s, no site advances (`hold`) and none leaves `wait`; over an Oklahoma run the gate is closed for no more than about 8 % of the seconds;
* R5 a second hit on a site at `scaffold` or later sends it back to `wait` with the higher `d0`; a hit below `RESET_RISE` does not;
* R6 a v2.4 state with damage loads, every object at 0.04 or more gets a site at `wait` (in id order), the rest heal; `--repair off` keeps every damage value for ever, byte-identical to the v2.4 run;
* R7 reconstruction draws no random number: `PLAIN_WORLD_HASHES` and every seeded world, and `tor_rng`'s end state, are unchanged.

### Protocol v2.5 additions (paste-ready; M0 moves this block into `PROTOCOL.md` unchanged, after "Save files (v2.4)", and edits one sentence in the Tornadoes section)
````markdown
## Launch (v2.5)
```
python -u sim/precip_sim.py ... [--repair off|slow|normal|fast]
```
* `--repair` (sandbox; default `normal`) is how fast damaged objects are rebuilt (below): `off` never (exactly the
  v2.3 / v2.4 behaviour: damage stays for ever), `slow` half speed, `normal`, `fast` three times. It is accepted and
  has no effect in every other mode. With `--load` the saved choice wins. A bad value exits non-zero with a stderr line.

## Reconstruction
Until v2.4 an object's `damage` only rose. Since v2.5 an object that a tornado damaged is **rebuilt over game time**
and ends whole. `damage` therefore has three kinds of row: a row **higher** than the last one is a tornado hit; a row
**lower** than the last one is the work (the sim has rebuilt part of the object); a row of `0` is the object finished
(it has no `damage` key in `hello.objects` any more, like one that was never hit). A viewer that does not know
`hello.repair` keeps the old rule (it ignores a row that is not higher) and so keeps showing the damage it last saw.

`hello.repair` (sandbox mode only; its presence means "this stream uses the v2.5 reconstruction model"):
```json
"repair":{"model":1,"mode":"normal","rate":1.0,
          "stages":["wait","clear","scaffold","structure","walls","roof","fitout","strip","done","regrow"],
          "crews":{"hamlet":2,"village":3,"town":4,"city":6}}
```
* `mode` ∈ `off | slow | normal | fast`, `rate` the work rate it means (0, 0.5, 1, 3). `stages` names the stage
  numbers used below (the viewer shows these names on its card; it may have its own).

A **site** is an object being rebuilt. Its **stages**, in order (a stage that is not needed is skipped; a lightly
damaged house has no `structure`; a streetlamp has only `clear` and `fitout`; a tree has only `regrow`):
| stage | name | what is going on, and what `p` (0 to 1) measures |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | `wait` | The storm is near, the wind too strong, or no crew is free. The damaged object stands as it was. `p` = the settle timer |
| 1 | `clear` | The site is fenced and the rubble is carried away. `p` = rubble cleared |
| 2 | `scaffold` | Scaffold goes up, bay by bay and lift by lift. `p` = share of the bays and lifts erected |
| 3 | `structure` | The frame is raised, floor by floor. `p` = height reached. `damage` falls |
| 4 | `walls` | Walls and cladding. `p` = share finished. `damage` falls |
| 5 | `roof` | Roof structure, then the covering. `p` = share finished. `damage` falls |
| 6 | `fitout` | Windows, doors and fittings, the lights come back. `p` = share finished. `damage` falls to 0 |
| 7 | `strip` | The scaffold comes down, top lift first. `p` = share taken down |
| 8 | `done` | The last row of a site, in the same frame as `damage` `[id, 0]` |
| 9 | `regrow` | Trees only: a snapped tree grows back from a sapling, a bent one straightens. `p` = growth; `damage` falls linearly |

`damage` is constant in stages 0 to 2 and falls in 3 to 6 (and in 9); while a site is open its value moves in steps
of 1/24 (two decimals), which is the viewer's own picture step. `frame.repair` (optional; only the sites that changed):
```json
"repair":[[17,3,0.35,0.92,0],[23,2,0.50,0.30,1],[41,8,1.0,0.12,0]]
```
Each row is `[id, stage, p, d0, hold]`: `d0` is the damage the object had when this rebuild was planned (constant for
the whole site, so a viewer needs nothing else to draw the broken parts and what is still to build), `hold` is `1`
while the work is stopped (a tornado is near or the gust is over the limit: the crane and the workers rest, the
progress does not move). A row is sent when the stage changes, when `hold` changes, and when `p` has moved by 0.04
since the last row for that id; the list is collected and sent at most every 3rd frame. The first row of a site
(stage 0) is sent as soon as the site exists; the last is stage 8 with a `damage` row of `0` in the same frame. A
**second hit** (a tornado raises the damage of a site) sends the site back to stage 0 with a higher `d0`; the viewer
drops its scaffold then. An id the viewer does not know is ignored.

`hello.objects[].repair` (optional; only for an object that is a site when the `hello` is made, so a loaded or a
late-joining viewer starts mid-build):
```json
{"id":17,"kind":"house","x":900.0,"y":962.0,"w":64.0,"h":52.0,"damage":0.61,"repair":{"stage":3,"p":0.35,"d0":0.92,"hold":false}}
```

`frame.repair_info` (optional; the summary for a card; sent on the first frame after a `hello` or a load, after a
`repair` command, and when a count changes, at most every 6th frame):
```json
"repair_info":{"mode":"normal","sites":5,"queued":3,"finished":12}
```
`sites` = objects with a site not in `wait`, `queued` = sites in `wait`, `finished` = rebuilt since the world began.

**Who is rebuilt, and when.** Every building kind that can be damaged (the kinds with a damage threshold: `house`,
`barn`, `church`, `windmill`, `shop`, `apartment`, `office`, `skyscraper`, `factory`, `water_tower`, `streetlamp`)
gets a site when its damage reaches 0.04 (below that it draws as intact and is healed after 20 s without a site).
Trees regrow (stage 9) without scaffold. Cars, roads, the tornado's scars on the ground and anything else are not
rebuilt. A site waits until no tornado is within 1 200 px and the gust at the site is 18 m/s or less, for 25 s on
end; a settlement has 2 (hamlet) to 6 (city) crews and the rest queue, oldest hit first. Work does not stop at night
or in rain. No site is ever started in the showcase, hurricane or world modes.

## Commands (v2.5)
| command | meaning |
|---|---|
| `{"cmd":"repair","mode":"slow"}` | `off`, `slow`, `normal` or `fast`: the work rate from now on (`off` freezes every open site and starts none; the damage stays) |
| `{"cmd":"repair","finish":true}` | every site is finished now: each object's damage goes to 0 in one frame |
| `{"cmd":"repair","finish":17}` | the same for one object |

A bad value, an unknown id or a missing key is ignored. Each accepted command is answered by one
`frame.repair_info` on the next frame. `repair` is ignored outside sandbox mode.

## Save files (v2.5)
The state file gains a `repair` block: the mode, each open site (object id, stage, the seconds of work done in the
stage, `d0`, the gate timers, `hold`), the queue order, the count finished, and the row bookkeeping, so *save → load → run
M* is frame-for-frame identical to an uninterrupted run, in the middle of a build. A state file without it (v2.4 and
older) loads with mode `normal` and **a site at stage `wait` for every damageable object whose saved damage is 0.04 or
more, in id order** (the rest heal): the player's damaged town begins to be rebuilt from the damage it was saved with.

## Older streams and viewers (v2.5)
* A v2.4 (or older) viewer on a v2.5 stream ignores `hello.repair`, `frame.repair`, `frame.repair_info` and
  `hello.objects[].repair`, and ignores every `damage` row that is not higher than the last: it keeps the last
  damage picture it saw for each object and draws no reconstruction. It does not fail.
* A v2.5 viewer on a stream without `hello.repair` (a v2.4 sim, a replay or a save written before) draws no
  reconstruction and keeps the old rule for `damage` rows.
````

**Compatibility, in one place.** Old viewer on new stream: keeps the damaged picture, never repaired (above). New viewer on old stream: the old guard stays and nothing is drawn. Old save on new sim: rebuilding starts from the saved damage, in `wait`. New save on old sim: the old sim ignores the unknown block and its damaged buildings stay as they are; the saved `damage` values are the partly-repaired ones, so a v2.4 sim shows the building at the damage it had when the game was saved. The tests that assert that damage only rises (`test_tornado.py::test_buildings_on_the_path_gain_damage_monotonically_and_the_rest_stay_whole`, the `damage` rows of `sim/tests/_schema.py`) are **rewritten, not deleted**: damage is still monotone while a tornado is damaging an object and the test keeps saying so about `ObjectField.apply_tornadoes` on its own (reconstruction is not driven there), and a new test states the new rule for the whole `Simulation`; `_schema.py` accepts a `damage` row of 0 only when the same stream's `hello` carries `repair`.

### How the viewer draws it (the bake constraint, and why this does not thrash the tile cache)
* **Still detail goes into the ground tile.** `Renderer.paintGround` paints the back row (at 0.9 scale and haze 0.36), the terrain, then the front row; each building comes from `BuildingPainter.paint`. A site's fence, scaffold, bare frame, fresh cladding, roof tarp, glazing, stacks of material, ladders and netting are all still, so they are drawn there by `ScaffoldPainter.paintStatic(g, o, lit, haze, stage, p, ...)`, called from `BuildingPainter.paint` after the building itself, using the same `Lit.lit(colour, haze)` the building uses, so **night, haze, the colour casts, wet (planks darker) and snow (a white line on the top deck) behave as they do for a building**. The scaffold's lines are never thinner than one device pixel, so a back-row scaffold at 0.9 scale and haze still reads. The shell is the existing damage painting run in reverse: `paint` already takes `o.damage`, and as it falls the ruin line rises, the torn corner closes and the roof bites close (`Lightning`'s shadow caster reads the same `o.damage`, so the shadow of a half-built tower follows it). `ScaffoldPainter` adds what that painting cannot show: the bare columns and floor slabs where the walls are not up yet, new cladding in a lighter shade, a roof tarp, glazing film.
* **Anything that moves is a live pass and only for the front row.** `SiteLife.paint` is called from the live object loop in `Renderer.drawWorld` for `layer != 1`, when the Advanced switch allows it and a site is in a working stage (2–7), like `paintDrops` and `paintLive`: the crane's jib swings and its hook and load travel, the hoist runs, workers walk the decks and climb the ladders, the net flaps with the wind at the building, the crane carries a red light that blinks at night. On `hold` they rest (workers shelter, the jib turns free with the wind). A back-row building has none of it, because anything drawn live over the ground tile would land on top of the front-row buildings that stand in front of it (the same reason running drops are front row only, Phase K). `BuildingPainter.liveWorks(o)` also returns false for an open site, so a windmill's sails, chimney smoke and the aviation light stay off while the building is under scaffold.
* **The bake key.** `Renderer.coverLevels` keys each building by `round(snow x 12) x 16 + round(wet x 6) + round(damage x 24) x 4096` plus the wind level in bits 20–22. Phase M adds `(stage x 16 + pStep) << 23`, which uses bits 23–30 and still fits an `int` (`255 << 23` is 2 139 095 040); the diagnostic mask `(1 << 20) - 1` that counts "nothing but the wind moved" must be widened to ignore the new bits. A tree under repair gets the same key (the tree rule `o.damage > 0 && kind tree`).
* **Levels added.** Only a building that is a site has the repair part of the key; a world with no site has exactly today's keys. A site passes through, in order, wait 1, clear 4, scaffold 8, structure `min(12, floors)`, walls 6, roof 6, fitout 6 and strip 8 steps, and the damage levels change inside structure to fitout, so the key moves about **30–60 times over a job** (a destroyed skyscraper about 60, a destroyed house about 40, a roof-bitten house about 35) and **not** the product of the two ranges (the 24 damage steps fall inside the 20–30 steps of stages 3 to 6 and move with them). `TileLayer` keeps no tile per level: a changed key bumps the version and repaints only the tiles that overlap the building (`dirtyX0` to `dirtyX1`, the building's x range plus 20 px, one or two 384 px tiles) on the worker threads while the stale tile stays on screen. So the cost is a **repaint rate**, not a cache size: at normal speed a town with six open sites repaints about 2–5 tiles a second (30–60 changes over 45–150 s per site), and the plan holds it down in three ways: (1) the sim sends a row only on a 0.04 step of `p`; (2) `ViewerState` applies the repair part of a key at most every 0.5 s of wall time per object and keeps the newest (so speed 8 does not multiply the repaints by 8); (3) `Shared.siteRepaints` counts them so MV3 can report the rate. **These numbers are arithmetic, not measured**: MV3 measures the repaint rate of a ten-site town at speed 1 and 8 and the frame cost, and the targets are at most 6 tile repaints a second at speed 1 and at most 12 at speed 8, with the mean frame time within the run-to-run spread of the baseline.
* **Cost of the live pass.** At most 6 workers and one crane per front-row site in view, drawn from rectangles and lines (the Phase K lesson on what a shape costs): target at most about 0.3 ms a frame with six sites in view at zoom 1–2.
