## Phase N — Water levels out, and land falls: overland flow sized by the flood (protocol v2.4.1; sim and viewer)

**Project context:** `[UPDATE]` — extends the build with Phases H–L (the whole project was read for this phase: Python 3.11 stdlib sim in `sim/`, a plain-`javac` Java2D viewer of about 70 files in `viewer/src/precip`, JDK 25, Windows 11, no git, desktop icon `C:\Users\lenard\Desktop\Precipitation.lnk` -> `run.bat`). It is built **now, before Phase M** (open question 77, answered), on the **water code Phase L wrote** (`sim/water.py` 952 lines: `TerrainMap`, `Profile`, `Body`, `WaterField`; `viewer/src/precip/WaterPainter.java`, `FakeWater.java`, `DebugFlood.java`), read on 2026-10-11. **Phase L's close-out is not finished** (LV recorded 2026-10-10 21:47; LS6 recorded VERIFIED 21:54 with `HIT_AREA` 1.15; LS is being finished right now by session `phase-l-sim-drains-finish-20261011-102413`, which may still change `sim/water.py` and `sim/tests/`), so **constants are quoted "as of 2026-10-11 10:25" and the first sim and viewer steps re-read them**. The user's request (2026-10-10, verbatim): "Make Water Level Out and add in Land Falls For water to go down hills easier i dont want it to look like water droplets going down like the current build i want it to vary in size depending on the height of the flood in the city or somewhere else". **The user's answer after looking at the running game (2026-10-11, verbatim, with a screenshot of a town's right edge at night): "its still the same as before i want it to level out at the lowest point of the world and if theres more land it will go there"**. The screenshot shows a flat town pad carrying several px of water with a level top that ends in a slanted ramp exactly where the road starts to fall away, the road beyond it dry (cars drive on it, rain splashes on it, nothing runs down it): water standing above lower ground, held by nothing visible. That is Phase L's **virtual curb** (`CURB` 10 px, `e_s = min(e0, e1) + CURB`) with the `RAMP` end; the planner's own pictures of the build show the same thing (`r1_end_f100.png`, below). **So the design is overturned in one place and settled in four:**
1. **No virtual curb, no wall.** Water never stands above ground that has lower ground next to it. A street span is ordinary ground that happens to have drains. Water runs down to the lowest point it can reach, fills that hollow to a level surface, and when the hollow is full it spills onward to the next lower land, and so on (answer to open question 75, **not assumed**).
2. **"If there's more land it will go there":** a rising body spreads over whatever land its level reaches, **including a town's street** (a pool whose level reaches a street span covers it and they are one level surface), and overflow goes on to the next lower ground instead of piling up. Phase L's "a street never merges with the hollow beside it" and "a full hollow hands its water over the town" are reversed.
3. **Land falls** = the water that runs down is a **film over the ground** (a stream that cascades on steep ground, a thin sheet on a gentle slope); it is the same water as the flood on a street, so its thickness follows how deep the flood is (open questions 74, 78; 74 and 76 are still the planner's defaults, **not confirmed**).
4. **Down hills easier** = rain that falls on a hillside runs down it as a thin sheet and arrives after a travel time, instead of appearing in the hollow at once (open question 76).

**What this means for the towns (census before the build, 8 seeds x rolling / hilly / mountainous / flat = 88 settlements):** with no curb a street holds standing water only if the terrain holds it. **45 of 88 (51 %) sit on ground that sheds: no hollow of their own and no hollow that can back up into them; they will never hold a standing flood, a town on a hilltop included, and the rain the drains cannot take runs off the low end as a sheet.** 8 of 88 have a pad that is itself a hollow of 3 px or more, 29 have a hollow beside them whose lower rim faces the town (it overflows toward it), 6 have both: **43 of 88 can ever see standing water on their street**. The median depth a pad holds on its own is 0.08 px (75th percentile 0.41; the deepest 66.6 px). The census counts the terrain's ability only; whether a given storm fills the hollow is for NS6 to measure. A shedding town can still show a thick **sheet** of water over its street when the drains are exceeded: on the starting constants a flat 814 px street with normal drains has 5.0 px at its low end in a steady rain and 10 px under a supercell, and with large drains none (prototype below), so **the size of the drains still decides whether the city floods**, but "flooded" now means a sheet running off the street, not a pool held by a wall.

**Start condition and order.** Phase N implementers start only after Phase L's LS outcome is recorded (the session above) and Phase L's LI closes: they rewrite parts of the files LS is finishing (`water.py`, `test_water.py`, `_schema.py`). **Phase N is built before Phase M (answered by the user).** To leave Phase M's text untouched, **the protocol is labelled v2.4.1** (an amendment of v2.4: `version` stays 2, the keys are additive, and the model change is switched on by `hello.flow`) instead of taking v2.5; Phase M keeps v2.5 and its own `M0`. N0 puts the block into `PROTOCOL.md` as its own `# v2.4.1 additions` section **immediately after the section "Save files (v2.4)"** (the last section of the file today, line about 721) and edits three existing v2.4 passages only by adding a pointer "(replaced by v2.4.1 when `hello.flow` is present)": the "Street flooding" paragraph, the `frame.flood` state list and the `curb` line of `hello.drains`. Phase M's M0 says "after Save files (v2.4)" too; sections are found by name, so whichever lands second sits next to the first (file order v2.4, v2.5, v2.4.1 is fine). Phase M's agents may not touch `sim/water.py` and Phase N's may not touch `sim/repair.py` or `sim/objects.py`; the files both phases edit (`simulation.py`, `Renderer.java`, `Model.java`, `ViewerState.java`, `FakeSim.java`, `DebugScreen.java`, `Main.java`, `Shared.java`) are edited by Phase N now and by Phase M after it, so Phase M's "designed against the code Phase L leaves behind" gets Phase N's code as well, and its MS0 / MV0 baselines are taken then.

> **Parallelism and file ownership.** Two implementers and the orchestrator; **NS and NV share no file** and never need to talk: the only coupling is the v2.4.1 block below (moved into `PROTOCOL.md` by N0) and the model table. NV proves itself on `FakeSim` + `FakeWater` and on a hand-written replay; NS proves itself headless and through the protocol schema test.
> * **NS — Sim flow agent** (`python-tooling-dev`): owns `sim/water.py` (the street without a curb, the film, drains on the film, the pool that covers a street, flood states, the wire lists, the save block), `sim/cars.py` (additive: `depth_at` also reads the film), `sim/tests/test_water.py` (the Phase L tests it supersedes are rewritten, not deleted), `tools/measure_flow.py` and `tools/record_flood_replay.py` (new), `tools/debug_plots/v241/` (new), and small edits to `sim/simulation.py` (`hello_message`, `_build_frame`, `to_state` / `from_state` pass-through, the `drains` command answer), `sim/tests/_schema.py`, `test_protocol_schema.py`, `test_saveload.py`, `test_cars.py` (additive), `test_perf.py`. **May not touch** `sim/objects.py`, `settlements.py`, `terrain.py`, `particles.py`, `viewer/`, `PROTOCOL.md`, `PLAN.md`, `saves/`, `config/`, `scrap/`.
> * **NV — Viewer flow agent** (`general-purpose`): owns `FlowPainter.java`, `FlowTest.java` (new), the new folder `viewer/snapshots/v241/`, and edits to `WaterPainter.java` (the end ramp and the dashed trickle are bypassed when `hello.flow` is present), `FakeWater.java` (the street model is rewritten), `DrainTest.java` (the checks that assert the curb or the flat-street vector are rewritten, recorded in place), `Model.java`, `ViewerState.java`, `Shared.java`, `Renderer.java` (`prepareShared`, one paint call next to the water pass), `FakeSim.java` (flow in `emit`, the `flood` scene terrain), `DebugFlood.java`, `UiSnapshot.java`, `Main.java` (one added test call). **May not touch** `sim/`, `PROTOCOL.md`, `PLAN.md`, `saves/`, `config/`, `scrap/`, `build.bat`. Each edit of a shared file is small and made after re-reading the spot (Phase I's rule).
> * **Orchestrator:** N0 (the contract) before either starts; NI (integration) after both finish. Each agent compiles into its own scratch folder (`javac -d`).

**What each part does**
| Part | Role |
|---|---|
| `PROTOCOL.md` | v2.4.1 contract: `hello.flow`, `frame.flow` (runs of cells with film depth `h` and flux `q`), `hello.drains` (`curb` 0, `overflow_depth`), `frame.flood` states by real water depth (and `backed`), the save rule, old streams |
| `sim/water.py` (NS) | The street as ordinary ground (a hollow only if the terrain makes it one), the film of water on the ground (cells, speed from slope, sink rule, rain on dry ground, drains acting on it), a pool that covers a street, flood states from real depth, the wire lists, save and load |
| `sim/cars.py`, `simulation.py`, tests (NS) | Cars slow in the film as in a body; the new keys through `hello` and the frame, the save block, the schema and send-rule tests, the superseded Phase L tests rewritten |
| `FlowPainter.java` (new, NV) | The stream and the street's sheet: a ribbon along the terrain with the sim's thickness, a moving surface, white water and spray on steep ground, a cliff fall behind a threshold |
| `WaterPainter.java` (NV) | A body's surface ends exactly where its level meets the ground (no ramp, no kerb) on a v2.4.1 stream, and joins the stream there |
| `FakeWater.java`, `FakeSim.java` (NV) | A Java mirror of the street without a curb and of the film so the viewer and Debug run without Python; the fake `flood` scene gets a hilltop town, a town beside a hollow and a falling road |
| `DebugFlood.java` (NV) | The existing Flood tab gains a slope control, flow numbers and an overlay; its curb marker goes |
| `Model.java`, `ViewerState.java`, `Shared.java`, `Renderer.java` (NV) | Parse and keep `flow`, eased per cell, computed once per frame by renderer 0 |

### What the current build shows (measured and pictured while planning; scratch scripts, to be redone in NS0 and NV0)
Everything below was run **read-only against the real sim and a scratch `javac` of the current `viewer/src`** (197 classes, 11 s) into the scratch folder `C:\Users\lenard\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\D--Lenard-Projects-Precipitation\d44c332b-de32-4f8d-ba42-1383778e62fd\scratchpad\n` (`pic\` for pictures; `spill_probe.py`, `record_storm.py`, `film_probe.py`, `census.py`, `census2.py` for the numbers; scratch `--config-dir` / `--saves-dir`, nothing under `viewer\out`, `saves/` or `config/`). A pinned supercell over the city of seed 12 (rolling, small drains) was recorded as a `--replay` file (every third frame, 160-215 s, midday held) so frames of the overflow could be opened.
* **"Droplets" (the user's complaint): `pic\c1_trickle_crop.png`** (a 4x crop of `pic\r1_end_f100.png`, replay frame 100, t = 170 s, `--cam 2830,930,2.4`): beyond the city's right end the overflow is a **dashed, pale line of short 5 px dashes 1.5 px thick** that follows the road for 64 px and stops (`WaterPainter.trickle`, `TRICKLE_PX` 64): the same size whatever the flood. At zoom 1 it is invisible (`pic\n4_city_t185.png`, `pic\n3_end_t185.png`, the real unpinned run).
* **Not level (1), the street end: `pic\r1_end_f100.png`, `pic\r2_end_f200.png`**: the street is full to its virtual curb (surface y 924.07, depth 9.99, `spill` true) and its water **stops in a straight cut at the pad's edge** with the ground falling away beside it (the pad end is y 934.0; the water stands 10 px above it, held by nothing visible). `pic\f3_vend_t62.png` (fake `flood` scene, village end, t = 62 s, zoom 4) shows the end drawn as a ramp (`RAMP` 1.6) over the road.
* **Not level (2), two bodies: `pic\f3_vend_t62.png`**: the village's surface (a thin white line) and a pool to its west stand about 6 px apart in height with about 38 px of dry ground between them and nothing linking them. In the real run (`pic\r1_end_f100.png`, `pic\r3_pool_f100.png`) the street's surface is y 924 and the receiving pool's is y 969-970 (45 px lower), 225 px away; water that goes over the curb **appears in the pool in the same sub-step** (`_settle` adds the excess to the target body at once).
* **Not level (3), arrival**: the receiving pool grew from 9 px to 63 px wide in 1.5 s of replay time (x 2958-2967 at t = 169.0 to x 2947-3010 at t = 170.7) and rose 1.4 px in 1.7 s, while its source is 225 px away: water appears there rather than arrives.
* **Not level (4), the town in the way**: of the hollows in 8 seeds x 4 terrains, **the lower rim faces a street across it in 14 of 28 (rolling), 8 of 24 (hilly), 10 of 22 (mountainous) and 8 of 16 (flat)**; `_target` uses `non_street`, so a full hollow hands its water to the far side of the town (also 3 of 22 / 3 of 23 street spills "past-town"). **Not seen in a picture; census only.**
* **The street's rim ignores the real divide**: `e_s = min(e0, e1) + CURB` (10 px); in 12 of 88 streets (rolling 4 of 22, hilly 4 of 23, mountainous 4 of 21; flat 0 of 22) the divide on the spill side is more than 0.5 px above the curb top (worst +21.5, +88 and +233 px), so the water leaves "uphill". The ground beyond a street's end: median run to the next rise 68 px (0 to 248), median fall 7.2 px, mean slope median 0.10 (10th percentile 0, 90th 0.94).
* **How much overflow there is (real sim, 330 s pinned storm over the biggest town, `HIT_AREA` 1.0 at the time):** supercell over **small** drains (cap 33.6 px²/s, a city of 814 px): overflow for **52 s** (seed 12; 66 s on the hilly seed), total 1 785 px², **mean 34 px²/s**, busiest 3-tick step **127 px²/s** (164 hilly); the ground beyond the curb is 112 px long, falls 31.9 px (slope 0.29, steepest 0.59), 120 px and 112.5 px (0.94, steepest 1.98) on the hilly seed. Cumulonimbus over small drains (peak 6.05 px), supercell over **normal** drains (0.07 px) and a second city (seed 11, peak 9.8 px) did **not** overflow. So an overflow stream exists only in a big storm over a small-drained town; that is why runoff from hillsides is in the plan (open question 76).
* **The film prototype** (a 32 px cell, `u = 23 sqrt(slope) h^(2/3)`, fed by the real field's overflow and by the rain on the slopes; never fed back into the field): the first cell beyond the curb held **2.06, 2.08, 2.55, 1.93 px** of water at 175-193 s (the formula for q = 34 on slope 0.22 gives 1.99), the water reached the receiving hollow within about 6 s (sampled every 6 s), and with the sink only at the bed cell a **4.8 px film piled on the slope-floor shelf** in front of the hollow (open question 80). Rain alone on a slope under a steady rain made 0.96-1.5 px on slopes of 0.22 and 0.54 and up to 4.5 px on the flat shelf.
* **The user's screenshot (2026-10-11, night, a town's right edge)** shows the same thing as `pic\r1_end_f100.png`: a flat pad with a level body of water that ends in a slanted ramp at the pad's edge and a dry road falling away beyond it. It is the case the plan must fix first: NV0 reproduces it (the first picture) and NV8 shows it after the change, with the water running **down the road and pooling at the bottom** and nothing standing on the pad above it.
* **A street as a sheet on its own pad (prototype, `pad.py` in the scratch folder; `KU` 23, slope floor 0.02, no curb, drains spread evenly over the span, 240 s of constant rain then dry; a flat street of 814 px, rain per px from the measured under-cloud rates at `HIT_AREA` 1.15: 0.157 / 0.208 / 0.294 px²/s per px, drains per px 0.040 / 0.098 / 0.299 for small / normal / large):**
| rain | drains | depth at the low end, peak | mean depth over the street | seconds to 3 px | seconds to dry after the rain |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| steady (nimbostratus) | small / normal / large | 7.5 / 5.0 / none px | 4.8 / 3.2 / - | 26 / 51 / - | 82 / 38 / - |
| downpour (cumulonimbus) | small / normal / large | 9.3 / 7.2 / none px | 6.0 / 4.6 / - | 18 / 28 / - | 87 / 47 / - |
| supercell | small / normal / large | 11.9 / 10.2 / none px | 7.6 / 6.5 / - | 12 / 16 / - | 91 / 54 / - |
  The sheet reaches 90 % of its peak in 43-78 s, so a storm of 80-180 s is mostly at its peak. Large drains take all of every rain class (0.299 px²/s per px against 0.294), small and normal drains do not. **At the starting constants a flat town floods (3 px) in every storm that exceeds its drains, including steady rain with normal drains, which the Phase L contract C2 forbids**; with a rougher street surface (`KU` 58 instead of 23, depth scales as `KU^-0.6`, factor 0.575) the peaks are 4.3 / 2.9 px (steady), 5.3 / 4.1 (downpour), 6.8 / 5.9 (supercell) for small / normal, which is near C2 for normal drains (2.9 px: ponding, C2 asks for `wet`) and C4 for normal (5.9 px: flooded) but misses C3 for normal drains (4.1 px: flooded, C3 asks for ponding at most) and C4 for small drains (6.8 px, below an `overflow_depth` of 8). So the constants cannot be taken from this table: NS6 fits them (open question 89).
* **Census of what holds or backs up water (88 settlements, 8 seeds x 4 terrains, `census3.py`):** rolling 22 = 9 shed, 2 hold on their pad, 11 backed; hilly 23 = 13 shed, 2 held, 5 backed, 3 both; mountainous 21 = 10 shed, 2 held, 7 backed, 2 both; flat 22 = 13 shed, 2 held, 6 backed, 1 both; "backed" means a hollow beside the town whose lower rim faces it. 45 shed, 8 held, 29 backed, 6 both.
**Not measured:** any effect of the film on the real field (conservation, save / load, the Phase L tests), the cost in the sim, the cost of drawing a stream, whether a storm of the measured length fills the hollows that can back up into a town, and any picture of a stream (there is none: the build has only the dashed line).

### The model in numbers (the one table both implementers build from) — `[REVISED]` 2026-10-11
**Why revised:** the first version of this table kept Phase L's virtual curb and added a weir store above it; the user's answer (above) forbids water standing above lower ground, so the curb, the weir (`CW`, `V_over`, `head`, `over`) and the old "spill over the lower end at `min(e0, e1) + CURB`" are **removed**, and a street's flood is the film itself. The weir was also unnecessary as a smoother: the film cell is a reservoir with a time constant of about `32 / u` = 2 s, which averages the 3-tick hit noise (measured: busiest 3-tick spill 127 px²/s against a mean of 34).

Water is area in the 2-D world, px². Every constant lives in `sim/water.py` (one place, the Phase L block extended), is mirrored in one commented block of `FakeWater.java`, and is **a starting value from planning arithmetic that NS6 re-tunes by measurement**. Phase L's constants are quoted as of 2026-10-11 10:25 (`HIT_AREA` 1.15, `BASIN_MIN_DEPTH` 1.0, `SOAK` 0.015, `FLOOD_DEPTH` 3, `INLET_K` 0.2667, `INLET_SPACING` 60, `SUBSTEP` 0.25, `PROCESS_EVERY` 3, `GROUND_STEP` 32) and **NS0 and NV0 re-read them**.
| Name | Start | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| Cell | 32 px (`GROUND_STEP`), cell `k` centred at `x = 32 k` (the rain bucket `k`) | The film is one volume `S` (px²) per cell, depth `h = S / 32`; only cells with water are kept |
| Where a cell's rain goes | A cell under a body's surface: straight to the body. **Any other cell, a street span included: into its own `S`** | Phase L sent the rain on a street span into a street body; that is gone |
| A street span | Ordinary ground with `sid` marks on its cells (for the drains and the flood report). `TerrainMap` treats it like a shallow basin: if `min(divide left, divide right) - bed < BASIN_MIN_DEPTH` (1 px) it is **not a hollow** (it dissolves into the neighbour across its lower divide); otherwise it is a hollow whose rim is that lower divide. **`CURB` is 0.** | The census (hold counted at 3 px): 14 of 88 settlements are hollows, 74 are not; at the 1 px rule NS0 recounts. A pad's median hold is 0.08 px |
| Direction | Toward the element's bed as in Phase L; on a street pad: toward its **lower end**, a tie toward the side whose neighbouring hollow has the lower bed, a second tie to the left | Rain on a town's slopes runs away from the town **down to the lowest ground**, or into it when it is the lowest ground |
| Speed and flux | `slope = max(SLOPE_MIN, (y_next - y) / 32 x dir)`, `u = KU x sqrt(slope) x h^(2/3)`, `q = u x h`; `KU` 23, `KU_STREET` 23 (start; the same law, a separate constant for the street's rougher surface), `SLOPE_MIN` 0.02 | `q` is px²/s leaving the cell. A cell passes `min(S, q x dt)` to the next cell each sub-step (explicit, safe while `u x dt / 32 <= 1`, i.e. `u <= 128` px/s at 0.25 s) |
| Steady thickness (**vector for the film**) | `h = (q / (KU sqrt(slope)))^0.6` and `u = q / h` | q = 5 / 34 / 127 px²/s on slope 0.22: `h` 0.63 / 1.99 / 4.39 px, `u` 7.9 / 17.1 / 28.9 px/s; q = 34 on slope 0.05 / 0.10 / 0.60 / 1.0: `h` 3.11 / 2.52 / 1.47 / 1.26 px. Doubling q multiplies `h` by 1.52 |
| **Vector for a street as a sheet** | A flat pad of `L` = 814 px, net excess (rain minus drains minus soak) `e` = 0.1 px²/s per px, steady: `q(x) = e x`, `h(x) = (e x / (KU_STREET sqrt(SLOPE_MIN)))^0.6` | At the low end q = 81.4, `h` = **6.90 px**; at 407 px 4.55 px, at 203 px 3.0 px; mean depth 4.30 px; stored 3 502 px²; the sheet is within 10 % of it after about 60 s (prototype: 43-78 s to 90 % of the peak) |
| Drains | Capacity `cap = inlets x INLET_K x size²` as in Phase L, now **taken from the water on the street**: each cell of the span loses `min(S, cap x 32 / span x dt)`; a body that covers the span loses `INLET_K x size²` for each inlet (position `x0 + (i + 0.5)(x1 - x0)/inlets` as `hello.drains` says) whose `x` is under its surface | Size still decides: 0.040 / 0.098 / 0.299 px²/s per px for the three classes against the rain's 0.157 / 0.208 / 0.294 |
| Sink | A cell is a sink when its ground is at or below the receiving body's level **or** within `BASIN_MIN_DEPTH` (1 px) of that hollow's bed; a pond, the world edge are sinks; the arriving volume is added to the body (a pond absorbs it, an edge loses it) | The flat shelf of the census piled 4.8 px with a sink at the bed cell only |
| A body | As Phase L (`Profile`, exact level, soak `SOAK`, merge, split) **with no curb and no head over the rim**: it clamps at its real rim and the excess enters the first cell beyond it. A pool whose level reaches a street span covers it; the merged body keeps the pool's id and its `frame.flood[].backed` is true | "If there's more land it will go there" |
| `FILM_SOAK` | 0 (start) per wet cell; raise toward `SOAK x 32` = 0.48 px²/s if a film outlives its rain by more than 30 s | Left at 0 so the arithmetic above is the model |
| `depth` of a settlement | The greatest water depth on its span: the thickest film cell, or the body depth at its lowest point | The v2.4 sentence "px of water over the street's lowest point" is the same thing on a body |
| States | `dry` (no water, inflow < 0.01), `wet` (rain falling, no water of 0.05 px or more), `ponding` (`0.05 <= depth < FLOOD_DEPTH` 3), `flooded` (`FLOOD_DEPTH <= depth < OVERFLOW_DEPTH` 8), `overflowing` (`depth >= OVERFLOW_DEPTH`, or a body that covers the span is itself running over its rim). "The city floods" = `flooded` or `overflowing` | `curb` is gone from the definition; `OVERFLOW_DEPTH` is the planner's (open question 87) |
| `inflow` | px²/s entering the span (rain on it, film arriving from upslope, a hollow's spill), 2 s mean | Phase L's was the rain on the span only |
| Cars | Depth at a car's `x` = the body's depth there, or the film's depth interpolated between cell centres; stall depth 1.8 x wheel radius as Phase L | `cars.py` reads the film |
| Wire thresholds | `h` sent in 0.05 px steps, cells under 0.05 px not sent; the viewer draws a cell only above `MIN_H_DRAW` 0.15 px | The thin runoff (0.2-1.5 px) shows, a damp film does not |
| Drawing (viewer-only numbers, the planner's) | White water where `slope >= 0.6` or `u >= 30` px/s; cliff fall where `slope >= CLIFF_SLOPE` 2.0 (behind a switch, open question 74); ribbon top edge `h x sqrt(1 + slope²)` above the ground; easing of `h` 0.12 s, a new run fades in over 0.3 s | They are not on the wire; only `h`, `q` and the ids are |

**What Phase L's contract becomes (explicit; the first version of Phase N said "does not move", which the curb's removal makes impossible).** The Phase L tests and vectors that **are superseded**, and what replaces each:
| Phase L item | Fate | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| The flat-street test vector (`d(t) = d0 + (Q/s - cap/s - SOAK) t` while `0 < d < CURB`; s = 400 px, size 4.7, 7 inlets, `cap` 41.2 px²/s, Q = 100: 0.132 px/s, 3 px after 22.7 s, rim at 10 px after 75.8 s; Q = 0: falls 0.118 px/s, 5 px clears in 42 s), in `test_water.py` and `DrainTest.java` / `FakeWater` | **Superseded as a property of every street; kept as a fixture:** a built heightmap whose pad has real 10 px walls (a hollow of 10 px) is a held street body with rim 10, so the numbers are **unchanged to 1 %** (recomputed from the constants if NS6 changes them) | the same numbers on the walled-pad fixture, plus the sheet vector above for a pad without walls |
| C1-C4 and the LS6 table (rain class x drain class -> peak state, depth, seconds to `flooded`) | **Superseded**: they were defined on a body held by a wall | C1'-C4' below, on the sheet depth, fitted in NS6 |
| C5 (a 5 px flood under normal drains clears within 120 s after the rain; a 1.5 px natural puddle forms within 30 s and is dry within 120 s) | Split: the street half is **superseded** (a 5 px sheet, prototype: 38-91 s to dry), the natural-puddle half **holds unedited** | C5' street half; the puddle half as it is |
| C6 (an unlimited natural pool, no width cap) | **Holds unedited** | - |
| "A street body is always present", "a street never merges with the hollow beside it", "a full hollow hands its water to the far side of the town" (`_target`, `non_street`), "`spill` at `max(y(x0), y(x1)) - CURB`", `overflowing` at `depth >= curb - 0.05`, `hello.drains.curb` = 10, the curb rows of `_schema.py` and `test_protocol_schema.py`, `test_water.py`'s end-to-end small-against-large check | **Superseded** (27 + 8 + 2 + 4 mentions of `curb` / `spill` / `overflowing` in `test_water.py`, `_schema.py`, `test_protocol_schema.py`, `test_saveload.py`, found by grepping the test folder for curb, overflowing and spill; and in the viewer `DrainTest.java` (the vector and the `hello` check), `FakeWater.java`, `DebugFlood.java` (the curb marker), `WaterPainter.java` (`RAMP`)) | rewritten to the definitions above, each test named in the session by NS0 / NV0 |
| `TestStandingWater` in `test_cars.py` (7 cases) | **Holds unedited** for bodies; film cases are added | `depth_at` reads the film |

Phase L properties that **must still hold unedited**: conservation of water (rain = stored + film + soaked + drained + absorbed + lost, within 0.1 %; the ledger keys are extended, none removed); `PLAIN_WORLD_HASHES` and the seeded stream (nothing here draws a random number, never the world's `random.Random`); the drain sizes (`size_for`, kind ranges, classes, `inlets_for`, `capacity`) and the `drains` command and `--drains`; save / load frame identity mid-flood; old saves load (a v2.4 `water` block loads, its street bodies' water is put on the street as film); determinism; `hello.water`; showcase, hurricane and world mode carry none of it; the legacy `puddle` objects.

**The tuning contract (NS6 measures; none of these may be met by a test that only checks "a flow exists")** — the failure fingerprint of this project is a test that asserts an event but not its size or duration (`.stacker/state.json`):
* N1 the film's steady thickness equals the vector above to 5 % for q = 5 / 34 / 127 on slopes 0.05-1.0 on a uniform test slope, and doubling the flux raises `h` by 1.52 +- 5 %;
* N2 the street sheet: the flat 814 px pad of the vector gives 6.90 px at its low end, 4.30 px mean and 3 502 px² stored to 5 % at steady state, and the walled-pad fixture reproduces Phase L's vector to 1 %;
* N3 on the 112 px run beyond seed 12's city (slope 0.29) the first water of an overflow reaches the receiving hollow between **3 and 9 s** after it enters the first cell (kinematic estimate 4-6 s), and a thin overflow (q = 5) takes longer than a thick one (q = 127);
* N4 conservation (above), and a film left on the ground after the rain stops is gone within 120 s;
* N5 the **level rule**, tested on every body and cell of the 32-world census after storms: the ground just outside a body's `[x0, x1]` is above its level (or it is a world edge), so **no water stands above lower ground**; no film cell lies under a body; a film never runs uphill (no cell whose next cell in its direction is higher than the ground it enters, other than into a sink); no cell with `slope < 0.05` holds more than 1.5 px at rest (the prototype's shelf held 4.8 px);
* N6 a street's flood is real: on the seed-12 city, supercell over **small** drains gives `depth >= 3 px` at the low end within the cloud's rain and `large` drains give none (the user's earlier request, still true), and **no water stands on a pad that has lower ground beside it** (the pad's `depth` is the sheet's, zero after it has run off);
* N7 a hollow that can back up into a town (the 35 of 88) fills in a long storm, covers the pad, and the town's state reads `flooded` with `backed` true; the water surface is one level over hollow and street; the submerged inlets drain it;
* N8 the flux at the third cell beyond a rim over a pinned supercell on seed 12 has a 2 s mean whose maximum is at most 1.5 x its mean (the old per-step spill was 3.7 x: 127 against 34);
* C1' drizzle (a raining cloud with `precip_rate` under 0.05): no class floods; C2' steady rain: `large` dry, `normal` at most ponding, `small` may flood only after 45 s of its peak rain; C3' downpour: `small` floods within the cloud's rain, `normal` ponds at most, `large` dry; C4' supercell: `small` reaches `overflowing`, `normal` reaches `flooded`, `large` never floods (merged storms measured and reported, not forced); C5' a 5 px sheet under `normal` drains clears within 120 s of the rain's end; C6' the natural pool and puddle contracts as Phase L's C5 (puddle half) and C6. **The arithmetic above says these cannot all be met by `KU_STREET` alone** (open question 89): NS6 fits `KU_STREET`, `SLOPE_MIN` for pads, `FLOOD_DEPTH` and `OVERFLOW_DEPTH` and, if the contract cannot be met, records the table and which row misses instead of tuning the tests.

### Protocol v2.4.1 additions (paste-ready; N0 moves this block into `PROTOCOL.md` unchanged, as its own section immediately after "Save files (v2.4)")
````markdown
# v2.4.1 additions (additive; `version` stays 2)

v2.4's standing water is amended: there is **no virtual curb**. Water never stands above lower ground; a street is
ordinary ground with drains. Water on the ground runs downhill as a film to the lowest ground it can reach, fills
that hollow to a level surface that spreads over whatever land its level reaches (a street included), and spills
onward when the hollow is full. Everything here is switched on by `hello.flow`: a stream without it is a v2.4
stream and means what v2.4 says. Every key is optional: a v2.4 viewer ignores the new ones (it draws the bodies
that exist and its own trickle, and no sheet of water on a street), and a v2.4.1 viewer on a v2.4 stream or save
draws what it did before. **Sandbox only** (with `hello.water`).

## Overland flow
`hello.flow` (present only when the sim carries the film):
```json
"flow":{"model":1,"cell":32,"min_h":0.05}
```
`frame.flow` (optional; the **complete** list of runs, replacing the previous list; `[]` = no flow). Sent on the first
frame after a `hello` or a load, then only when a run appeared, vanished, or a cell's `h` moved by 0.1 px or its `q`
by 10 %, at most every 3rd frame; once everything is dry it is sent one last time as `[]` and then not again.
```json
"flow":[{"k0":87,"dir":1,"src":3,"dst":4,"h":[2.05,2.1,1.9,1.4,0.9],"q":[36.0,35.2,34.8,30.1,22.0]},
        {"k0":22,"dir":-1,"src":0,"dst":2,"h":[0.35,0.55,0.8],"q":[2.1,4.0,6.3]}]
```
* A run is a chain of neighbouring cells, **downhill**: cell `i` of the run has its centre at `x = 32 x (k0 + dir x i)`,
  `h[i]` is the depth of the film there (px, steps of 0.05), `q[i]` the flux leaving it (px^2/s, one decimal); the
  speed the viewer shows is `q / h`. A cell is in at most one run; a run has at most 40 cells and is cut where a body
  begins. **A street's own sheet is part of a run** (its cells lie inside the settlement's span).
* `src` - the id of the body that feeds the run's first cell, or the **settlement `id`** of the street the run starts
  on, `0` = the rain on the slope only. `dst` - the id of the body the last cell runs into, `-1` = off a world edge,
  `-2` = into a pond (absorbed).
* **How the viewer draws it:** a ribbon along `hello.world.terrain` over the cells, its top `h` above the ground
  (times `sqrt(1 + slope^2)`), eased per cell (about 0.12 s), a new run fading in (about 0.3 s); never above the
  ground by more than `h`, beyond the run, or inside a body's water.

## Standing water and flooding without a curb
* `hello.drains.curb` is **0.0** on a stream with `hello.flow` (a viewer that ignores the new keys reads 0 and draws
  no kerb or ramp); `flood_depth` stays 3.0; new `overflow_depth` (default 8.0).
* A body's ends are where its level meets the ground (`x0`, `x1`) and the ground just outside is higher; there is no
  ramp and no wall. `frame.water` lists a `street` body (id = its settlement's `id`) **only while the street itself is
  a hollow** that holds water; a pool whose level reaches a street covers it and is listed as a `pool`. `spill` is true
  while a body runs over its real rim.
* `frame.flood[]` entries (the complete list, as v2.4) have the same keys, with new meanings: `depth` - the greatest
  water depth on the settlement's span (the thickest film cell, or the body's depth at its lowest point); `inflow` -
  px^2/s entering the span (rain, film from upslope, a hollow's spill), 2 s mean; `state` - `dry`, `wet`, `ponding`
  (`0.05 <= depth < flood_depth`), `flooded` (`flood_depth <= depth < overflow_depth`), `overflowing`
  (`depth >= overflow_depth`, or the body that covers the span is running over its rim); optional `backed` (default
  false) - true while a body that came from a hollow covers part of the span. "The city floods" = `flooded` or
  `overflowing`; a town on high ground has no hollow to hold water and floods only as a sheet running off its
  street, when its drains are exceeded.
* `cars[].depth` is the depth of the body or of the film at the car's `x`.

## Save files (v2.4.1)
The `water` block gains `film`: `[[k, S], ...]` (the cells that hold water). A v2.4 `water` block loads: the water
its street bodies held is put on the street as film. A state without `film` loads with a dry ground; an older sim
ignores the key.
````

### How it is built (the sim and the viewer, in short)
* **Sim.** Phase L's `TerrainMap` already cuts the ground at its divides into elements and dissolves a hollow shallower than `BASIN_MIN_DEPTH`; Phase N applies the same rule to a street span (no hollow of its own: it dissolves into its neighbour) and removes the curb. Rain on a dry cell goes into the film; the film runs downhill cell by cell and ends in a body (a sink) or off the edge; the drains take water from the street's cells (or from a body that covers the street). A body clamps at its **real** rim and passes the excess into the first cell beyond. Everything else in `WaterField` (the basin map, `Profile`, soak, merge, split, the 2 s inflow window) is kept; the merge rule that joined only pool-with-pool now also joins a pool with a street that is a hollow and covers a street that is not.
* **Viewer.** `WaterPainter.prepare` already eases each body once per frame in `Shared.water`; `FlowPainter` does the same for the runs into `Shared.flow` (renderer 0, never read live by a band: Phase J's lesson), and `paint` draws the ribbons right after the bodies, before the lightning. The ribbon is a live pass (it changes every frame), so nothing of it is in a baked tile. The cost budget is about +0.3 ms a frame with six runs in view at zoom 1-2.

Done when: all four are in the session and (b) agrees with "What the current build shows" or the difference is written down.

2. **[REVISED] NS1 — The street without a curb (`sim/water.py`).** *Was:* a weir store above the curb (`CW`, `V_over`, `head`, `over`). *Revised 2026-10-11, reason:* the user's answer forbids water standing above lower ground, so there is no curb to hold anything and no head over a rim; the film cell's own time constant (about 2 s) smooths the spill. Now: `TerrainMap` treats a street span as ordinary ground: it keeps the `sid` marks of its cells (drains, flood report, inlet positions) and dissolves it, like a hollow shallower than `BASIN_MIN_DEPTH`, into the neighbour across its lower divide unless `min(divide left, divide right) - bed >= BASIN_MIN_DEPTH`, in which case it is a hollow whose rim is that lower divide; `CURB` is 0 (the constant stays until NS7 has removed its uses); a `WaterField` no longer creates a street body for every settlement (`sbody` only for a street that is a hollow); a body clamps at its **real** rim and its excess goes into the first cell beyond. Done when: on the 32-world census no body's end has lower ground just outside it (N5), the walled-pad fixture reproduces Phase L's vector to 1 % (N2), the held street's rim equals the lower divide, and conservation holds (N4) with the film not yet there (the excess is passed on as before, for this step only).

3. **[REVISED] NS2 — The film, on the ground and on the street.** *Revised:* the first version fed the film only from a body's spill and from rain on slopes; now the rain on a street span also lands in the film. In `water.py` add `Film`: cells of 32 px along the world, direction and sink flags derived from the `TerrainMap` (static; a pad's direction toward its lower end, a tie toward the lower neighbouring bed, then left), `S` per cell, the sub-step of the model table (explicit, `min(S, q x dt)`, clamp `u`), `KU` / `KU_STREET`, the sink rule, the entry of rain on dry cells (a street span included) and of a body's excess into the first cell beyond its rim, the drains acting on the cells of the span (`cap x 32 / span` per cell), and the arrival at a sink added to the receiving body (a pond absorbs, an edge loses). Active cells only are visited. Done when: the film vector holds to 5 % (N1), the sheet vector holds to 5 % (N2), N3 holds on seed 12's 112 px run (3-9 s), the flat shelf stays under 1.5 px (N5), conservation holds with the film as a stored term (N4), and a film left after the rain is gone within 120 s.

4. **[REVISED] NS3 — The wire and the flood states.** *Revised:* no `over` / `q` on `frame.water`; the flood is read from the film. `hello_flow()`, `flow_list()` (runs by walking downhill from each source and from each street, cells under 0.05 px dropped, a run cut where a body begins, at most 40 cells), `emit()` with the send rule of the v2.4.1 block, `hello_drains()` with `curb` 0.0 and `overflow_depth`, `flood_list()` with `depth` = the greatest depth on the span, `inflow` including film and spill arriving, the new `state` definitions and `backed`, and `water_list()` listing a `street` body only while the street is a hollow that holds water. Done when: `_schema.py` accepts every optional v2.4.1 key and rejects a wrong type, showcase / hurricane / world frames carry none, the state words follow the depth bands of the model table at 0.05 / 3 / 8 px (a table test), and the frame is at most about 0.5 kB larger with six runs than without (measured).

5. **[REVISED] NS4 — Cars, wiring, save, load and old saves.** *Revised:* cars now read the film. `cars.py`: `depth_at(x)` returns the body's depth or the film's depth interpolated between cell centres, the existing `TestStandingWater` cases unedited; `simulation.py`: `hello_message` adds `flow` next to `water` and `drains` (a copy, as Phase L), `_build_frame` adds `flow`, `to_state` / `from_state` carry `film` and the bodies' volumes; a v2.4 `water` block loads (a held street's water becomes film on the street), a state without `film` loads dry. Done when: run N -> save -> load -> run M equals an uninterrupted N+M run frame for frame **while a street is a flowing sheet and a pool is spilling onward**; a v2.4 state written by the code of NS0 loads and continues; a car in a 6 px sheet slows and stops as in a body of 6 px; `--headless-frames 3` prints a `hello` with `flow`.

6. **[REVISED] NS5 — A hollow covers the street beside it; "more land, it goes there" (ON).** *Was:* "the street's rim at the real divide" (default applied) — now part of NS1, since the curb is gone. *Revised 2026-10-11, reason:* the user answered that a rising body spreads onto whatever land its level reaches, a town's street included, and that overflow goes on to the next lower ground. In `water.py`: a pool whose level reaches a street span covers it (the street's cells are part of the pool's profile), a street that is a hollow merges with a full pool across the rim they share (the larger keeps its id; the merged body's `backed` is true), `_target` no longer uses `non_street`, a full hollow with a street across its lower rim fills the street and, when the street's ground is the pad of a shedding town, the water runs on over the far side as film. Submerged inlets drain the body. Done when: N7 holds on the census worlds (the 35 of 88 towns that can be backed up), the water surface is one level over hollow and street in a picture-checked test, a shedding town (45 of 88) never shows standing water on its pad (N6), and conservation holds.

Run the planning probes against the built field: stream thickness and delay for a just-overtopping village (q = 2-10 px²/s: `h` 0.36-0.96 px on slope 0.22) and for the supercell city (q = 34, peaks 127: 1.99 and 4.39 px), and the street sheet for the three rain classes x three drain classes (the prototype table at `KU` 23: 5.0 px normal in steady rain, 10.2 under a supercell), over at least eight seeds and the four terrain kinds, the three rain classes of LS6, and fit `KU`, `KU_STREET`, `SLOPE_MIN`, `FILM_SOAK`, the sink width, `FLOOD_DEPTH` and `OVERFLOW_DEPTH` to the contract. Record a table in this file (Phase N verification): rain class x drain class x terrain -> peak state, peak depth at the low end, seconds to 3 px, seconds to dry, and the stream's thickness at the first cell beyond the street. If a constant leaves its start value change it in `sim/water.py` only and recompute the vectors. If the contract cannot be met (the arithmetic says it cannot with one constant, open question 89) say so with the table and which row misses; do not tune the tests to the numbers. Time 900 headless frames at 3 840 x 1 080 in a rain scene with a street sheeting, old against new in isolation (the known `test_perf` tornado timing failure is compared old against new before it is blamed). Done when: `python -m unittest discover sim/tests` runs, the properties listed as "must still hold unedited" are green without an edit, 30 ticks/s holds, the film step costs at most about 0.1 ms a tick on average (only active cells; if more, process every 6th tick), and each rewritten test is listed in the session with what replaced it.

## Phase O — Houses with people, buildings that come apart and fall back (viewer only; no protocol change)

**Project context:** `[UPDATE]` — extends the build with Phases H–N (the whole project was read for this phase: Python 3.11 stdlib sim in `sim/`, a plain-`javac` Java2D viewer of about 70 files in `viewer/src/precip`, JDK 25, Windows 11, no git, desktop icon `C:\Users\lenard\Desktop\Precipitation.lnk` -> `run.bat`). **Status 2026-10-12 `[UPDATE]`: built, `[PARTIAL]`** (OT, OH, OW built and green in `--selftest` at `-Xmx2200m`; OC done in the sim, so "no `sim/` file is edited" below holds for the viewer parts only; open items in "Left open"). Designed against the code of 2026-10-11: Phase M is **planned and unbuilt**; Phase N's files are not touched. **Viewer only**: no `PROTOCOL.md` change and no file of `sim/` is edited (see "Protocol and sim"). SA-Asker session `phase-o-plan-houses-with-people-collapse-20261011-182828` (five advisory questions, defaults applied, open questions 99-103).

**The user's request (2026-10-11, verbatim):** "I want you to change the Way Trees and houses look. Make them More Realistic with individual leaves you can see and for the houses you can see each and everydetail like people inside the houses as shadows people going on walks and people looking out the porches and backyards also. and make broken houses not have floating blocks in the air in one of their stages it should not be there and make the houses and buildings visually fall or collapse or swept away and once the tornado is gone it will fall back down with damaged roads and piles of rubble. also make the way clouds spawn more realistic there are so many cumulonimbuses at night"

| Reading (the orchestrator's, not confirmed by the user) | Part |
|---|---|
| Trees with individual leaves | **OT**, dispatched, not planned here |
| Fewer cumulonimbus at night; clouds spawn as by day | **OC**, dispatched, not planned here |
| Houses with every detail (siding, roof tiles, gutters, door furniture, framed windows with curtains, porches, fences, backyard props) and **people**: shadows moving behind lit windows, people on porches and in backyards, walkers in the street | **OH** |
| A broken building never has a piece hanging in the air, at any damage level | **OH** (rule F) |
| While a tornado is on a building it visibly comes apart or is swept off; when the tornado is gone what was lofted falls back, leaving rubble piles and damaged road | **OW** |

> **Parts and files (no file in two parts).**
> * **OT — Trees** (session `phase-o-viewer-trees-with-individual-lea-20261011-182829`): viewer agent, new `TreePainter.java` and the tree block of `BuildingPainter.java` only, compiles into `viewer/out_trees`. Dispatched; not re-planned. Built `[PARTIAL]`: steps OT1-OT3 below; it also changed one line each in `Renderer.java` and `Main.java`.
> * **OC — Clouds follow the day** (session `phase-o-sim-clouds-follow-the-day-20261011-182828`): sim agent, cloud spawning only, no protocol change. Dispatched; not re-planned. Built `[VERIFIED]` for the targets, `[PARTIAL]` for storm climates: steps OC1-OC3 below; it edited `sim/atmosphere.py`, `clouds.py`, `simulation.py`, `worldsim.py`, which is the one place Phase O touched `sim/`.
> * **OH — Houses and people** (`general-purpose`): owns `BuildingPainter.java` **except the tree block**, `DebugLineup.java`, new `Cast.java`, `Figures.java`, `Life.java`, `FloatTest.java`, `LifeTest.java`, `viewer/snapshots/vO/h/`.
> * **OW — Wreck** (`general-purpose`): owns new `Wreck.java`, `Chunks.java`, `Rubble.java`, `RoadWear.java`, `WreckTest.java`, `viewer/snapshots/vO/w/`, and small additive edits (re-read first) to `Renderer.java` (`paintGround`, `coverLevels`, `drawWorld`), `ViewerState.java` (the damage-row block), `Shared.java`, `FakeSim.java` (scene `hit`), `Main.java` (scene name, one selftest call).
> * Neither may touch `sim/`, `PROTOCOL.md`, `PLAN.md`, `saves/`, `config/`, `scrap/`, `build.bat`; OH may not touch `Renderer`, `ViewerState`, `Model`, `FakeSim`, `Main`; OW may not touch `BuildingPainter`, `DebugLineup`. Each compiles with `javac -encoding UTF-8 -d <own scratch folder> viewer/src/precip/*.java`; nobody runs `build.bat` (a game runs from `viewer\out`).
> * **Coupling, two calls:** OW wires OH's `Life.paint` and `Life.level` into `Renderer`; OW renders the **existing** `BuildingPainter.paint(g, o.withDamage(d), ...)` into its own images, so it needs nothing new from OH.
> * **Order:** OW starts now. OH starts when OT reports done (the tree block is OT's until then); earlier it may edit only `paint`, `jagged`, the house functions and `Ctx`, never from `paintTree` down, re-reading before each edit. OH7 waits for OW6. OI after both.

**What each part does**
| Part | Role |
|---|---|
| `BuildingPainter.java` (OH) | Rule F (nothing floats), heap as a height-field, the swept slab, house detail, baked people |
| `Cast.java`, `Figures.java`, `Life.java` (new, OH) | Who is where (seeded from the object) and the presence rule; the figure shapes; the live pass (walkers, a figure crossing a lit window, porch idling) and `Life.level` for the bake key |
| `Wreck.java`, `Chunks.java` (new, OW) | A damage row under a grounded tornado becomes chunks cut from the building's own picture; they loft, are released, fall to a pile |
| `Rubble.java`, `RoadWear.java` (new, OW) | Piles beside a ruin; potholes, cracks and torn asphalt on the road beside it; pure functions of the saved damage, baked |
| `Renderer`, `ViewerState`, `Shared`, `FakeSim`, `Main` (OW) | Bake-key bits, dirty range, the live slot, damage rows under a tornado, scene `hit`, selftest |
| `DebugLineup.java` (OH) | Buildings tab: damage-ladder stepper with a floating-pieces overlay, back-row view, hour and people switches, a Hit button |

### What the current build shows (measured while planning; scratch harnesses, redone as tests in OH1 and OW6)
A scratch `javac` of `viewer/src/precip` (7.5 s, nothing under `viewer\out`); every `DebugLineup` entry that is not a tree (9 house variants, 9 other kinds, the lamp) x **6 seeds x damage k = 0..24 of 24** (the bake's levels), day, dry, `BuildingPainter.paint` onto a transparent image at scale 1 and 2. A **floating piece** = an 8-connected component of 40 px or more (alpha 128 or more) with no path to the foundation band.
* **Census:** 385 of 2 736 damaged cases (**14.1 %**; 14.4 % at scale 2) have one; **0 of 144 intact**. By level: k 1-6 **2 %**, 7-12 **17 %**, 13-18 **24 %**, 19-22 **18 %**, 23-24 **1 %** (the ruin line removes everything above the walls); onset k 4-7. By kind (of 144 cases): station 51 %, factory 49 %, manor 27 %, farmhouse 22 %, apartment 17 %, water tower, office, barn 15 %, adobe 11 %, cabin 10 %, skyscraper 8 %, church 7 %, shop 6 %, townhouse and cottage 4 %, bungalow 3 %, windmill and hut 1 %, streetlamp 0. Largest: an office's top three floors **1 271 px** hovering (k 18), factory 595, skyscraper 482, manor 301.
* **Four kinds (pictures opened):** (a) a roof or top-floor slab cut loose where the six bites meet (office k 18, apartment and manor k 16); (b) a chimney, spire, cross, mast or pipe left above a cut (church k 12, factory k 8, station k 10, water tower k 11); (c) bare **rafters drawn over empty sky** (cottage k 8-17, barn k 10, 16): 1.2 px strokes, under the opaque threshold, but at alpha 40, 12 px, 16 px above the ground **1 420 of 2 736 cases (52 %)** have one; (d) rubble flecks within about 18 px of the ground, meant to be there, not counted.
* **Cause:** `BuildingPainter.paint` (about lines 354-386) erases bites, torn corner and ruin line from the building's image with `DstOut` and never asks whether what is left is still joined to the ground; rafters, studs, broken windows, soot and rubble (about 421-471) are drawn onto `g` after the image with at most a three-point `solid` test (line 364), so they can sit on sky.
* **Cost today:** a damaged paint 0.8-1.8 ms at scale 1, 2-5 ms at 2, 3.6-13.8 ms at 4 (cottage to skyscraper, JIT warm); a connected-component pass over a whole 420 x 700 canvas 0.9-1.2 ms at 1, 14-21 ms at 4; over the painter's own, smaller image it should cost a fraction (OH1 measures).
* **A tornado hit today** (forced EF3 / EF4 over a town, real sim read-only, seeds 11-13; the fake `supercell` scene snapshotted): funnel down **57-69 s**, moving **2.7-7.4 px/s**, 87-123 px wide; a destroyed small building goes 0 -> 1 in **4-6 s** (a townhouse 24 s) as about **100 `damage` rows of 0.01, ~17 a second**; the last row comes **1-7 s before** the funnel lifts; towers stay partial (0.2-0.94). The picture changes only by **re-baking the building at each 1/24 step**: cuts jump in, nothing moves. What flies is the sim's kind-7 debris, **at its 260-row cap** while the funnel is down (mean 229-235), brown 1.5-7 px chips with no link to a building, **gone 2 s after the funnel lifts**. Nothing falls back that a player can follow; no pile stands beside a ruin; the road is untouched; `drawScars` marks the path live and is not saved.
* **Not measured:** wet, snow and wind ladders; a hit through the live viewer (the picture is the fake's); the cost of any new pass; how the houses look to the user at night.

### The model in numbers (the one table OH and OW build from)
Every constant is a planner's **starting value** in one commented block of the class that uses it; OH8 and OW7 re-tune by measurement.
| Name | Start | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| Ladder | `k = round(damage x 24)`, 0..24 | The still picture is a **pure function of (object, k, wet, snow, wind level, life level)**: no clock, no history. Phase M runs it backwards and relies on that |
| **Rule F** | Paint everything (cuts, rafters, studs, windows with `SrcAtop`, soot, heap) into the building's own image; then every pixel of alpha >= 40 is 8-connected to the foundation band (rows from `y - 2`); unattached components of 6 px or more are removed and drawn as heap pieces in their mean colour | Every k, kind, variant, seed, both rows, scale 1-4, and the resting states below. Streetlamp exempt (0 %) |
| Heap | Height-field over the footprint +- `w/2 + 16`: each piece (1.4-4.8 px, count `6 + 22 d + w/10 d + w/3 ruin` as now) rests on the field | Rubble rests on the ground or on a piece, so it cannot float |
| Resting states | each k with airborne 0 and 1; the swept slab; after landing | `FloatTest` runs them all |
| Swept (q. 100) | house variants, shop, streetlamp: at `d >= 0.96` and `unit(seed) < 0.55` **no walls**, foundation, steps and heap only; other kinds keep the ruin line | Still a function of (object, k) |
| Chunk source | For a row of `o` from `k0` to `k1` while a grounded tornado is within `2.2 x width + 90` px: the pixels that vanished, `still(o, k0)` minus `still(o, k1)`, cut on a grid of `clamp(h/5, 8, 22)` px (cells of 12 px or more), at most **8 chunks a step** (largest first, the rest go to the heap), 40 a building, **160 in the scene** | A roof flies as a roof. No tornado near (replay, load, Phase M's falling rows): no chunk, the tile just changes |
| Chunk flight | Mirror of `sim/particles.py` `update_debris` (one commented block): gravity 480 px/s^2; within `3.2 x core + 40` px of the axis and above base + 30: updraft `(900 + 14 wind)/mass` up to 420 px/s, pull 2.4/s to the axis, swing 260 px/s^2 with the vortex `phase`; `mass = clamp(area/60, 1, 6)`; `core` from `Tornado.width`. **Release** when the funnel lifts, the chunk leaves the swing zone, or after `T_LOFT_MAX` 40 s | Light bits fly high, big ones tumble; the loft ends when the funnel moves on or lifts: "once the tornado is gone it falls back" |
| Fall, landing | After release: gravity plus a horizontal ease to the chunk's **designated pile** `Rubble.spot(o, j)`, arrival after `sqrt(2 dy/480)`; a 0.4 s dust puff, then dropped from the live pass | A chunk ends **on the ground at a pile**, never in the air or in a wall |
| Rubble piles | For `d >= 0.30`: `min(12, round(d (2 + w/20)))` piles at `o.x + side (w/2 + 6 + R u_j^2)`, `R = clamp(40 + 0.9 h, 60, 260)`, `side`, `u_j` from the seed; 6-16 px wide, 2-7 high, height-field on `terrain.at(x)` | Pure function of (object, k); survives a reload with nothing in the save |
| Airborne bit | While an object has chunks in flight its piles draw at 35 %; at landing the bit clears and they draw in full | One bit in the bake key, one re-bake per event |
| Road wear | Each `street` span, each building with `d >= 0.30`: over `o.x +- (w/2 + 36)` strength `d x smooth(1 - (abs(x - o.x) - w/2)/36)`; potholes `round(1 + 5 (d - 0.3)/0.7 x w/50)` (5-13 px, 2 px deep), cracks 14-40 px, torn patches 10-30 px; none on the highway; after `paintRoads` | From saved damage only; empty road the tornado crossed gets none (q. 101) |
| Bake key bits | existing: snow, wet, `k << 12`, wind `<< 20`; new: life level 0-4 `<< 24`, sheltered `<< 27`, airborne `<< 28`; dirty range `o.w/2 + 20 + Rubble.reach(o)` for `d >= 0.30` | `Life.level` is OH's; `sheltered` (grounded tornado within **1 500 px**, 5 s hysteresis) and `airborne` are OW's |
| People (q. 99, 102) | Decoration: seeded from the object id and the clock, no path-finding, no wire. `n = clamp(round(w/22 + u), 1, 4)` (+1 from two floors); a window figure in about 55 % of lit windows (lit as today, `Ctx.on`); porch figure for variants with a porch; yard figure where a prop exists; outdoors only when `night < 0.55`, `wet < 0.35`, `snow < 0.5`, not sheltered, then `0.35 + 0.5 day` in four steps. Broken windows (`d >= 0.2`) stay dark, so no figure in a ruin | Window bust 7-9 px, standing 12-14 (a door is about 16), seated 9, child 8-9, dog 6; drawn only from `LIFE_MIN_PX` 1.6 (the beads' threshold), below it the picture is unchanged |
| Walkers | `clamp(round(sqrt(population)/3), 1, 12)` a settlement, on `street` spans of `hello.roads` inside it, kerb line `terrain.at(x) - 1`, ping-pong 12 px inside the ends at 14-20 px/s from a hash of (settlement, index) and `anim`; one in five has a dog; only when `day > 0.4`, `wet < 0.3`, `snow < 0.6`, not sheltered, flood depth at x under 1 px | Drawn after the trees, before the cars, in front of both rows |
| Cost targets | bake: house paint +25 % at most, prune <= 1 ms for a house at scale 4; live: `Life` <= 0.15 ms, `Chunks` <= 0.4 ms a frame (12 walkers, 20 crossing windows, 160 chunks in view) | Measured old against new on one scene; the 3-7 ms budget is unconfirmed (question 17) |

### Bake and live: what the back row gets
Still detail is baked into the ground tile; anything animated is a live pass; a live overlay may be drawn only for **front-row** buildings (`layer != 1`), since the live pass lies over the whole tile and a back-row overlay would land on the front buildings in front of it. A third to two thirds of every town is back row (35 of 104 houses, 13 of 34 offices, 25 of 39 apartments, 15 of 23 skyscrapers).
| Thing | Front row | Back row (`layer` 1, 0.9 scale, haze 0.36) |
|---|---|---|
| House detail | baked | baked (same painter) |
| People | **baked** standing figures; **live** adds a figure crossing a lit window (a window every 20-90 s, 3-5 s across) and porch idling (1 px sway / 3 s) | **baked standing figures only** (a still shadow in the lit window, a still porch or yard figure); no crossing, no idling |
| Street walkers | live, in front of both rows | the same walkers (the street is in front of both) |
| Ladder, swept slab, heap, rule F | baked, re-baked at each 1/24 | baked; each 1/24 step is a visible step of the collapse |
| Chunks in flight | live, always | live **only above `frontTop(x)`** (highest front-row roof line over x + 8 px, built when the objects are set); lower, a chunk is not drawn (it is behind the front row) and its pile shows in the bake |
| Rubble piles | baked after the front row and the roads | baked **with the back row**, before terrain and front row |
| Road wear | baked after `paintRoads` | same |
Not planned: live window figures for back-row windows no front building covers (open question 104).

### Protocol and sim: none needed
**No protocol block, no sim part.** The collapse is driven by what the wire carries: `frame.damage` rows (~17 a second under a hit) and `frame.tornadoes` (`x`, `width`, `reach`, `phase`); piles and road wear are pure functions of `damage`, which the sim already saves; people are decoration. An old viewer on a new stream, a new viewer on an old stream or save, and `sim/water.py` are unaffected. **Would need the sim, not planned:** wear on empty road the tornado crossed (a persisted path, q. 101), cars reacting to rubble (`sim/cars.py`, q. 103), injured people (q. 102).

### Relation to Phase M (planned, unbuilt)
Phase M lowers `damage` in 1/24 steps and holds it constant in `wait`, `clear` and `scaffold`. Compatible because: (1) the ladder is a pure function of (object, k), rule F holding at every k, so Phase M may run it backwards; (2) chunks come only from **rising** rows under a tornado; (3) piles and road wear read **one function**, `Wreck.level(o)`, which returns `damage` today; Phase M's MV changes only it: `d0 x (1 - p)` while the stage is `clear`, 0 from `scaffold` on, so **a site's rubble and road wear are cleared before the scaffold goes up** (if Phase M is built first, OW applies this itself from `hello.repair`); (4) the airborne bit is never set by a falling row. Phase M's on-site rubble clearing is its own; Phase O's piles are off-site and its heap is inside the building image. **[VERIFIED 2026-10-12] Phase M note:** `Wreck.level(o)` (`Wreck.java` line 51, still `return o.damage`) is the single function Phase M changes; rubble (`Rubble.java`) and road wear (`RoadWear.java`) are derived from damage, so they clear when damage falls. Phase M is still unbuilt.

## Open questions
Advisory, asked in SA-Asker session `big-world-plan-md-20261008-153813`; the default applies if unanswered.
1. Side panel contents. Default: right = existing timeline/stats/events, left = new World panel (settlements, terrain, view).
2. Autosave target. Default: overwrite the slot's `state.sav` atomically, keep one `.bak`.
3. Background versus sky. Default: replaces gradient and stars; sun/moon and day-night dimming still apply.
4. Settlement guarantee. Default: village + city guaranteed only for `normal`/`dense` at width ≥ 3840; `sparse` guarantees a village; `none` means none.
5. Unresolved: whether 30 ticks/s holds at Huge (9600×1400) in pure Python. Measured in A10; may need a lower cap on that preset.
6. Phase E, deferred (asked in SA-Asker session `fair-clouds-and-cumulus-to-cb-plan-md-20261008-195348`; default applies if unanswered): stratus / altostratus thickening into nimbostratus through the same `from_type` / `morph` mechanism. Default: out of scope for v2.1; revisit once the cumulus → cumulonimbus morph is verified.
7. Phase F, under-cloud darkening (asked in SA-Asker session `v2-2-plan-md-phase-f-20261008-212812`, Q2; default applies if unanswered): trigger and strength. Default: camera centre inside a cloud's footprint, eased over about 1.5 s, at most about 45 % dim scaled by that cloud's darkness and `form`; setting on/off plus strength; dim drawn after the sky/backdrop and before particles so the background picture and rain are unchanged.
8. Phase F, saving in the guided modes (same session, Q3; default applies if unanswered): Hurricane Formation and Cloud Formations create no save slots (the sim answers `save` with `ok:false`); Esc returns to the menu and the last tab / stage is kept in `settings.json`.
9. Phase F, `--mode hurricane` seam in `precip_sim.py` (same session, Q1; default applies if unanswered): Sim A wires it; Sim B exposes `HurricaneSim(seed)` with `hello_message()`, a per-tick step returning the frame dict, and `handle_command(cmd)`. The two agents must agree on these names before Integration.
10. Phase F, unresolved: real figures for the six regions (transects, station means, annual precipitation) must come from sources Sim A can actually reach; if a source cannot be verified, that figure is recorded here rather than guessed. Also unresolved: whether the 30 ticks/s budget still holds in sandbox with the extra preset work at Huge size (measured in F18).
11. Phase G, `presets.py` ownership (asked in SA-Asker session `v2-3-plan-md-phase-g-20261009-100057`, Q1; unanswered, default applies): Sim World owns `sim/presets.py` including `climate_block()`; Sim Core 1 only reads it and falls back to a generated climate (`source:"generated"`) if a preset has no monthly normals yet.
12. Phase G, shared-file seams (same session; default applies): `precip_sim.py` is edited by Sim Core 1 (`--month/--hour/--year-days`) and Sim World (`world` in `MODES`); each re-reads the file before editing. Viewer Sandbox owns `Model`, `ViewerState`, `Renderer`, `ShowcaseScreen`, `NewSimScreen`; Viewer World owns `AppShell`, `MainMenuScreen` and the new world files; neither edits `SimProcess.java` (extra arguments go through its existing argument list).
13. Phase G, interpretations chosen as defaults (brief, not reopened): "gasses" = humidity (water vapour), CO2, aerosol; "lifetime tour" = the supercell tab of Cloud Formations (`ShowcaseScreen.java`) with an ordered tour and one guaranteed tornado per cycle; "dot or state" = curated real-data location dots plus country / US-state regions that map to a preset, with no invented data for arbitrary points; "snapshots" = verification images in `viewer/snapshots/v23/` (the F12 screenshot key, `Action.SCREENSHOT`, already exists and must also work in world mode).

19. Phase G, decisions made without approval (every `ASSUMED` entry in the five sessions, one line each; none were answered):

   - Sim Core 2: bad `spawn` type ignored but the cloud still spawns; EF thresholds from Texas Tech WISE (2004) / NWS SPC converted to m/s; `atmosphere.supercell_raw` reworked (Oklahoma wind 4 m/s gave zero shear); car lanes per direction, wrecks ignored by traffic, drivers brake for tornadoes; `test_perf` busy-sky precondition 1000 to 800.
   - Sim World (Q1-Q3 closed on defaults): Iceland dropped; Atacama and Antarctica kept with weaker wind-source caveats; place elevation used for the lapse correction (none when absent). Biome work: `Atmosphere.arid` for desert only; a named town may stand on its own summit span; 12 places dropped for lack of room; highest-ground test relaxed 120 to 150 px; `frozen_water` = biome flag OR mean <= 0 C; ponds never dig below the lowest ground; Sydney placed west of the Blue Mountains and Dubbo dropped.
   - Viewer Sandbox: default keys 1/2 3/4 5/6 7/8 9/0 C T K (Q1); Climate HUD as its own top-right panel (Q2); `build.bat` not run while a game was running from `viewer/out`; biome scenery tints derived from `biome.id` and no snow line on a desert far ridge.
   - Viewer World: paged region chips in `NewSimScreen` (Q1); placeholder monthly curve labelled as such (Q2, moot once the real `presets.json` landed).

The folder's 10:44 timestamp is the Viewer World agent's `--selftest` deleting its own slot, but that run's log lists only its own slot before its first delete, so the two saves were already gone; every delete in `SelfTest` targets a slot the run created, and no Python test or tool deletes under `saves/`. As a precaution `--selftest` now uses a temporary saves folder and refuses to run in the real one. Consequence: the real-save load tests (G5, G12) skip and were covered with synthetic v2.2 states. Whether the two saves can be recovered is unknown. `saves/` now holds only `new-zealand-april-tmml9y`.

Of question 33: 13 renders take about 0.35 s per type at Medium; the first forming and last dissipating steps draw (form 0.11 and 0.09); the safe tracker lives in each renderer's `Shared`, so the two pictures do not leak into each other; the double-click on the icon is still not done.
