Phase H2, decisions made without approval (session `branching-lightning-in-cloud-fog-glow-20261010-112947`): in Epilepsy-safe mode the branches are drawn, at the same width and opacity as the channel (one path, so overlaps cannot exceed the peak), not thinner; with a channel in the cloud the three point lights now fade out 170 px above the cloud base instead of at the cloud top (the cloud is lit by the channel instead); only the host cloud glows (a second cloud overlapping it is not lit by the hidden channel), and the glow's mask is that cloud's image where it stood when the bolt appeared; branches add no light of their own and cast no shadows, and are drawn across buildings; about one branch in ten off the channel may reach the ground; ice crystals and other particles drawn over the cloud are brightened by the glow like everything else there; the old checks #101 and #102 are now #110 and #111. Not covered: `build.bat` (H2.7); the heaviest bolt costs about 0.6 ms (H2.6); showcase, replay and lesson modes were not pictured (same `drawLightning`); `f_real_sim_cb.png` shows the real simulation's clouds with a bolt put in by `--bolt`, not a strike the simulation produced.
35. Phase J, the readings of the ambiguous labels (asked in SA-Asker session `advanced-graphics-plan-md-phase-j-20261010-152416`, Q1-Q6; nobody answered within a four-minute wait, so every default below was applied and recorded `ASSUMED`; the user may overrule any of them before J7-J14 are built):
   - #8 "No tint" (Q1, default A). Planned as the colour casts only: the night-blue and dusk-orange mixes in `Lit.lit`, the warm and purple casts in `CloudPainter.Light.of`, the dusk-orange veil on a background picture, and the cold-white colour of the lightning light. The code shows that this is the smaller half of what the time of day does to a colour: `Lit.light` (0.38 at night to 1.0 by day) multiplies every surface, and the sky gradient, stars, sun and moon follow the hour too. With A a night scene stays dark and loses only its blue; if the user means "the scene never darkens", that is B (also skip the `light` factor, so every surface is lit as at noon, sky and stars unchanged), a one-line change inside the same `Lit.lit` and `Lit.differs`. A third reading is the per-cloud colour cast `Model.Cloud.tint` that the sim sends (greenish hail storms, `CloudPainter` ~756 and ~860); it is not planned. Recommendation: A, then look at `h_no_tint_dusk.png`; if night still reads as "tinted" to the user, switch to B.
   - #4 "Global lightning" (Q2, default A). Planned as every present bolt, wherever it is, lighting the whole view by one uniform level (alpha squared times `GLOBAL_LEVEL`, scaled by the scene light), with no falloff, no shadows and no glow: the flash the game had before Phase H, built on the existing grid pipeline (`Direct.brighten` no longer exists). The code does not contradict this reading, but it shows the cost of it: it is the one lightning mode in which a bolt on the other side of a 9 600 px world flashes the player's screen, which is what Phase H set out to remove. Recommendation: A as the user asked, with the hint line of the switch saying so. B (uniform, but only for bolts within `REACH` of the view) is a one-condition change.
   - #3 "Lightning detail off" (Q3, default A). Planned as unshadowed local light and no glow in the cloud; the bolt, its three point lights and their falloff stay. The other reading, no light at all, is the same thing as No flash (#9), so it would leave two switches doing one job. Recommendation: A.
   - #2 "Render" (Q4, default A). Planned as a render-resolution cap of 0.75× and 0.5×, stored under the new key `graphics.advanced.renderScale`. The code supports it without a change to `ViewerPanel` (`sx = cap` whenever the display scale is above the cap, so a 100 % display renders at 0.5× and stretches the buffer); what it does not do is keep the `Renderer`-drawn text sharp (side panels, labels, the FPS readout are drawn into the buffer; only the `Overlay` of menus and dialogs is drawn after the blit). Other readings of the bare word "Render" (a render-distance or a frame-rate cap) were not planned because the brief fixes this one. Recommendation: A; the option shows a one-line note about the soft text.
   - `background.tint` (Q6, default A). The Background tab's "Tint with time of day" keeps its stored value; the effective backdrop tint is that value and not No tint. So with No tint on, the picture behind the sky no longer darkens at night or warms at sunset, whatever the Background tab says, and the Background toggle is not greyed out (a possible later polish).
   - #10 "No snow" (Q5, default A). Planned as everything snow: flakes, snowpack objects, ground cover and the road tint, roofs and trees, and the cap of the far mountains above the freezing line. A pond that is frozen because the sim's cover on it is above 0.5 stays frozen. If the user wants permanent mountain caps left alone, that is B and removes one condition from `TerrainPainter` (J15).
36. Phase J, #1 Max particles has no stable particle id (unresolved, to be measured in J5): the wire carries no id and `rot` is stepped every third or sixth tick, so the keep test uses the fall-line cell and the size. Rain falls along a fixed line, so its decision stands; snow drifts and a thinned flake may flip in or out between two ticks. The check counts the flips between consecutive frames (target under 3 % at 50 %); the fallback is a coarser cell for kinds 2-5; a particle id on the wire would settle it but needs a `PROTOCOL.md` change, which this phase excludes. **Resolved in J5 (2026-10-10):** the hash flickered (9.8 % of raindrops, 33 % of other particles a frame); the viewer now follows the particles itself and hands each one a rank it keeps (0.00 % change sides on the built-in sim). Still not measured on the Python sim's particles.
37. Phase J, #6 Don't render what you can't see (unresolved): J11 shows that off-screen objects, clouds, cars, funnels, bolts and particles are already culled, so what is left is the occlusion by opaque cloud pixels, the repeated scans of the particle array and the whole-tile blits. None of it is measured yet. Cloud images are fully opaque only in the cores of dense clouds at High quality, so the gain of the occlusion test may be small; J12 allows a `[SKIPPED]` with the figures. Because the option defaults to on, it may only ship if it is pixel-identical (J12, J21); the culling that already exists is not behind the switch and stays on, since turning it off would only make the game slower for no change in the picture. **Measured in J12 (2026-10-10):** the occlusion test is exact and built, and finds 0 blits at the default zoom and about 41 a frame under a storm at zoom 1.3; no measurable gain either way. Asked as session Q1 whether the switch should stay; unanswered, assumed yes.
38. Phase J, #7 Simple clouds, the recipe (unresolved at planning): `CloudPainter.render` is about 630 lines of grid, noise and light march; the plan names where to cut (`cellPx`, `cap`, `oct`, `steps`, no rim or striations) but the exact flat colouring will be decided by looking at pictures of all nine types, and merged clouds and supercells (extra passes `P_SHEET`, `P_WALL`, `P_HEAP`) may need a few lines of their own to keep their silhouette. **Decided in J13 (2026-10-10):** see the recipe there; merged clouds and supercells needed no lines of their own beyond the darker supercell base.
39. Phase J, not planned, noticed on the way (no action unless the user asks): the Hurricane and World-map screens use `HurricanePainter` and `WorldPainter`, not `Renderer`, so only #2 (Render) changes them; Debug's Lightning tab renders with the same flags as the game, so a player's Advanced choices show there too; `Settings.renderScale` and the new `graphics.advanced.renderScale` both cap the same value, and the Graphics page does not say that the Advanced page can lower it further; a quiet-machine bench of the baseline has not been made (Phases H8, H2.6 and G20 have the same gap).
40. Phase J, noticed while building (2026-10-10; no action taken): (a) `CloudPainter.render` is one method of about 630 lines, too long for the JIT to compile well; moving the per-pixel loop of the simple clouds into a method of its own made them about ten times cheaper (8.3-8.9 ms against 82-88 ms a render), so the same move would very likely speed up every cloud at Low, Medium and High, but it has to be proved pixel-identical first. (b) At render scale 0.75x the small text the renderer draws into the buffer is hard to read; drawing the HUD after the stretch, as the dialogs are, would fix it. (c) "Lightning detail off" lets the light through the ground as well, so the soil under a strike is lit; keeping the terrain in the shadow map and dropping only the buildings would cost little. (d) "No tint" leaves the sky gradient alone, so the haze of the far hills keeps the colours of the hour. (e) The switches of the Advanced page that concern light do nothing while Epilepsy-safe lightning is on, and the page does not say so.
41. Phase K, a windsock can stand in a puddle (unresolved; asked in SA-Asker session `plan-md-phase-k-record-20261010-192829`, Q1; default applies if unanswered). Seen by the orchestrator on mountainous seed 7 and explained by `generate_world` (`avoid_sets["windsock"] = (foot, pond_spans, street_spans)`, windsocks placed after puddles). Default A: leave as built. B: add the puddle spans to the windsock's avoid set; that changes only windsock `x` / `y` (no extra `rng` draws) but moves the full-world hashes in `test_presets.py` again and is a new sim task. **ANSWERED by the work (2026-10-11): moot.** Windsocks were removed from the game in Phase L (LB1, LA3), so there is no windsock to stand in a puddle; B's avoid-set change no longer applies.
42. Phase K, puddle decisions made without approval (session `puddles-settle-to-the-lowest-point-20261010-173720`, every `ASSUMED` entry; Q2 of the record session asks about the third; default applies if unanswered): `PUDDLE_TOL` 1.0 px rather than 0.5 (the viewer draws the lens 1 px below `y`); a blocked puddle is forced down onto the ground when that is at most `PUDDLE_FORCE_MAX` 3 px and otherwise moved to the nearest allowed level spot, first within 400 px, finally anywhere in the world (the first rule is superseded, K11); growth capped per puddle to the width of its hollow (at most 140, at least 20, centred, inside the world), derived from the terrain and not saved; `PUDDLE_BANK` 3.0 px, which cut the mean grown width in hilly worlds from about 65 to 37 px and in mountainous ones from about 75 to 35 (5 px would keep more; default A keeps 3.0, B raises it and needs a new float-gap measurement). Existing saves keep their puddle positions because objects are stored in the save; only new worlds settle.
43. Phase K, rain-dot and droplet decisions made without approval (session `rain-dot-droplets-on-windows-and-cars-20261010-173720`, its single `ASSUMED` entry split into items; no question was asked there; Q4 of the record session asks whether to keep items 3-5; default applies if unanswered): (1) the dot is white with radius `max(0.7, half the streak head width + 0.15)` logical px and a blue rim (`0x6C98E0`, alpha 150 of 256, +0.45 px) that fades in over zoom 1.5-2.8, with "No trails" the rim at every zoom and the floor 1.0 px; (2) the glint left as it was in selection and size (see 44); (3) no Advanced switch hides the droplets, because none obviously applies and the painters do not see the Advanced settings; (4) running drops only on front-row buildings (`layer != 1`), because on the back row they would be drawn over the buildings in front of them, which are in the same baked tile, so back-row panes get still beads only; (5) beads only through `Ctx.window` panes: the church's arched windows, the factory's roof lights and the barn's loft get none; (6) a storm-damaged building (`damage >= 0.04`) keeps the beads baked into its remaining glass but gets no running drops; (7) snow wins: building beads scale by `1 - 4 x snow` and are gone from snow 0.25, cars get none on the roof under a snow cap or when wrecked or airborne; (8) building beads appear in six steps (the wetness levels the tiles are keyed by), not smoothly; (9) wipers leave 45 % of the windscreen beads; (10) `BuildingPainter.java` kept its CRLF line endings, the other two files LF; (11) `Renderer.drawWorld` got a ten-line hook outside the rain block; (12) the `AdvancedTest` comment about the old drop sprite's reach (line ~617) is now out of date and was not edited, the file not being the agent's.
44. Phase K, the glint next to the dot (unresolved; Q3 of the record session; default A keeps it): at zoom 4 the glint star (reported about 14 px, arm up to 9 px by `glintQ`) is larger than the dot, and it is now centred on the dot instead of sitting behind the tip. The brief said to keep the glint and report if it dwarfed the dot; no picture was judged for that and nobody looked in a live window. B: shrink the glint at high zoom. **ANSWERED by the work (2026-10-11):** the glint became a tiny dot by default (LA1), so the star no longer dwarfs the drop dot; the Star stays selectable in the Advanced tab as the old look, and the dot's own size and glow are question 91.
45. Phase K, figures in the two sessions that could not be reconciled with the code or with each other (no action; recorded so nobody quotes them as settled): the session says 30 pictures were opened and `viewer/snapshots/v2/rain-dot/` holds 29 files; the puddle outcome gives "1239 puddles" for the float-gap table but "3935/4368 plain" for the unchanged count (672 plain-world puddles were in the baseline sample, so the denominators are different samples); the glint star's "about 14 px" and the dot's "4-5 px at zoom 4" were read from crops, and the formula in `Renderer.java` gives a smaller dot for typical drops (K2); the baseline suite is reported as 568 tests with 1 failure in the session log and as 2 failures plus 3 errors (missing files in a copy outside the project) in a saved log, both of them perf failures under load that the new code shares; `tools/bench_sim.py`, asked for in the brief, is not reported as run; the desktop icon's write time moved from 15:18 to 17:53 with its content unchanged and no recorded cause (K15).
46. Phase L, "puddles grow unlimitedly" (asked in SA-Asker session `phase-l-plan-drains-flooding-protocol-20261010-194541`, Q1; unanswered, default applies): read as **no width cap**, with the water still stopping at the rim of its hollow, spilling into the next hollow, and being lost at a world edge. B would let water rise until the world is one lake; C would keep a large cap (say 600 px). A literal "unlimited" cannot exist on a heightmap without drowning it, so this is the nearest honest reading. Phase K's cap (`puddle_cap`, 140 px) stays only on the legacy objects (K16). **Built as A (2026-10-11):** `sim/water.py` has no width cap; the user has not commented on it (the user's later answer, question 75, overturned the curb, not the cap).
47. Phase L, where a drain's size comes from (Q2; default applies): seeded per settlement by its kind (`normal`), plus the New Simulation choice Small / Normal / Large / Random and the live `drains` command; the sizes are a function of the seed, the settlement id and its kind and do not touch the world's random stream. Alternatives: one global size, or size from population only. The kind ranges (hamlet 2-4, village 3-5.5, town 3.5-6.5, city 4-8 px) overlap on purpose so that some cities have small drains; they are the planner's numbers. **Built as A (2026-10-11), measured:** the seeded `normal` sizes and the New Simulation / `drains` command exist; because the rain classes are close, the towns that flood in `normal` mode under one storm are mostly the short ones (questions 92-93).
48. Phase L, what a flood does (Q3; default applies): the water rises over the street, a card shows settlement, size, inflow against capacity, depth and flooded yes / no, and cars slow and stop (stall depth 1.8 x wheel radius, so a sedan stands at 5.8 px). **No building damage from flooding, no car washed away, no drowning**: left out because it would touch the damage model, the save format and `BuildingPainter`; B (buildings take damage above a depth) and C (cars float away) are not planned.
49. Phase L, rain from outside a town (Q4; default applies): only rain that falls on a settlement's own span counts for its drains, and runoff from the hills around it is routed past it, so the drain size alone decides. B (the whole catchment of the town's hollow flows in) is more realistic for valley towns, but then a town's location decides as much as its drains and the tuning contract C1-C4 would not hold. **Built as A (2026-10-11).** Phase N changes how rain from the slopes reaches a town (question 76); this answer is superseded there.
50. Phase L, the old puddle objects (Q5; default applies): `puddle` objects stay in `hello.objects`, in saves and in the seeded stream, and the sim keeps growing them with the Phase K cap for any viewer that does not know `hello.water`; the v2.4 viewer does not draw them. B (remove them) renumbers ids after them and re-records the full-world hashes in `test_presets.py` again. **Built as A (2026-10-11); one visible consequence:** with `hello.water` present Debug Weather's "Ground water > Puddle" showed a wet street and no puddle until the viewer follow-up (item 2, in progress) draws a v2.4 pool there.
51. Phase L, Debug (Q6; default applies): "add the feature to the Debug Game mode" is read as a new ninth tab, **Flood** (key 9), on a scripted `FakeSim` world with a Java copy of the water model (`FakeWater.java`) and a FakeSim-only `rain` command, plus pools showing in the existing Weather tab; the drain size goes through the real `drains` command. B (only a switch in the Weather tab) is smaller but cannot show size against rain rate. Debug's glint Dot / Star and wind control are LA4, not this tab. **Built as A (2026-10-11), with differences:** two stacked pictures instead of a side corner, a fixed late-morning clock, starting sizes 3.0 and 8.2 px (question 97).
52. Phase L, flat worlds (Q7; default applies, and measured in LS0): a world with no hollow of at least 1 px (`--terrain flat`, the showcase stage) holds **no standing water**, only wet ground; showcase, hurricane and world mode do not carry the v2.4 keys at all, so the lesson modes are unchanged. B (a thin film on wet flat ground) would be a different drawing and is not planned. Whether a `--terrain flat` sandbox has any hollow at all is unknown until LS0. **ANSWERED by measurement (LS0, 2026-10-10):** a flat sandbox **does** have hollows (4.1 of 1 px or more, median depth 3.8 px) because settlement pads are levelled at their own height and water collects between raised pads; the reading "a flat world holds no standing water" is true only without settlements, and showcase, hurricane and world mode carry no water at all, as planned.
53. Phase L, the glint setting and the droplets (Q8; LA's readings, not the planner's design): "a graphics setting for it" = the Advanced tab option Dot (default) / Star; "the droplets go with the wind ... a chance to go from the gravity's degrees from the degrees the wind is facing" = running drops and beads on windows and cars leaning between straight down and the wind's slant, each drop with its own deterministic share. The sim's own rain already leans with the wind (particle `vx` follows `wind.u`), so no sim change is planned for it. If the user meant the rain streak itself, or a random per-drop angle that changes every frame, that is a new viewer task. **Built (2026-10-11), with a correction to the note above:** the sim's rain does lean with the wind, but by under 2 degrees, so LA could not use it as the reference; the droplets lean by wind speed (question 90). The Dot / Star setting is `graphics.advanced.glintStar`.
54. Phase L, decisions made without being asked (every `ASSUMED` entry of the plan session, one line each; none was answered): standing water is fill-and-spill pools on the heightmap's hollows (a level surface, not an ellipse); a hollow shallower than 1 px is not one; ponds are closed hollows that swallow the rain that runs into them; drains exist in every settlement kind including hamlets; one street body per settlement bounded by 10 px virtual curbs that are not in the terrain; flooded means depth 3 px or more and "overflowing" means the curb is reached; inlets every 60 px and the capacity is inlets x size^2, so size alone decides; the classes are 3.0 / 4.7 / 8.2 px; a settlement's state words are dry / wet / ponding / flooded / overflowing; `frame.water` is the complete list at up to 10 Hz and `frame.flood` at up to 5 Hz, both only on change; a street body's id is its settlement's id; water is painted after the cars and the rain so a car stands in it and a drop reaching it is covered; the drains card, the pipe overlay (`D`) and the size keys `,` `.` are the planner's UI; the `rain` command exists only in `FakeSim`; water exists only in sandbox mode; an old save gets the same seed-derived sizes a new world would have and empty pools; the soak rule loses water in proportion to width; snow that melts on warm ground in a preset already becomes rain hits and so feeds pools, nothing else melts into them. **Built as listed (2026-10-11), with these notes:** water is also lost over a rim into a pond or off a world edge (the ledger counts it); a merged pool splits again when its level falls (not in the list); a street body is listed only from 0.05 px of depth; the virtual curbs are superseded by Phase N.
55. Phase L, numbers that are not measured (unresolved until LS6): `HIT_AREA`, `INLET_K`, `SOAK`, the class sizes and the kind ranges are starting values from arithmetic on one scratch measurement (rain hits per bucket per second 3.4 / 4.3 / 8.0 for nimbostratus / cumulonimbus / supercell; the gaps between classes are small, about 2.4x, and there is no real "drizzle" class). If the real classes are too close for the tuning contract C1-C6 to hold, the miss is recorded with its table instead of being tuned away. Also unmeasured: how often two storms stack over one town, and whether the water step costs 0.3 ms a tick. **ANSWERED by measurement (LS0, LS6, LS8; 2026-10-11):** `HIT_AREA` became 1.15, `INLET_K`, `SOAK`, the class sizes and the kind ranges stayed; the rain classes measured 4.36 / 5.78 / 8.19 hits per second per bucket (about 1.9x apart, not 2.4x), a drizzle is the weak tail of a nimbostratus, and the contract was met in part (C2 and C3 for towns of 650 px and up, C1 / C4 and short towns missed: questions 92-95); two merged clouds deliver about 2x one cloud; the water step costs 0.002-0.023 ms a tick, far under 0.3 ms. Still unmeasured: the flood tables at speed x8 and with merged clouds.
56. Phase L, asked for or implied but not turned into a step: building damage from flooding and cars washed away (48); runoff from the hills into a town (49); snowmelt flooding; a flooded-town mark on the minimap; sound; truly unlimited water (46); a visible internal pipe outside the `D` overlay (the pipe is "internal": it is only drawn as a cutaway on request); the user's wording "have a chance" for the droplets beyond LA's per-drop share (53). Windsock removal and the glint / droplet work are built by LA and LB from their own briefs, not designed here.
57. Phase L, sequencing assumptions (planner's): LS starts after LB's outcome is recorded and LV after LA's, because they share `sim/tests/_schema.py` and `Renderer.java`, `FakeSim.java`, `ViewerState.java`, `Settings.java`, `DebugScreen.java`, `Main.java` respectively; if the orchestrator starts them earlier, each shared edit must be re-read first and the build may fail inside a file the agent did not touch (then wait and retry, Phase I18). `PROTOCOL.md` v2.4 is frozen for LS and LV: a change needs a blocking SA-Asker question and a lockstep edit, as in Phases A-G. Phase K's open questions 41 (a windsock in a puddle) and 42 (the puddle cap) are **moot for what the player sees** from Phase L on (K16) and are left as written. **ANSWERED by the work (2026-10-11):** the sequencing held (LA's outcome 2026-10-10 20:35 then LV at 20:36; LB's outcome 20:38 then LS at 20:41) and `PROTOCOL.md` v2.4 stayed frozen for both implementers; the only later edits to it that this sync knows of are Phase N's v2.4.1 and its own six clarifications of v2.4 statements the sim does otherwise.
58. Phase M, what "regenerate" means (asked in SA-Asker session `phase-m-plan-buildings-regenerate-with-s-20261010-202031`, Q1; unanswered, default applies): every damaged or destroyed building is **rebuilt by the sim over game time and ends whole** (`damage` back to 0, lights and live details back). This relaxes v2.3's "damage never decreases" to "rises with a hit, falls only while the object is being rebuilt" (the one changed sentence of `PROTOCOL.md`, M0). B: damage fades back with no process (rejected, the user asked for the process). C: only some kinds or only on request (`--repair off` and the `repair` command already give "on request" as a setting).
59. Phase M, the stages (Q2; default applies): wait for the storm, site fenced and rubble cleared, scaffold up (bay by bay, lift by lift), structure floor by floor, walls and cladding, roof, windows and fittings, scaffold down, finished; a stage whose need is under 0.05 is skipped, so a roof-bitten house has no structure stage and a facade-damaged tower is scaffold + cladding + windows with no demolition; a destroyed building is built from the ground up inside its scaffold. Tower crane for the kinds in `DAMAGE_BIG` and for the church, a skip, ladder and pallets for the small ones, **workers drawn** (up to six tiny figures) on front-row buildings only. B: same stages, no crane or workers (the Advanced switch of 66 already gives that). C: another stage list.
60. Phase M, the wire (Q3; default applies): reuse `frame.damage` (allowed to fall, in 1/24 steps, only for an open site) plus a new `frame.repair` list of `[id, stage, p, d0, hold]`, `hello.repair`, `hello.objects[].repair`, `frame.repair_info` and a `repair` command; `version` stays 2. A v2.4 viewer ignores the lower rows and the unknown keys and keeps its last damage picture; a v2.5 viewer on an older stream draws nothing new. B (a separate list while `damage` stays monotone until a single drop to 0) would let the existing viewer guard stand but would draw the shell from a number that does not change; it was not chosen because the existing painter, run in reverse by a falling `damage`, is what draws the rising walls.
61. Phase M, timing (Q4; default applies, **numbers from measurement, see "What the sim delivers today"**): work seconds at normal for a destroyed building of reference size: house 150, shop 160, barn 140, windmill 210, water tower 240, church 300, apartment 330, factory 380, office 560, skyscraper 780, streetlamp 25; partial damage takes less (never under 45 s, lamp 20 s); a size factor 0.7–1.5 and a per-object jitter 0.85–1.15; work starts 25 s after the tornado is gone and the gust is down; 2 / 3 / 4 / 6 crews for a hamlet / village / town / city. Measured: a tornado is grounded 57–73 s, natural tornadoes in an Oklahoma world come one per about 800 s (gaps 318–1 600 s), and the model leaves a forced tornado's town scaffolded 31–43 s after it is gone and finished 187–628 s after. Modes: slow x0.5, normal, fast x3. B halves and C doubles every time. Only the Oklahoma preset with its clock held was measured for the gap; the default world and other presets make fewer supercells, so their towns stay whole longer.
62. Phase M, night (Q5; default applies): work **continues at night** and the site is lit by work lamps (a sim day is only 300 s, night about 140 s; stopping at night would make a house take two days). B: work stops at night (a `gate` condition on `time_of_day`, one line in `repair.py`).
63. Phase M, a second hit (Q6; default applies): a tornado that raises the damage of an open site by 0.03 or more sends it back to `wait` with the new, higher damage as `d0`, the plan is recomputed, the work done is lost and the scaffold is gone (the next bake simply has none). B: keep the progress and only add the new damage (damage is one number, so it cannot know what was built). Note that a destroyed building whose damage has fallen below 1.0 can be hit again, which a never-lowered damage could not.
64. Phase M, trees (Q7; default applies): damaged trees **regrow without scaffold** (a snapped tree grows back from a sapling over 480 s of work at normal, a bent one straightens); a separate small step (MS4, MV5) that can be dropped. B: trees stay damaged. Cars, roads, the tornado's ground scars and wrecked cars are not rebuilt or cleared (see 72).
65. Phase M, controls and Debug (Q8; default applies): a live `repair` command (off / slow / normal / fast, finish one or all), a New Simulation choice "Reconstruction" (Off / Slow / Normal / Fast, default Normal), the key `B` cycling the speed, chips in the Climate-panel style, a **site card** (kind, stage name, percent, "waiting" / "work stopped: wind", the town's counts) for the building nearest the view centre that is a site, and a tenth Debug tab **Rebuild** (key `0`) with a scrubber per building kind. **There is no building inspect card in the viewer today** (verified: only the cloud life card, and Phase L's drains card), so the card is new and its "nearest the view centre" selection is the planner's, not a click on a building. B: only the Debug tab and the command.
66. Phase M, Advanced graphics (Q9; default applies): one switch, **"No construction details"** (bit 12, key `noSiteDetails`, off by default so the crane, workers, flapping net and crane light show), stored only when non-default as Phase J's rule says. It removes the live pass only; the scaffold in the ground tile is never switched off. B: no setting.
67. Phase M, the other reading of "regenerate" (Q10; default applies): towns **growing new buildings** on empty lots over time is **not** in Phase M; it is a growth model, not repair. Recorded as a follow-up: the same scaffold stages (`clear` to `done`) would serve it, so `ScaffoldPainter` and the stage table are built to be reusable by a site that has no `d0`.
68. Phase M, where it runs (planner's, not asked): **sandbox mode only**, like Phase L's water. Showcase, hurricane and world modes are unchanged, so the showcase supercell tab's damage stays as the lesson shows it; their `hello` has no `repair`.
69. Phase M, on by default for old worlds too (planner's, not asked): `--repair` defaults to `normal`, so a v2.4 save with damaged buildings **starts rebuilding when it is loaded** and the player's wrecked town heals; `--repair off` and the New Simulation / `B` / `repair` choices restore the old behaviour (damage stays for ever). Existing tests that run a world long after a tornado and read the damage must pass `repair="off"` or read the maximum (MS6).
70. Phase M, decisions made without being asked (planner's; none answered): nine stages numbered 0–9 with `done` only as a last row; the row shape `[id, stage, p, d0, hold]` and its send rule (0.04 of `p`, at most every 3rd frame); `damage` moves in 1/24 steps while a site is open so it equals the viewer's bake level; `damage` is constant in `wait`, `clear` and `scaffold` and falls through structure, walls, roof and fitout, so the painting of the ruin line, torn corner and roof bites is run in reverse; `T_FULL`, `SHARE`, `need`, `size_k`, jitter, `SETTLE_S` 25, `SAFE_RADIUS` 1 200, `GUST_LIMIT` 18, `HEAL_BELOW` 0.04, `CREWS`, `RESET_RISE` 0.03; oldest hit first in the queue; the viewer's geometry (bay 16 px, lift 11 px, six workers); the bake step counts; the 0.5 s coalescing of bake changes; the tenth Debug tab and the key `0`, `B` for the speed, the card under the drains card; streetlamps have no scaffold; the church takes a crane; farm buildings (no settlement) have their own crew; `FakeRepair` and the FakeSim-only `wreck` command.
71. Phase M, numbers that are not measured (unresolved until MS7 and MV3): every constant in the model table is a starting value from arithmetic and from the scratch measurements of one machine under load (87 % busy at the time); the tornado gap was measured on one preset (Oklahoma, May 16 h, clock held, 4 seeds); the schedule check used the median size of each kind and an ideal crew queue, not the real simulation; the wind gate was sampled once a second at the building tops, not at the 5 Hz the sim will use; the viewer's repaint rate, frame cost and live-pass cost are **estimates only** (targets 6 / 12 tile repaints a second at speed 1 / 8, 0.3 ms for the live pass) and nothing of the viewer was built or measured; whether `Lightning`'s shadow caster looks right for a half-built tower is not known.
72. Phase M, asked for or implied but not turned into a step: growth of new buildings (67); cars, wrecked cars, roads and the tornado's ground scars (`Renderer.drawScars`) staying as they are after the rebuild; building damage from flooding (Phase L, 48) feeding the same model; sound; people, evacuation or a cost / materials economy; a visible queue ("waiting for a crew") beyond the card text; construction at night stopping (62); rebuilding a destroyed building as a different one (the rebuilt building is the same object: same kind, variant, colours and seed, so the street looks as it did).
73. Phase M, sequencing assumptions (planner's): Phase M starts after Phase L's LI closes (it edits the same files: `sim/objects.py`, `simulation.py`, `_schema.py`, `Renderer.java`, `BuildingPainter.java`, `FakeSim.java`, `ViewerState.java`, `Model.java`, `Settings.java`, `DebugScreen.java`, `Main.java`), so nothing here is built against a half-edited file; M0 moves the v2.5 block into `PROTOCOL.md` first and then MS and MV may run at once because they share no file; the v2.5 block in this file is the contract until M0 and is frozen for MS and MV afterwards (a change needs a blocking SA-Asker question and a lockstep edit, as in Phases A–L); the `test_perf` tornado timing failure is a load artefact seen on old and new code alike and is compared in isolation (MS6, MS8).
74. Phase N, "Land Falls" (asked in SA-Asker session `phase-n-plan-water-levels-out-land-falls-20261010-214630`, Q1; **assumed**, no answer: default A applies): read as **water running over the land down a slope as a continuous stream that cascades (white water) where the ground is steep**, carried by a film of water on the ground between a source and the body that receives it. B: literal waterfalls only at cliff-like drops, nothing on gentle slopes (the film is still needed; only the drawing changes). C: the stream everywhere plus a real fall with mist at cliffs (NV3 holds the cliff drawing behind a threshold, `CLIFF_SLOPE` 2.0, so C is one switch).
75. Phase N, what "level out" means (Q2; **ANSWERED by the user 2026-10-11**, verbatim with a screenshot: "its still the same as before i want it to level out at the lowest point of the world and if theres more land it will go there"). This overturned the planner's default A and the first draft's B-only-if-asked: **water never stands above ground that has lower ground next to it; there is no virtual curb; a street span is ordinary ground that has drains; water runs to the lowest point it can reach, fills that hollow to a level surface that spreads over whatever land its level reaches (a town's street included), and when the hollow is full it spills onward to the next lower land.** The four things the planner saw in the build (the water hanging 10 px above the ground at a street end with a ramp, two bodies at different heights with nothing between, water appearing instead of rising, a full hollow handing its water over the town) are all removed by this: the first by dropping the curb, the second and third by the film, the fourth by NS5. The first draft's weir store above the curb was dropped (NS1).
76. Phase N, hillside runoff (Q3; **assumed**, default A applies): **drawn** as a thin sheet (the film under rain, 0.2 to 1.5 px on a gentle slope under a steady rain, up to about 3 px after 200 px of slope under a supercell, nothing drawn under 0.15 px), as well as the street's sheet and the overflow stream. B: only a street's sheet and a hollow's overflow (`MIN_H_DRAW` raised so the rain-only film never shows, a one-constant change in the viewer).
77. Phase N, order against Phase M (Q4; **ANSWERED 2026-10-11 by the coordinator on the user's behalf: build Phase N now, before Phase M**; Phase M has not started). Labelled **v2.4.1** (an amendment of v2.4) so that Phase M's text and its v2.5 label stay untouched; N0 says where the block goes in `PROTOCOL.md`. The first draft's default (after M, v2.6) is superseded. Phase N starts after Phase L's LS outcome and LI (session `phase-l-sim-drains-finish-20261011-102413` is finishing LS).
78. Phase N, "vary in size depending on the height of the flood" (planner's reading, not asked; revised 2026-10-11): the flood on a street is now **the film itself**: its depth over the street (deepest at the low end) is the flood's height, and the stream running down the road is the same film continuing, so the stream's thickness follows the flood's height by one law (`h = (q / (KU sqrt(slope)))^0.6`, about the 0.6 power of the flux). A deeper flood sends more water, a thicker stream. The first draft's weir (`q = CW head^1.5`) is gone. A literal reading, "the stream is as thick as the flood is deep", would put a 10 px stream over a gentle slope and was not chosen.
79. Phase N, numbers that are not measured (unresolved until NS6 and NV9): `KU` 23, `KU_STREET` 23, `SLOPE_MIN` 0.02, `FILM_SOAK` 0, the sink rule width (1 px), `FLOOD_DEPTH` 3, `OVERFLOW_DEPTH` 8, `MIN_H_DRAW` and every viewer geometry value are starting values from arithmetic and from scratch probes on seed 12 only (a supercell over small drains: 52 s of overflow, mean 34 px²/s, 127 at the busiest step, measured at `HIT_AREA` 1.0; LS6 then chose 1.15) and a sheet-on-a-pad prototype (flat 814 px, constant rain). The film router was prototyped outside the project and never fed back into the real field, so conservation, save / load and the effect on the Phase L tests are **not** measured.
80. Phase N, flat shelves (unresolved, measured once): with the sink only at the bed cell the prototype piled a 4.8 px film on a stretch of slope-floor ground (seed 12, x 2848-2880, slope 0.02) that is really the floor of the receiving hollow. The sink rule in the model table (ground within `BASIN_MIN_DEPTH` of the bed, or under the body's level) is the planner's fix; NS2 measures it on the census (N5: under 1.5 px at rest).
81. Phase N, the street's rim (found in the census; **superseded 2026-10-11**): the first draft raised a street's rim to the real divide where it was above `min(e0, e1) + CURB` (12 of 88 streets, worst +21.5 / +88 / +233 px). With no curb the rim **is** the real divide for every street that is a hollow (NS1), and a pad that is not a hollow has no rim (it sheds). Census facts kept: the median depth a pad holds on its own is 0.08 px.
82. Phase N, cars and film (**revised 2026-10-11**): a street's flood is now a sheet, so `cars.py` takes a car's depth from the film as well as from a body (NS4, additive; the 7 `TestStandingWater` cases stay unedited). A car stalls at 1.8 x wheel radius as before (a sedan at 5.8 px), which a 6-10 px sheet at the low end of a pad reaches.
83. Phase N, an Advanced switch for the stream (not asked, not planned): there is no "No flowing water" option; the stream is a live pass of at most about 0.3 ms. If the user wants one it is the Phase J pattern (one bit, `--adv`), a small viewer step.
84. Phase N, Debug (planner's, following Phases I, L and M): the existing Flood tab gets a slope control (a steep run and a cliff in its fake terrain), a settlement picker with a hilltop village, a town beside a hollow and a hollow hamlet, a flow readout (`h`, `q`, speed) and a "show flow numbers" overlay; no new tab. Not asked by the user in this request.
85. Phase N, decisions made without being asked (planner's; none answered): the 32 px cell as the unit of the film; a linear-reservoir cell with a Manning-like speed; drains spread evenly over a street's cells and taken from whatever water is there; a pad's direction toward its lower end; the wire shape (`hello.flow`, `frame.flow` runs with `h[]` and `q[]`, `hello.drains.curb` 0, `overflow_depth`, `flood[].backed`); the viewer computes the speed as `q / h` from the wire and never from the model; white water from slope or speed thresholds; rain on a bucket under a body still goes straight to the body, every other cell is film; the film is sandbox-only like all of v2.4; showcase, hurricane and world mode carry none of it; the label v2.4.1.
86. Phase N, asked for or implied but not turned into a step: rivers and a permanent stream bed that stays after the rain; erosion; a flow that carries cars, rubble or snow; snowmelt runoff; sound; a flow indicator on the minimap; building damage from flooding (Phase L 48, 56). (A pool covering a town's street, formerly listed here, is now NS5.)
87. Phase N, what the flood states mean without a curb (planner's, not asked; follows from the user's answer): `ponding` 0.05 to 3 px, `flooded` 3 to 8 px, `overflowing` 8 px or more (or a covering body running over its rim); `depth` is the greatest water depth on the street; `FLOOD_DEPTH` 3 is Phase L's, `OVERFLOW_DEPTH` 8 is new (the old `overflowing` meant "at the curb", 10 px). `hello.drains.curb` is sent as 0.0 so an old viewer that reads it draws no kerb. B: keep `overflowing` for "water leaving the street" (any sheet running off its low end), which is true of every shedding street in any rain it cannot drain and would make the word meaningless.
88. Phase N, what the user should know before the build (from the census, 88 settlements in 8 seeds x 4 terrains): **45 of 88 (51 %) sit on ground that sheds and will never hold a standing flood (a town on a hilltop included)**; 14 of 88 have a pad that is a hollow of 3 px or more, 35 have a hollow beside them whose lower rim faces the town; 43 of 88 can ever see standing water on their street. A shedding town still shows a **sheet** of water running off its street when the drains are exceeded (starting constants: 5 px at the low end of a flat 814 px street with normal drains in a steady rain, 10 px under a supercell, none with large drains), so drains still decide whether the city floods, but a town cannot be drowned by a wall that is not there. Not asked of the user beyond the screenshot answer.
89. Phase N, the Phase L contract cannot stay and may not be reachable with one constant (unresolved until NS6): C1-C4, the flat-street vector and the LS6 table are superseded (the "What Phase L's contract becomes" table lists each, with its replacement). The starting arithmetic misses C2' and C3' for flat pads (normal drains: 5.0 px steady, 7.2 px downpour at `KU` 23; 2.9 and 4.1 px at `KU` 58) and C4' for small drains under an `OVERFLOW_DEPTH` of 8. NS6 fits `KU_STREET`, `SLOPE_MIN`, `FLOOD_DEPTH` and `OVERFLOW_DEPTH` and records the table; if the contract is not reachable the miss is stated, not tuned away. Which Phase L tests change was counted by `grep` (27 + 8 + 2 + 4 mentions in four test files) and is listed one by one by NS0.
90. Phase L, as built, droplet slant (asked in SA-Asker session `phase-l-plan-sync-20261011-113218`, Q1; from LA's `ASSUMED` entry items 3-6; default applies if unanswered): running drops on windows and cars lean by the **wind speed**, not by the rain streaks (the real sim's rain leans under 2 degrees): 0 below 1 m/s, linearly to 50 degrees at 9 m/s, each drop by its own uniform 0..1 share of that angle; still beads get an upwind tail (not more beads on one side, because their places would jump at each wind level); a moving car adds its own speed as head wind. Default A keeps this, so drops lean much more than the streaks. B: follow the streak slant exactly (almost no lean). C: a random angle per drop that changes every frame (flicker, and it would force tile re-bakes).
91. Phase L, as built, the glint dot (Q2; LA's item 2; default applies): a bright dot of radius `1.3 x` the drop's own dot plus 0.3 px (at least 1.15 px), pulsing 0.90-1.10, with a faint glow out to 2.3 radii (alpha 60-156 of 256); judged on pictures only, never in a live window. Default A keeps the glow. B: a plain dot with no glow. C: a smaller dot.
92. Phase L, as built, short towns (Q3; from LS6; default applies): towns under 650 px wide flood even `normal` drains under a cumulonimbus (7 of the 8 tested), because the rain core covers the whole street; so for them the storm, not the drain size, decides. Default A accepts and records it. B: scale a street's capacity so a short town needs the same size per px of rain as a long one. C: raise the `normal` size range of hamlets and villages.
93. Phase L, as built, supercell against downpour (Q4; from LS6; default applies): a supercell and a cumulonimbus deliver nearly the same rain intensity to a town and differ by how long it rains (174 s against 99 s), so a supercell floods `normal` drains in only 8 of 14 towns (of 650 px and up) and overflows `small` drains in 11 of 14; the contract C4 asked for more. Default A accepts and records it. B: make supercell rain heavier in the sim (touches the cloud and rain model, a new sim task). C: lower the `normal` class capacity (would break C3, which holds today).
94. Phase L, as built, drizzle (Q5; from LS6; default applies): a short drizzle (13-39 s at 2.6-3.8 hits per second per bucket) leaves about 2 px of standing water on `small` drains, where the contract C1 said "at most `wet`". It is under the 3 px that count as flooded but is visible water. Default A accepts. B: raise `SOAK` or `INLET_K` so a drizzle stays wet (re-tunes C2-C4 too).
95. Phase L, as built, speed x8 (Q6; from LS0; default applies): at speed x8 the sim delivers about 37 % less rain per **simulated** second (`PRECIP_TICK_CAP`, 14 particles per cloud per tick: cumulonimbus 3.66 against 5.78 hits per second per bucket), so floods come later and shallower at fast speed than at speed 1; the flood tables were measured at speed 1 only. Default A leaves it and records it. B: lift the cap while the speed is above 1 (touches `particles.py` and the performance budget).
96. Phase L, as built, the Drains default (Q7; from the viewer agent's `ASSUMED` item 4; default applies): the New Simulation choice (Small / Normal / Large / Random) becomes the remembered default as soon as a world is created, and the Settings screen has no row for it. Default A leaves it. B: add a Settings row.
97. Phase L, as built, the Debug Flood tab (Q8; from the viewer agent's `ASSUMED` item 10; default applies): two stacked pictures (towns above, hollows below) instead of the planned side corner, a village at 3.0 px and a city at 8.2 px to start, and the fake's clock held in the late morning so the picture never goes dark (a fake day is 5 minutes). Default A keeps this. B: the side corner of the plan. C: let the clock run.
98. Phase L, as built, the drains card and the pipe overlay (Q9; from the viewer agent's `ASSUMED` items 6 and 11 and its measurements; default applies): the card is hidden when every panel is put away and costs about 0.2 ms a frame whenever a town is in view, with or without water (the plan wanted nothing measurable without water); the `D` pipe overlay is never remembered between runs. Default A keeps both. B: show the card only while rain or water is on a town. C: remember the `D` overlay in `settings.json`.

99. ## Phase P — Sound: rain, thunder, traffic, towns, siren, tornadoes and the hurricane (viewer only; no protocol change)

**Project context:** `[UPDATE]` — extends the build with Phases H–O (the whole project was read for this phase: Python 3.11 stdlib sim in `sim/`, a plain-`javac` Java2D/Swing viewer of 87 files in `viewer/src/precip`, JDK 25.0.3, Windows 11, no git, desktop icon `C:\Users\lenard\Desktop\Precipitation.lnk` -> `run.bat`). **Status 2026-10-12 `[UPDATE]`: nothing built, every step `[PLANNED]`.** The viewer has **no sound of any kind today** (a search of `viewer/src`, `sim/` and `tools/` for `javax.sound`, `AudioSystem`, `SourceDataLine` and `.wav` finds nothing; `assets/` holds `precipitation.ico`, `precipitation.png` and `world/` only). Files marked `[NEW]` are new, every other file named below is an `[UPDATE]` of an existing one. **Viewer only**: no `PROTOCOL.md` change and no file of `sim/` is edited (see "Protocol and sim"). Planned against the code of 2026-10-12. SA-Asker session `phase-p-plan-sound-20261012-130825` (seven advisory questions, see "Open questions (Phase P)"). Phase O's question 108 listed "sound (wind, crashing)" as asked for but not planned; this phase is the sound the user has now named, and it does not take up wind or crashing.

**The user's request (2026-10-12, verbatim):** "C:\Users\lenard\Downloads\donrain-rain-110508.mp3 C:\Users\lenard\Downloads\liecio-calming-rain-257596.mp3 Liecio for All rain clouds except cumulus cumulonimbus supercell and nimbostratus. donrain for cumulus cumulonimbus supercell and nimbostratus. Dont Let these sounds play unless not raining or snowy. For thunder there are multiple variants [7 files] These are the variants. [5 car files] Cars Passing by the more cars the more muffled or amplified. [2 city files] cities these two should overlap. [siren] when a super cell has a wall cloud and precipitating. [tornado-sound, hurricane-storm] Tornadoes. [hurricane-storm] hurricane simulation"

| Reading (the orchestrator's, not confirmed by the user) | Part |
|---|---|
| Two rain beds: `liecio` for every raining type except cumulus, cumulonimbus, supercell, nimbostratus; `donrain` for those four. Silent whenever nothing is falling | **PC2** |
| Seven thunder files: one picked per lightning bolt, never the same twice in a row, delayed and quietened by distance | **PC3** |
| Five car files: a pass-by when a car passes the listener; more cars = louder and denser, far traffic muffled | **PC4** |
| Two city files that loop together (overlap) while a town or city is in earshot | **PC5** |
| Siren while a supercell is precipitating and has a wall cloud | **PC6** |
| Tornado file and hurricane-storm file layered while a tornado is on the ground | **PC7** |
| Hurricane-storm file loops on the hurricane screen | **PC8** |
| Decode, mix and play with no new dependency; settings; silent on pause, menus, leaving, tests | **PA, PB, PD, PE** |

> **Parts and files (no file in two parts).** Order: PA, then PB (needs PA's WAVs only for PE), then PC, then PD, then PE. PB and PC are one agent's work in sequence because the director's test (PE1) needs the mixer's `render`.
> * **PA — Assets** (`python-tooling-dev`): new `tools/make_sounds.py`, new `assets/sounds/*.wav`, `assets/sounds/manifest.json`, `assets/sounds/CREDITS.md`.
> * **PB — Engine** (`general-purpose`): new `SoundBank.java`, `SoundMixer.java`, `Sound.java`.
> * **PC — Director** (`general-purpose`): new `SoundDirector.java` only (a pure function of frame + view; no audio import).
> * **PD — Wiring and settings** (`general-purpose`): small edits (re-read each first) to `Settings.java`, `SettingsScreen.java`, `ViewerPanel.java`, `AppShell.java`, `Main.java`, `UiSnapshot.java`, `AdvancedTest.java` (line 278 only).
> * **PE — Tests and close** (`general-purpose`, then the user): new `SoundTest.java`, one call added to `Main`'s selftest line; the listening check and the shortcut check are the user's.
> * Nobody may touch `sim/`, `PROTOCOL.md`, `saves/`, `config/`, `scrap/`, `Renderer.java`, `Lightning.java`, `Model.java` or `build.bat`. The sound classes only read `Model`. Each part compiles with `javac -encoding UTF-8 -d <own scratch folder> viewer/src/precip/*.java`; nobody runs `build.bat` (a game runs from `viewer\out`).

**What each part does**
| Part | Role |
|---|---|
| `tools/make_sounds.py` (PA, new) | One run turns the 19 chosen MP3s in `C:\Users\lenard\Downloads` into 16-bit 44100 Hz stereo WAVs in `assets/sounds/`, cuts the 847 s rain to a loop, crossfades loop seams, levels each file, writes the manifest. The viewer never needs ffmpeg |
| `assets/sounds/` (PA, new) | What ships: 19 WAVs (about 118 MB), `manifest.json` (id, group, duration, loop points, level, loudest moment of each car), `CREDITS.md` |
| `SoundBank.java` (PB, new) | Reads the manifest and the WAVs; short sounds into `short[]`, the long beds memory-mapped so they cost no heap; a missing or short file becomes a silent stand-in, never an exception |
| `SoundMixer.java` (PB, new) | One `SourceDataLine`, one daemon thread: looping beds, one-shot voices, per-voice gain smoothing and a one-pole low-pass ("muffled"), pause hold, dead-man fade, `render(float[], int)` for tests without a device |
| `Sound.java` (PB, new) | The facade the app holds: `Sound.open(root, settings)` returns the real thing or a do-nothing `Sound.NONE` (headless, no device, `--no-sound`) |
| `SoundDirector.java` (PC, new) | Frame + view + hello in, target gains and one-shot requests out. Holds the little memory it needs (bolts already heard, cars already scheduled, the siren's latch). Knows nothing about Swing or audio |
| `Settings`, `SettingsScreen` (PD) | A `sound` block in `config/settings.json`; a **Sound** tab: master, mute, five group sliders, test buttons |
| `ViewerPanel`, `AppShell`, `Main` (PD) | A per-frame hook in the panel; the app owns one `Sound`; it is switched on in `Main.launchApp` only, and told about the session, pause, menus, leaving and exit |
| `SoundTest.java` (PE, new) | The suite: every rule below on made-up frames, the mixer's `render`, the assets, the settings round trip, "nothing plays under `--selftest`" |

### What the current build shows (read while planning; every line was opened in the code)
* **Cloud** (`Model.java` 297-339, parsed 1012-1076): `type` / `kind` (0 cumulus, 1 cumulonimbus, 2 nimbostratus, 3 stratocumulus, 4 altostratus, 5 altocumulus, 6 stratus, 7 cirrus, 8 supercell; `Model.CUMULUS` ... `Model.SUPERCELL`), `stage` (`"precipitating"` is one of `Model.STAGES`), `precipType` (the sim writes `none`, `rain`, `snow`, `mixed`, `graupel`; `sim/clouds.py` 1448-1468), `precipRate` (the viewer's `CloudShade` already treats above 0.02 as raining), `fair`, `form`, `x`, `w`, `puffs`, and for supercells `wall` 0..1 (clamped, line 1071), `rotation`, `hook`, `mesoX` (the wall cloud's world x, NaN when not sent). A cloud that cannot rain (altocumulus, cirrus: `precip=("none",)` in `sim/clouds.py` 56-90) keeps `precipType none`. In practice the light bed belongs to **stratocumulus, altostratus and stratus**; cumulus, cumulonimbus, nimbostratus and supercell carry the heavy one.
* **Lightning** (`Model.Bolt` 421-424, parsed 814-825): `pts` (x0, y0, x1, y1 ... ending on the surface) and `alpha`; **no id**. The sim (`simulation.py` 459-490) adds at most one bolt per tick, only from a precipitating `is_cb` cloud (cumulonimbus or supercell), starts it at alpha 1.0 and takes 1.8 a second off, so a bolt lives 0.55 s, about 17 frames at 30 a second, and is in every one of them. `Lightning.key(bolt)` (`Lightning.java` 367) is a stable hash of the first point, the last x and the point count, already used to recognise "the same bolt" across frames. `frame.events` carries `"lightning from <cloud name>"` once per bolt but no position, so it is **not** used. `Main.injectBolt` (`--bolt`) and `DebugWorlds` put bolts into frames the same way, so they sound too (except in Debug, see question 118).
* **Cars** (`Model.Car` 286-295): `x`, `speed` (px/s), `dir` (+1 / -1), `state` (`driving`, `stopped`, `tossed`, `wrecked`; `sim/cars.py` 140-378), `variant` (sedan, hatchback, pickup, van, truck, bus). Street cars cruise 24-38 px/s, highway cars 46-72 px/s (`cars.py` 37-38). There is **no road field on a car**; whether it is on the highway is read from `Hello.roads` (`Road.highway()`) and its x.
* **Settlements** (`Model.Settlement` 116-123): `kind` is one of `hamlet`, `village`, `town`, `city` (`sim/settlements.py` 527-528), `x0`, `x1`, `population`. They are in `hello`, not in frames.
* **Tornadoes** (`Model.Tornado` 276-283, parsed 845-871): `x`, `reach` (0 funnel hanging, 1 on the ground, falls again while `roping`), `stage` (`funnel`, `touchdown`, `mature`, `roping`), `strength` 0..1, `ef`, `width`, `cloud`. `onGround()` is `reach >= 0.995`; the sim itself starts the vortex's ground effects at `GROUND_REACH = 0.9` (`sim/tornado.py` 38). A supercell needs `rotation >= 0.7` and `wall >= 0.6` to drop one (`clouds.py` 1267-1268).
* **Camera** (`Camera.View`, `Camera.java` 26-73): the finished per-frame view, published in `Renderer.view` (`Renderer.java` 163, volatile) and already read by `ViewerPanel.sendView`. The listener is the view's centre column, `lx = (wx0 + wx1) / 2`, exactly as `CloudShade.target` takes "under a cloud"; the visible width is `span = wx1 - wx0`; `zoom` and `worldW` are there too. Nothing else about the camera is needed.
* **Hurricane screen**: reached from the menu (`MainMenuScreen.HURRICANE`, key `H`, line 224 and 166), or `--mode hurricane` (`Main.launchApp` 628), through `AppShell.startHurricane()` (567) -> `beginMode("hurricane", ...)` (671) -> `HurricaneScreen` as `modeScreen` with `session.mode == "hurricane"`. Its messages are `type: "hurricane"`, parsed into `HurricaneModel.Frame` (`paused`, `category`, `vmaxMs`, `vmaxKt`, `stage`), **not** into `Model.Frame`; the sandbox frame is not there, so the director must read the hurricane model for this mode. Esc and the Menu button call `app.leaveNow()` (`HurricaneScreen` 188, 603); Space / P / the button send the sim's pause (173, 191). The Debug mode has a tab with the same name (`DebugScreen.TAB_IDS`); that is a different screen.
* **Pause, menus, leaving, exit** (`AppShell.java`): Esc in a game -> `openPause()` (782) sends `{"cmd":"pause"}` and pushes `PauseScreen`; `resumeFromPause()` (794); the sim's frames then carry `paused: true` after one round trip. `leaveNow()` (836) -> `showMenu()` (505) sets `session = null` and builds the menu's decorative `FakeSim` sky (its own `ViewerPanel`, line 511); `begin()` (715) and `beginMode()` (671) set `session` and make the panel; `exitNow()` (878) ends in `onExit`. `stack` (line 51, private) holds the dialogs; in a lesson mode `modeScreen` stays on it, so "the stack is not empty" does **not** mean "paused" there. `simProblem` (927) covers a dead simulation (`gs.simError()`).
* **Per-frame hook**: `ViewerPanel.paintComponent` (255-327) ends each painted frame with `sendView(b1)` (323); it runs for the world renderer and for a `Scene` (`HurricaneScreen`, `WorldScreen`, `DebugScreen`) alike. It stops when the window is not repainted (minimised), so the mixer needs its own dead-man fade.
* **Where a setting goes** (`Settings.java`): fields 36-88; `read(Map)` 136-213 (one `Json.obj(m, "<block>")` per block, clamped); `toJson()` 215-273; blocks that a player who never uses them should not gain are written only when they differ from the default (`adv`, `defaultDrains`, `debugUnlocked`). `AppShell.settingsChanged()` (310) -> `applySettings()` (316), the one place every setting is applied. `SettingsScreen.java`: `TABS` and the index constants (18-19), `paint` switch (51-57), widgets `ui.slider(frac, x, y, w, v -> ...)`, `ui.toggle`, `ui.seg`, `ui.button` (`Ui.java` 271-310), Tab / Up / Down cycle the tabs (`key`, last method). `AdvancedTest.java` 278 asserts `SettingsScreen.TABS.length == 5`; `UiSnapshot.SCREENS` (line 21) lists the picture names.
* **Where sound must not start**: `--selftest` / `--selftest-ui` end in `System.exit` at `Main.java` 204-214 before any window; `--snapshot` and `--bench` take the `diagnostic` branch (257-324) which has **no `ViewerPanel`** (`Main.snapshot` 749 and `Bench` 73 drive a `ParallelRenderer` directly); lesson-mode snapshots go to `ModeSnapshot` (245-256); `--ui-snapshot` to `UiSnapshot`. But `AppShell` is constructed by 10 test and snapshot classes (`grep "new AppShell"`: `AdvancedTest`, `DebugScreen`, `DebugTest`, `DrainTest`, `FloatTest`, `FlowTest`, `ModeSnapshot`, `SelfTest`, `UiDriveTest`, `UiSnapshot`), so the switch cannot live in `AppShell`'s constructor. The only code path of a real window is `Main.launchApp` (615), which also serves `--fake` and `--replay`.
* **Not found / not measured**: no field says "the rain reaches the ground under this x" (the sim's `base_w` and flank span are not sent), so the footprint is the cloud's own (as `CloudShade` does); no car pass event exists, only positions; the content, loudness and loudest moment of the 19 files are unmeasured (PA2 measures them); whether `javax.sound.sampled` finds a line on the user's machine is untested.

### The model in numbers (the one table PC builds from)
Every constant is a planner's **starting value** in one commented block at the top of `SoundDirector`; PE3 re-tunes by ear. `smooth(t)` is the viewer's smoothstep on 0..1 (`Lit.smooth`). Distances are world px along x; `R = max(700, 0.9 span)`.
| Sound | Fields that drive it | Rule (start values) |
|---|---|---|
| Gate for everything | `session`, `Frame.paused`, `HurricaneModel.Frame.paused`, `AppShell` stack, `gs.simError()`, `Frame.speed` | Nothing is audible unless a session is running, not paused, no dialog other than the lesson mode's own screen is open, and the sim has no error. All targets 0 and the mixer held otherwise |
| "Seen from afar" | `span`, `worldW` | Every bed and shot is multiplied by `far = 1 - 0.55 smooth((span / worldW - 0.55) / 0.45)`, the same factor `CloudShade` uses, so the whole world in view is not a wall of noise |
| Rain, light bed `rain_light` (Liecio) | clouds with `stage == "precipitating"`, `precipType != "none"`, `precipRate > 0.02`, `!fair`, `kind` in {3 stratocumulus, 4 altostratus, 5 altocumulus, 6 stratus, 7 cirrus} | `near = 1 - smooth(dist(lx, [x0, x1]) / R)` with `[x0, x1]` the cloud's footprint (`x +- w/2`, widened by the puffs, as `CloudShade`); `level = 0.25 + 0.75 min(1, precipRate)` times `form`; `g = 1 - product(1 - near x level x typeFactor)`. **No raining cloud in earshot = exactly 0.** `typeFactor`: rain 1, mixed 0.8, graupel 1, snow 0.35 (question 115) |
| Rain, heavy bed `rain_heavy` (donrain) | the same, `kind` in {0 cumulus, 1 cumulonimbus, 2 nimbostratus, 8 supercell} | the same formula on its own set; both beds can sound at once. A snow-only cloud adds nothing to this bed in the default |
| Thunder (7 variants) | `Frame.lightning` (`Bolt.pts`, `Bolt.alpha`), `Lightning.key`, `hello.w` | A bolt whose key is not in the director's "heard" set and whose `alpha >= 0.7` fires one shot; the first frame after a reset, a load or a pause fires none (the whole frame's keys are only remembered). Strike point = last `pts` pair. `d = abs(strikeX - lx)`; `RT = max(2400, 2.2 span)`; gain `(1 - smooth(d / RT))^1.5`; delay `0.15 + min(6, d / 900)` s; low-pass `lerp(16000, 900 Hz, smooth(d / RT))`; variant random from the 7, never the one before; keys forgotten after 10 s; at most 3 thunders sounding |
| Car pass-by (5 variants) | `Frame.cars` (`x`, `speed`, `dir`, `state`, `id`), `manifest.cars[i].peak` | A car with `state == "driving"`, `speed >= 8`, approaching the listener (`dir` toward `lx`) and within `0.6 R`, whose predicted crossing `T = abs(x - lx) / speed` is within the file's `peak + 0.5` s, starts a voice so the file's loudest moment lands on `T`; one voice per car id per 60 s; at most 3 at once; variant random, never the same twice. Traffic gain `0.35 + 0.65 (1 - exp(-N / 5))`, `N` = driving cars within `R` (more cars = louder and denser, question 117); low-pass `lerp(14000, 1500 Hz, smooth(abs(x - lx) / R))` set at the start of the voice |
| Town ambience (`city_a` + `city_b`) | `hello.settlements` (`kind`, `x0`, `x1`) | `dist = max(0, x0 - lx, lx - x1)`; `Rc = max(600, 0.7 span)`; `size`: city 1.0, town 0.6, village and hamlet 0 (question 116); `g = max over settlements of size x (1 - smooth(dist / Rc))`; **both files run at 0.7 g at once**, always advancing (muted at 0) with different lengths (55 s, 159 s) from seeded offsets, so they overlap at a different phase every time |
| Siren (`siren`, looped) | `kind == 8`, `stage == "precipitating"`, `precipRate > 0.02`, `precipType != "none"`, `wall`, `mesoX`, `Frame.tornadoOf(id)` | On at `wall >= 0.35`; stays on until `wall < 0.20` and the cloud has no tornado (a latch per cloud id); not a supercell, not precipitating, or no wall = off. Distance to `mesoX` (to `x` when NaN); `RS = max(2400, 2.2 span)`; `g = 1 - smooth(d / RS)`; loops with a 0.5 s crossfade |
| Tornado (`tornado` 1.0 + `hurricane` 0.6 layered) | `Frame.tornadoes` (`x`, `reach`, `strength`) | `ground = smooth((reach - 0.9) / 0.1)`; `g = 1 - product(1 - ground x (0.55 + 0.45 strength) x (1 - smooth(d / RTo)))`, `RTo = max(1800, 1.6 span)`; a funnel still hanging (`reach < 0.9`) and a roping one with `reach < 0.9` are silent; low-pass by distance as thunder |
| Hurricane (`hurricane`, looped) | `session.mode == "hurricane"`, `modeScreen instanceof HurricaneScreen`, `HurricaneModel.Frame.paused`, `vmaxMs` | `g = 0.45 + 0.55 clamp(vmaxMs / 70, 0, 1)` while the screen is up and not paused; nothing else plays in this mode |
| Speed | `Frame.speed` | Above 2x, thunder and car shots are not fired (question 121); beds go on |
| Group volumes | `Settings` | Rain = both rain beds, Thunder, Traffic, Towns, Storm = siren + tornado + hurricane; each times Master, times 0 when muted. Defaults: master 0.7, rain 0.8, thunder 0.9, traffic 0.7, towns 0.5, storm 0.9 |

**When the sound is silent, ducked or ended** (all through `Sound`, set from `AppShell`)
| Situation | Where it is caught | What the sound does |
|---|---|---|
| Main menu (decorative sky) | `showMenu()` sets `session = null`, and its panel gets no hook | silent; beds fade out in 0.4 s, shots stop |
| Sim paused (Space / P, the pause menu, a paused save) | `Frame.paused`, and `AppShell` stack non-empty over a sandbox | beds fade to 0 in 0.3 s and **hold**; a thunder in flight freezes and goes on at resume |
| Settings, Confirm, Message open over a game | the stack's top is not `modeScreen` | the same as paused (the pause menu already paused the sim; the check also covers the one frame before the sim answers) |
| Settings opened while paused: hearing the volume | the Sound tab's **Test** buttons | a preview voice that ignores the hold, at the group volume |
| Leave (Save & Leave, Leave without saving, Esc in a lesson mode) | `leaveNow()` -> `showMenu()` | `session = null` -> fade out 0.4 s, all voices dropped |
| Quit | `exitNow()` | `sound.close()`: fade, `line.drain()` capped at 200 ms, `line.close()` |
| Sim dies | `simProblem`, `gs.simError()` | silent |
| Window not repainting (minimised) | no hook calls for 1 s | the mixer's dead-man: fade to 0 |
| No audio device, a missing WAV | `Sound.open`, `SoundBank` | one line on stderr, silent, the game goes on |
| `--selftest`, `--selftest-ui`, `--snapshot`, `--bench`, `--ui-snapshot`, headless | never reach `Main.launchApp`; `Sound.open` also returns `NONE` when `GraphicsEnvironment.isHeadless()` | no line is opened, no thread started |

### Protocol and sim: none needed
**No protocol block, no sim part.** Every input in the table is already on the wire (`clouds`, `lightning`, `cars`, `tornadoes`, `hello.settlements`, `frame.paused`, `frame.speed`, the hurricane message). An old stream, a `--replay` file or a pre-Phase-P save plays with sound without change. **Would need the sim, not planned:** a bolt id and a strike position event (so no frame can be missed), the rain's real ground footprint under a supercell (`base_w`, flank span), a road id on each car.

Done when: `--check` prints 19 `OK` lines and ignores the others; `ffmpeg` and `ffprobe` are found on PATH (they are, in `...\WinGet\Links`), else it stops with a one-line message.

| Id (in `assets/sounds/`) | Source MP3 | s | Hz | ch | Use |

| `car_1` ... `car_5` | tanweraman-car-pass-by-337871 (3.4), soundque-car-passing-sound-soundque-field-recording-442774 (6.9, 48 kHz), km007-car-pass-by-9111 (5.8), spinopel-car-passing-by-272055 (26.5, mono), spinopel-passing-car-393073 (8.9, mono) | | | 2 / 1 | one-shots |

2. **[PLANNED] PA2 — `tools/make_sounds.py`: convert, cut, loop, level.** Stdlib Python (`subprocess`, `wave`, `array`, `json`, `argparse`) around `ffmpeg`; run as `python tools\make_sounds.py [--src DIR] [--out assets\sounds]`. Each file: `ffmpeg -y -i <mp3> -ac 2 -ar 44100 -c:a pcm_s16le -map_metadata -1 -fflags +bitexact <id>.wav` (mono becomes stereo by duplication; 24 and 48 kHz are resampled). `rain_heavy`: analyse a mono 8 kHz decode, pick the steadiest (lowest RMS variance) 120 s window on a 10 s grid, cut it with `-ss/-t`. Every loop (`rain_*`, `city_*`, `siren`, `tornado`, `hurricane`): an equal-power crossfade of 2 s (0.5 s for the siren) of the tail over the head, so the file loops with no click; the manifest records `loop: true`. One-shots: trim leading and trailing digital silence below 1/1000 of full scale, 20 ms fade out. Level: per-file integrated loudness from `ffmpeg -af ebur128` scaled to the group's target (beds -26 LUFS, one-shots peak -3 dBFS, siren -20 LUFS) and written as `gain` in the manifest rather than baked in, so PE3 can re-tune without re-running ffmpeg. Cars: the loudest 50 ms window of the envelope is stored as `peak` (seconds) so PC4 can land it on the crossing. Done when: 19 WAVs exist, each re-read with `wave` as 44100 Hz, 16-bit, 2 channels; every loop's seam step (last sample against first) is below 4 percent of full scale; `rain_heavy.wav` is 120 s; the sizes add up to about 118 MB (beds 96, one-shots and siren 23) and the script prints that total; running it twice gives identical files.
3. **[PLANNED] PA3 — Manifest and credits.** `assets/sounds/manifest.json`: per id `file`, `group` (`rain`, `thunder`, `traffic`, `towns`, `storm`), `seconds`, `loop`, `gain`, and `peak` for the cars. `assets/sounds/CREDITS.md`: the 19 file names and, from the name prefix, the author (donrain, liecio, universfield, pwlpl, loswin23, freesound_community, u_q2hb2391vb, tanweraman, soundque, km007, spinopel, guillermoanaya, musicnation782, juliush) with the line "looks like Pixabay downloads; **licence not verified** - the user checks each file's page before this project is shared". Done when: the manifest names exactly the 19 ids and the credits name exactly the 19 files, no other.

**Phase PA check:** `python tools\make_sounds.py --check`, then the full run, then the numbers above printed; a WAV of each group is opened in any player once (the user, PE3). No viewer file is touched, so the viewer's build is unchanged.

### Engine — PB (`general-purpose`)
1. **[PLANNED] PB1 — `SoundBank.java` `[NEW]`.** `SoundBank.load(File assetsDir)` reads `manifest.json` with the viewer's own `Json`, opens each WAV with `javax.sound.sampled.AudioSystem` (built into the JDK; no MP3 decoder is needed or wanted), converts to the mixer's format and keeps one-shots as `short[]` stereo. Beds (`rain_*`, `city_*`, `tornado`, `hurricane`, about 96 MB) are memory-mapped from the data chunk (`FileChannel.map`, read-only) so the heap does not grow. A missing, unreadable or wrong-format file becomes a one-second silent stand-in and one stderr line. `-Xmx` is not raised (`run.bat` passes none). Done when: a unit check loads all 19, a deleted file does not throw, heap use after loading is under 30 MB (the one-shots), and loading takes under 1 s from a warm disk.
2. **[PLANNED] PB2 — `SoundMixer.java` `[NEW]`.** Format 44100 Hz, 16-bit, stereo, little-endian; `AudioSystem.getSourceDataLine`, buffer 4096 frames, blocks of 1024 (about 23 ms), a daemon thread `sound-mixer` filling in float and clipping with a soft limiter. API: `setBed(id, target)`, `fire(id, gain, lowpassHz, delaySeconds)` (queued on a `ConcurrentLinkedQueue`), `setHold(boolean)`, `setMaster(g)`, `preview(id)`, `touch()` (the director calls it each frame; no touch for 1 s fades everything to 0), `close()`. Each voice has a smoothed gain (one-pole, 0.25 s) and an optional one-pole low-pass; beds keep advancing their position at gain 0 and are simply not summed; the hold freezes one-shots without dropping them. `render(float[] out, int frames)` is the thread's body and is public to the package so the test needs no device. The director writes target gains into a `volatile float[]`; nothing the director does blocks. Done when: `render` of a loop across its seam has no step above the PA2 bound, two beds sum without exceeding 1.0 after the limiter, a one-shot ends and frees its voice, a hold freezes the position, a 1 s silence from the director fades the master to 0; the thread costs under 1 percent of one core while 8 voices play (measured).
3. **[PLANNED] PB3 — `Sound.java` `[NEW]`.** `static Sound open(File root, Settings s)`: returns `Sound.NONE` when `GraphicsEnvironment.isHeadless()`, when the system property `precip.nosound` or the environment variable `PRECIP_NOSOUND` is set, when `assets/sounds/manifest.json` is absent, or when no `SourceDataLine` can be opened (the `LineUnavailableException`, `IllegalArgumentException` and `SecurityException` are caught, one line is printed). Otherwise it owns a `SoundBank`, a `SoundMixer` and a `SoundDirector`. Methods the app needs: `session(GameSession or null)`, `frame(ViewerPanel, double dt)`, `blocked(boolean)`, `apply(Settings)`, `available()`, `close()`. `NONE` does nothing and `available()` is false. Done when: on this machine `open` returns a working `Sound`; with `PRECIP_NOSOUND=1`, headless, or the manifest renamed it returns `NONE` and the viewer still starts.

**Phase PB check:** `javac` clean into a scratch folder; the unit checks above; 10 seconds of a looped bed played through the real line once while the planner (or the user in PE3) listens for clicks at the seam.

### Director — PC (`general-purpose`)
1. **[PLANNED] PC1 — `SoundDirector.java` `[NEW]`: input, output, gate, listener.** `Mix step(Input in)` where `Input` holds `Model.Hello`, `Model.Frame` (or null), `HurricaneModel.Frame` (or null), `mode`, `lx`, `span`, `dt`, `paused`, `blocked`, and the volumes; `Mix` holds the bed targets (an array indexed by a `Bed` enum) and a list of `Shot(id, gain, lowpassHz, delay)`. A `Random` seeded from `hello.seed` makes the variant choices repeatable in tests. The gate and the `far` factor of the table are applied here. Done when: with a null frame, a paused frame, or `blocked`, every target is exactly 0 and no shot is produced; the class imports nothing from `javax.sound`, `java.awt` or `javax.swing`.
2. **[PLANNED] PC2 — Rain beds.** The two formulas of the table. Done when: a dry sky, a `fair` cumulus, a precipitating cloud with `precipType "none"`, a forming or dissipating cloud, and a raining cloud 3 R away each give 0 for both beds; a raining stratus gives only `rain_light`; a raining nimbostratus, cumulus, cumulonimbus and supercell only `rain_heavy`; one of each at once gives both; a snowing stratus gives the snow factor of question 115; the gain rises smoothly as the view moves under the cloud (no step above 0.05 per pixel of `lx`).
3. **[PLANNED] PC3 — Thunder.** The heard-set of `Lightning.key`, the alpha gate, distance, delay, low-pass, the no-repeat variant, the cap of 3. Done when: a bolt present in 17 consecutive frames fires exactly once; 200 bolts never repeat a variant back to back and use all 7; a bolt at 0 px is louder, earlier and brighter than one at 2 000 px; the first frame of a new session, and a bolt first seen at `alpha 0.5`, fire none; above 2x speed none fire.
4. **[PLANNED] PC4 — Cars.** The crossing prediction, the per-car 60 s memory (pruned when the id leaves the frame), the cap, the traffic gain and the distance low-pass. Done when: no cars, only `stopped`, `tossed` or `wrecked` cars, or cars moving away fire nothing; one car crossing fires one voice whose predicted loudest moment is within 0.3 s of the crossing; 20 cars give a larger traffic gain than 2; 8 cars crossing at once start 3 voices; the same car id does not fire twice within 60 s.
5. **[PLANNED] PC5 — Town ambience.** The two overlapping beds. Done when: with default settings a hamlet or village gives 0 and a town or city does not; both bed targets are equal and positive together in earshot and both 0 beyond `Rc`; the two beds never change gain by more than 0.05 between two frames of a view panned at 400 px/s.
6. **[PLANNED] PC6 — Siren.** The latch. Done when: a precipitating supercell at wall 0.40 is on; at wall 0.25 after being on it stays on; at 0.15 with no tornado it goes off; at 0.15 with a tornado it stays on; a cumulonimbus with wall 1.0, a supercell not precipitating, and a supercell at wall 0.30 that was never on are all off.
7. **[PLANNED] PC7 — Tornado.** Both layers. Done when: reach 0.5 gives 0; reach 0.9 gives 0; reach 1.0 and strength 1 gives the full level of the layer; reach falling through 0.9 in `roping` fades out; two tornadoes give more than one and never above 1.0; distance lowers the gain and the cutoff.
8. **[PLANNED] PC8 — Hurricane screen.** Reads `HurricaneModel.Frame`. Done when: in mode `hurricane` with the screen up and not paused the `hurricane` bed is 0.45-1.0 by `vmaxMs`, every other bed is 0, and a paused model gives 0.

**Phase PC check:** the pure checks above run in `SoundTest` (PE1) with no audio line; a made-up supercell scene (precipitating, wall 0.5, one bolt, one tornado at reach 1, one town, three cars) is stepped for 60 simulated seconds and the siren, tornado, thunder, city, light/heavy rain and car targets are printed once as a table that is read against the table of numbers.

In `begin()` (715) and `beginMode()` (671), for the modes `sandbox`, `showcase` and `hurricane` only (question 118), install `p.frameHook` and call `sound.session(gs)`; `showMenu()` (505) calls `sound.session(null)`; `applySettings()` (316) calls `sound.apply(settings)`; `exitNow()` (878) calls `sound.close()` first. A package-private `boolean soundBlocked()` returns `session == null || gs.simError() != null || (top() != null && top() != modeScreen)`. The hook reads `state.cur` under `synchronized (state)` (as `doAction` does) and the volatile `renderer().view` (as `sendView` does), and allocates nothing per frame. 5. **[PLANNED] PD5 — Pause, menus, leaving, quitting.** Behaviour only; the code is in PD4. Done when, by hand and in `SoundTest` where it can be done without a line: Space pauses and the beds fade and hold; the pause menu, Settings over it and a Confirm over it keep it held; Resume brings it back; Leave without saving fades to the menu and a new game starts clean (no voice from the old one); a lesson mode's Esc ends it; Quit leaves no stuck note.

**Phase PD check:** `javac` clean; `--selftest-ui` passes at `-Xmx2200m` with `AdvancedTest` 278 changed and nothing else red (`ui-test 58` is known to be flaky on the tree build, Phase O); `--bench 300` of the default scene and `--snapshot` of three scenes are unchanged against a run before PD (they never reach the hook); `run.bat --fake` opens and plays.

### Open questions (Phase P)
Numbers continue the file's list (the last one above is 114). The planner applied the **default** shown and nothing else; the session questions Q1-Q7 were put to the user (see the status line at the end of this phase for what came back).
115. Snow (session Q1): the request says "Dont let these sounds play unless not raining or snowy", read as "play only while raining or snowing". **Default: B**, in snow the light bed plays at 35 percent; mixed 80 percent; graupel as rain (there is no hail sound among the 19). Alternatives: A silent in snow, C the same as rain.
116. City ambience reach (Q2): **default A**, towns and cities only (town 0.6, city 1.0); hamlets and villages are silent. Alternatives: B village too, quieter; C every settlement.
117. "The more cars the more muffled or amplified" (Q3): **default A**, more cars = louder and denser, distance muffles through a low-pass. Alternatives: B more cars = more muffled; C louder only.
118. Which modes make sound (Q4): **default A**, Sandbox, Cloud Formations and Hurricane Formation; silent in the main menu's decorative sky, the World map and the hidden Debug mode (including its Lightning and Hurricane tabs). Alternatives: B Sandbox and Hurricane only; C all of them.
119. Siren rule (Q5): **default A**, supercell, precipitating, `wall >= 0.35` on, off below 0.20 unless one of its tornadoes exists. Alternatives: B from `wall >= 0.6`, the sim's tornado gate; C only while a tornado exists.
120. Size (Q6): **default A**, about 118 MB of 44.1 kHz stereo WAV in `assets/sounds/`. Alternative: B beds at 22.05 kHz mono, about 47 MB in all (the session question said 35 and 60 MB; both were wrong, 47 is the arithmetic: beds 96 MB / 4 + one-shots 23 MB), duller rain.
121. Speed (Q7): **default A**, above 2x only the beds sound; thunder and car shots are muted. Alternative: B everything at every speed.
122. Not asked, applied by the planner: (a) the heavy bed is the steadiest 120 s of the 847 s file, chosen by RMS variance, not by ear (PA2; PE3 may pick another stretch); (b) the siren is a loop with a 0.5 s seam; (c) sounds follow the **view's centre**, not the mouse, and at the whole-world view are quietened by 55 percent at most (`far`); (d) `rain_light` also takes altocumulus and cirrus in case a stream ever lets them rain; (e) thunder comes only from bolts in the frame, so a bolt that lives and dies between two painted frames (paint stalled over 33 ms, or 8x speed) is not heard - the sim would need a bolt id (not planned); (f) sound is on by default with master 0.7; (g) the thunder heard-set forgets a key after 10 s, so a long-lived duplicate hash would be heard once.
123. Not done unless the user asks: the car horns and other MP3s that sit in Downloads (not among the 19); a wind or building-crash sound (Phase O question 108); rain sound that depends on the ground (roof, puddle, water); muting when the window loses focus; separate left and right panning by the sound's position (the output is stereo but every voice is centred; panning by `x - lx` is a small later change in the mixer); the licence of the files (unverified, `CREDITS.md`).
124. The weather already running when a game loads: the first frame sets the thunder heard-set and the car memory silently, and the beds fade in over 0.3 s, so a load never starts with a clap; is a fade-in of 1.5 s on load preferred?

**Session status 2026-10-12:** questions 115-121 were posted to the user in the SA-Asker session at 13:12 and waited on for 2 minutes; **no answer came back**, so each default above was applied and recorded as `ASSUMED` in the session. Every one of 115-121 stays open here until the user answers; an answered one is to be removed from this list by PE5 (rule: the list shrinks as the user is questioned).
